# NEET-PG 2015 (AIPGMEE 2015) — Recall Questions

> **Exam held:** December 2014 (for 2015 admissions).
> **Source:** [Medical Legends blog](https://medicallegends.blogspot.com/2015/05/aipgmee-2015-question-paper-with.html) — AIPGMEE 2015 Anatomy section (Q8–Q19).

Additional questions can be parsed from `raw-dump/2015-2018/` if needed.

## Anatomy

### Q1 — External carotid artery: NOT a branch

All of the following are branches of the external carotid artery **except**:

- A. Superior thyroid artery
- B. Anterior ethmoidal artery
- C. Occipital artery
- D. Posterior auricular artery

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Anterior ethmoidal artery** — A branch of the **ophthalmic artery** (from internal carotid), not the ECA. ECA branches (mnemonic: Some Anatomists Like Freaking Out Poor Medical Students): Superior thyroid, Ascending pharyngeal, Lingual, Facial, Occipital, Posterior auricular, Maxillary, Superficial temporal.

</details>

<!-- SOURCE: medicallegends.blogspot.com AIPGMEE 2015 Anatomy | OPTIONS_GENERATED: false -->
### Q2 — Cranial nerve palsy localisation (III, IV, VI + V1)

Paralysis of 3rd, 4th, 6th nerves with involvement of the ophthalmic division of the 5th nerve localises the lesion to:

- A. Cavernous sinus
- B. Apex of orbit
- C. Brainstem
- D. Base of skull

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Cavernous sinus** — The cavernous sinus contains CN III, IV, V1, V2, and VI. Apex of orbit syndrome (Rochon-Duvigneaud) involves III, IV, V1, VI and the optic nerve (II). Brainstem lesions usually cause crossed signs (ipsilateral cranial nerves + contralateral long-tract signs).

</details>

<!-- SOURCE: medicallegends.blogspot.com AIPGMEE 2015 Anatomy | OPTIONS_GENERATED: false -->
### Q3 — Carpal tunnel contents: NOT included

The carpal tunnel contains all of the following **except**:

- A. Median nerve
- B. Flexor pollicis longus tendon
- C. Flexor carpi radialis tendon
- D. Flexor digitorum superficialis tendons

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Flexor carpi radialis tendon** — FCR passes **outside** the carpal tunnel, in its own osteofibrous sheath within the flexor retinaculum. Carpal tunnel contains: median nerve + 9 tendons (FPL, 4× FDS, 4× FDP).

</details>

<!-- SOURCE: medicallegends.blogspot.com AIPGMEE 2015 Anatomy | OPTIONS_GENERATED: false -->
### Q4 — Femoral ring boundaries: NOT a boundary

The femoral ring is bounded by the following structures **except**:

- A. Femoral vein
- B. Inguinal ligament
- C. Femoral artery
- D. Lacunar ligament

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Femoral artery** — Femoral ring boundaries: Anterior = inguinal ligament; Posterior = pectineal ligament/superior ramus of pubis; Medial = lacunar ligament; Lateral = femoral vein. The femoral **artery** lies lateral to the femoral vein, outside the femoral ring.

</details>

<!-- SOURCE: medicallegends.blogspot.com AIPGMEE 2015 Anatomy | OPTIONS_GENERATED: false -->
### Q5 — Vas deferens: false statement

All of the following statements regarding vas deferens are true **except**:

- A. Terminal part is dilated to form ampulla
- B. It crosses the ureter in the region of the ischial spine
- C. It passes lateral to the inferior epigastric artery at the deep inguinal ring
- D. It is separated from the base of bladder by the peritoneum

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Separated from base of bladder by peritoneum** — FALSE. The vas deferens is in **direct contact** with the base of the bladder (no peritoneum between them). The terminal part forms the ampulla, it crosses the ureter near the ischial spine ("water under the bridge"), and passes lateral to the inferior epigastric artery at the deep inguinal ring.

</details>

<!-- SOURCE: medicallegends.blogspot.com AIPGMEE 2015 Anatomy | OPTIONS_GENERATED: false -->
### Q6 — Chorda tympani nerve: false statement

The following statements concerning the chorda tympani nerve are true **except** that it:

- A. Carries secretomotor fibres to the submandibular gland
- B. Joins the lingual nerve in the infratemporal fossa
- C. Is a branch of the facial nerve
- D. Contains postganglionic parasympathetic fibres

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Contains postganglionic parasympathetic fibres** — FALSE. Chorda tympani carries **preganglionic** parasympathetic fibres (from superior salivatory nucleus) that synapse in the submandibular ganglion; postganglionic fibres then innervate the submandibular and sublingual glands. It also carries taste from anterior 2/3 of tongue.

</details>

<!-- SOURCE: medicallegends.blogspot.com AIPGMEE 2015 Anatomy | OPTIONS_GENERATED: false -->
### Q7 — Sacrococcygeal joint type

The type of joint between the sacrum and the coccyx is a:

- A. Symphysis
- B. Synostosis
- C. Synchondrosis
- D. Syndesmosis

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Symphysis** — The sacrococcygeal joint is a secondary cartilaginous joint (symphysis) with a fibrocartilaginous disc between the apex of the sacrum and the base of the coccyx. May ossify (synostosis) in old age.

</details>

<!-- SOURCE: medicallegends.blogspot.com AIPGMEE 2015 Anatomy | OPTIONS_GENERATED: false -->
### Q8 — Prostatic urethra: false feature

The prostatic urethra is characterised by all of the following **except** that it:

- A. Is the widest and most dilatable part of the urethra
- B. Presents a concavity posteriorly
- C. Lies closer to the anterior surface of the prostate
- D. Receives prostatic ductules along its posterior wall

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Presents a concavity posteriorly** — FALSE. The prostatic urethra presents a concavity **anteriorly** (urethral crest projects on the posterior wall). It is the widest and most dilatable part, lies closer to the anterior surface, and prostatic ductules open on the posterior wall (in the prostatic sinuses lateral to the urethral crest).

</details>

<!-- SOURCE: medicallegends.blogspot.com AIPGMEE 2015 Anatomy | OPTIONS_GENERATED: false -->
### Q9 — Uterine lymphatic drainage: NOT a destination

The following group of lymph nodes receives lymphatics from the uterus **except**:

- A. External iliac
- B. Internal iliac
- C. Superficial inguinal
- D. Deep inguinal

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Deep inguinal** — Lymphatic drainage of uterus: fundus → para-aortic (along ovarian vessels); body → external iliac; cervix → internal iliac, external iliac, sacral; round ligament → **superficial** inguinal. Deep inguinal nodes are NOT a drainage site for the uterus.

</details>

<!-- SOURCE: medicallegends.blogspot.com AIPGMEE 2015 Anatomy | OPTIONS_GENERATED: false -->
### Q10 — Epiphyseal plate growth processes: NOT a feature

All of the following physiological processes occur during growth at the epiphyseal plate **except**:

- A. Proliferation and hypertrophy
- B. Calcification and ossification
- C. Vasculogenesis and erosion
- D. Replacement of red bone marrow with yellow bone marrow

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Replacement of red bone marrow with yellow bone marrow** — FALSE. Red-to-yellow marrow conversion is a separate developmental process occurring in the medullary cavity (begins in distal long bones in infancy, progresses centrally). At the epiphyseal plate, chondrocytes undergo: resting → proliferation → hypertrophy → calcification → ossification, with vascular invasion (vasculogenesis) and matrix erosion.

</details>

<!-- SOURCE: medicallegends.blogspot.com AIPGMEE 2015 Anatomy | OPTIONS_GENERATED: false -->
### Q11 — Benign prostatic hypertrophy: lobe enlarged

Benign prostatic hypertrophy results in obstruction of the urinary tract. The condition is associated with enlargement of the:

- A. Entire prostate gland
- B. Lateral lobes
- C. Median lobe
- D. Posterior lobes

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Median lobe** — Median lobe enlargement (transition zone) causes obstruction of the internal urethral orifice → BPH symptoms. Posterior lobe is the typical site for prostate **cancer** (peripheral zone). Lateral lobes also enlarge but predominantly the median lobe causes obstruction.

</details>

<!-- SOURCE: medicallegends.blogspot.com AIPGMEE 2015 Anatomy | OPTIONS_GENERATED: false -->
### Q12 — Per rectal examination: NOT palpable anteriorly

In an adult male, on per rectal examination, the following structures can be felt anteriorly **except**:

- A. Internal iliac lymph nodes
- B. Bulb of the penis
- C. Prostate
- D. Seminal vesicle (when enlarged)

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Internal iliac lymph nodes** — Internal iliac nodes lie **deep in the pelvis along the iliac vessels** and are NOT palpable on PR examination. Anteriorly palpable on PR: prostate (normal/enlarged), bulb of penis, enlarged seminal vesicles, bladder base, sigmoid colon.

</details>

<!-- SOURCE: medicallegends.blogspot.com AIPGMEE 2015 Anatomy | OPTIONS_GENERATED: false -->

### Q13 — Upper limb deformity in Erbs palsy

Upper limb deformity in Erb's palsy?

- A. Adduction and lateral rotation of arm
- B. Adduction and medial rotation of arm
- C. Abduction and lateral rotation of arm
- D. Abduction and medial rotation of arm

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Adduction and medial rotation of arm** — C5-C6 injury paralyzes deltoid, supraspinatus, infraspinatus, biceps; unopposed pectoralis and subscapularis cause adduction and medial rotation (waiter's tip).

</details>

---
### Q14 — Nerve roots involved in Erbs palsy

Nerve roots involved in Erb's palsy:

- A. C5, C6
- B. C6, C7
- C. C7,C8,T1
- D. C5,C6,C7,C8,T1

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. C5, C6** — Erb's palsy involves upper trunk of brachial plexus (C5-C6) from excessive head-shoulder traction; Klumpke's involves lower trunk C8-T1.

</details>

---
### Q15 — Root value of thoracodorsal nerve

Root value of thoracodorsal nerve ?

- A. C5,C6,C7
- B. C8,T1
- C. C6,C7,C8
- D. Ti T2

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. C6,C7,C8** — Thoracodorsal nerve (C6,C7,C8) arises from posterior cord of brachial plexus and supplies latissimus dorsi muscle.

</details>

---
### Q16 — Teres minor is supplied by

Teres minor is supplied by

- A. Suprascapular nerve
- B. Infrascapular nerve
- C. Thoracodorsal nerve
- D. Axillary nerve

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Axillary nerve** — Axillary nerve (C5,C6) from posterior cord supplies teres minor and deltoid; also gives upper lateral cutaneous nerve of arm.

</details>

---
### Q17 — Limb shortening

Limb shortening

- A. 1,2 true & 3,4 false
- B. 2,3 True & 1,4 false
- C. 1,2,3 true & 4 false
- D. All are true

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. All are true** — Limb shortening can be true (bone loss), apparent (pelvic tilt/adduction), supratrochanteric (above greater trochanter), and infratrochanteric (below) - all causes are valid.

</details>

---
### Q18 — Insertion of levator scapulae is

Insertion of levator scapulae is ?

- A. Lateral border of scapula
- B. Suprolateral part of scapula
- C. Superior part of medial scapula border
- D. Inferior angle of scapula

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Superior part of medial scapula border** — Levator scapulae inserts on medial border of scapula above the root of the spine; elevates and rotates scapula downward.

</details>

---
### Q19 — Number of lobes in breast

Number of lobes in breast

- A. 5
- B. 10
- C. 15
- D. 30

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. 15** — Breast contains 15-20 lobes arranged radially, each drained by a lactiferous duct opening separately at the nipple.

</details>

---
### Q20 — Which of the following arises from

Which of the following arises from infraglenoid tubercle -

- A. Longheadofbiceps
- B. Longheadoftriceps
- C. Shortheadofbiceps
- D. Coracobrachialis

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Longheadoftriceps** — Long head of triceps arises from infraglenoid tubercle of scapula; long head of biceps from supraglenoid tubercle.

</details>

---
### Q21 — Not attached on medial border of

Not attached on medial border of scapula ?

- A. Serratus anterior
- B. Levator scapulae
- C. Rhamboides major
- D. Teres major

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Teres major** — Teres major attaches to lateral border (lower part) of scapula; serratus anterior, levator scapulae, rhomboids attach to medial border.

</details>

---
### Q22 — Medial boundry of Cubital fossa

Medial boundry of Cubital fossa ?

- A. Brachioradialis
- B. Pronator teres
- C. Supinator
- D. None

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Pronator teres** — Cubital fossa medial boundary is pronator teres; lateral is brachioradialis; floor is brachialis and supinator; roof is bicipital aponeurosis.

</details>

---
### Q23 — True about lumbricals is

True about lumbricals is

- A. Flex IP joints and extends MCP joint
- B. 1st and 2nd supplied by radial nerve
- C. 3 and 4 supplied by superficial branch of ulnar
- D. Origin from tendons of flexor digitorum profundus

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Origin from tendons of flexor digitorum profundus** — Lumbricals originate from flexor digitorum profundus tendons, insert on extensor expansion, flex MCP and extend IP joints.

</details>

---
### Q24 — Lateral boundary of cubital fossa is

Lateral boundary of cubital fossa is formed by:

- A. Brachioradialis
- B. Pronator teres
- C. Brachialis
- D. Biceps

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Brachioradialis** — Brachioradialis (radial nerve, C5-C6) forms lateral boundary of cubital fossa; pronator teres forms medial boundary.

</details>

---
### Q25 — Muscle of Arm with additional supinator

Muscle of Arm with additional supinator action?

- A. Brachialis
- B. Biceps
- C. Coracobrachialis
- D. Triceps

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Biceps** — Biceps brachii is the most powerful supinator of forearm (when elbow flexed), in addition to flexing elbow.

</details>

---
### Q26 — The nerve supply of nail bed

The nerve supply of nail bed of index finger is ?

- A. Superficial br of radial nerve
- B. Deep br of radial nerve
- C. Median nerve
- D. Ulnar nerve

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Median nerve** — Median nerve supplies palmar and dorsal nail bed skin of lateral 3.5 digits including index finger via digital branches.

</details>

---
### Q27 — Which leaves the pelvis

Which leaves the pelvis ?

- A. Piriformis
- B. Sciatic nerve
- C. Superior gluteal vessel
- D. Inferior gluteal vessel

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Sciatic nerve** — Sciatic nerve leaves pelvis through greater sciatic foramen below piriformis to enter gluteal region; piriformis is the landmark.

</details>

---
### Q28 — Abduction and adduction of foot occurs

Abduction and adduction of foot occurs at which joints

- A. Ankle
- B. Subtalar
- C. Tarso-metatarsal
- D. None

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Subtalar** — Subtalar (talocalcaneal) joint allows inversion-eversion; abduction-adduction of foot accompanies these movements at subtalar joint.

</details>

---
### Q29 — All are true about femoral triangle,

All are true about femoral triangle, EXCEPT?

- A. Lateral margin is formed by sartorius
- B. Floor is formed by adductor longus
- C. Contains the femoral vessels
- D. None of the above

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. None of the above** — Femoral triangle: lateral border sartorius, medial adductor longus, base inguinal ligament; floor iliopsoas/pectineus/adductor longus; contains femoral vessels.

</details>

---
### Q30 — Content of femoral canal

Content of femoral canal

- A. Femoral branch of genitofemoral nerve
- B. Genital branch of genitofemoral nerve
- C. Femoral vein
- D. Lymph node

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Lymph node** — Femoral canal contains lymph node of Cloquet, lymphatics, and loose areolar tissue; allows venous distension and is the site of femoral hernia.

</details>

---
### Q31 — A patient presents with defective adduction

A patient presents with defective adduction of the hip joint and pains in the hip and knee joint. Which nerve is involved

- A. Obturator nerve
- B. Femoral nerve
- C. Saphenous nerve
- D. Sciatic nerve

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Obturator nerve** — Obturator nerve (L2-L4) supplies adductors of thigh; gives articular branches to hip and knee joints (Hilton's law - referred pain).

</details>

---
### Q32 — Structure which lies outside the femoral

Structure which lies outside the femoral sheath

- A. Femoral artery
- B. Femoral nerve
- C. Femoral vein
- D. Genitofemoral nerve

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Femoral nerve** — Femoral nerve lies lateral to and outside the femoral sheath; sheath contains femoral artery, vein, and canal (lymphatics).

</details>

---
### Q33 — Which structure(s) passes behind the inguinal

Which structure(s) passes behind the inguinal ligament:

- A. Femoral branch of genitofemoral nerve
- B. Femoral vein
- C. Psoas major
- D. All

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. All** — Behind inguinal ligament: psoas major, iliacus, femoral nerve/artery/vein, lymphatics, femoral branch of genitofemoral nerve, lateral cutaneous nerve of thigh.

</details>

---
### Q34 — Lateral border of ischeorectal fossa is

Lateral border of ischeorectal fossa is formed by?

- A. Gluteus maximus
- B. Perineal membrane
- C. Pelvic diaphram
- D. Obturator internus

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Obturator internus** — Ischiorectal fossa lateral wall: obturator internus muscle with its fascia containing pudendal canal (Alcock's); medial wall is levator ani.

</details>

---
### Q35 — Arterial branches which supply the head

Arterial branches which supply the head and neck of the femur is/are:

- A. Medial circumflex artery
- B. Lateral circumflex artery
- C. Profunda femoris artery
- D. All

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. All** — Head/neck of femur supplied by medial and lateral circumflex femoral arteries (branches of profunda femoris), artery of ligamentum teres.

</details>

---
### Q36 — Femoral nerve supplies all except

Femoral nerve supplies all except ?

- A. Pectineus
- B. Sartorius
- C. Vastus medialis
- D. Obturator externus

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Obturator externus** — Femoral nerve (L2-L4) supplies anterior compartment thigh; obturator externus is supplied by obturator nerve (posterior division).

</details>

---
### Q37 — Superior gluteal nerve does not supply

Superior gluteal nerve does not supply ?

- A. Tensor fasciae latae
- B. Gluteus medius
- C. Gluteus minimus
- D. Gluteus maximus

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Gluteus maximus** — Superior gluteal nerve (L4,L5,S1) supplies gluteus medius, minimus, tensor fasciae latae; gluteus maximus is supplied by inferior gluteal nerve.

</details>

---
### Q38 — What is true about adductors of

What is true about adductors of thigh –

- A. Ischial head of adductor magnus is an adductor
- B. Profunda femoris artery is the main blood supply
- C. Ischial head of adductor magnus originates from adductor
- D. Adductor magnus is the largest muscle

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Adductor magnus is the largest muscle** — Adductor magnus is the largest muscle of medial compartment; has adductor (pubic) and hamstring (ischial) parts with dual nerve supply.

</details>

---
### Q39 — A nerve injured in radical neck

A nerve injured in radical neck dissection leads to loss of sensation in medial side of the arm, nerve injured is?

- A. Long thoracic nerve
- B. Thoracodorsal nerve
- C. Dorsal scapular nerve
- D. Medial cutaneous nerve of arm

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Medial cutaneous nerve of arm** — Medial cutaneous nerve of arm (T1) from medial cord supplies sensation to medial arm; vulnerable in radical neck/axillary dissection.

</details>

---
### Q40 — Secondary ossification center for lower end

Secondary ossification center for lower end of femur?

- A. Present at birth
- B. Appears at 6 months of age
- C. Appears at 1 year of age
- D. Appears at 5 years of age

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Present at birth** — Lower end of femur ossification center appears at 9th intrauterine month - present at birth; medicolegal evidence of full-term fetus.

</details>

---
### Q41 — Lower limit of superior mediastinum is

Lower limit of superior mediastinum is at which level -

- A. Ti
- B. T1
- C. T8
- D. T10

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. T1** — Superior mediastinum extends from thoracic inlet to sternal angle of Louis (T4-T5 disc level, plane of Ludwig); contains arch of aorta.

</details>

---
### Q42 — Azygous vein drains into

Azygous vein drains into:

- A. Right subcostal vein
- B. Superior vena cava
- C. Braciocephalic
- D. Right ascending lumbar vein

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Superior vena cava** — Azygos vein arches over right lung root at T4 level and drains into superior vena cava; receives hemiazygos and accessory hemiazygos.

</details>

---
### Q43 — Arch of aorta begins and ends

Arch of aorta begins and ends at which level:

- A. T2
- B. T3
- C. T4
- D. T5

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. T4** — **C. T4** — Arch of aorta begins at the sternal angle (manubriosternal joint) at T4-T5 disc level and ends at the same level on the left side, forming a U-shape.

</details>

---
### Q44 — Which among the following is NOT

Which among the following is NOT a branch of Arch of Aorta?

- A. Brachiocephalic
- B. Right common carotid
- C. Left common carotid
- D. Left Subclavian

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Right common carotid** — **B. Right common carotid** — Three branches of arch: brachiocephalic, left common carotid, left subclavian; right common carotid arises from brachiocephalic trunk, not directly from arch.

</details>

---
### Q45 — Intercostal nerve is a branch of

Intercostal nerve is a branch of ?

- A. Brachial plexus
- B. Dorsal rami of thoracic spinal nerves
- C. Ventral rami of thoracic spinal nerves
- D. Ventral rami of cervical spinal nerves

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Ventral rami of thoracic spinal nerves** — **C. Ventral rami of thoracic spinal nerves** — Intercostal nerves (T1-T11) are anterior/ventral rami of thoracic spinal nerves; T12 is subcostal; they supply intercostal muscles and overlying skin.

</details>

---
### Q46 — Distance of cricopharynx from incisor teeth

Distance of cricopharynx from incisor teeth

- A. 15 cm
- B. 22.5 cm
- C. 27.5 cm
- D. 40 cm

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. 15 cm** — **A. 15 cm** — Cricopharyngeus (upper esophageal sphincter, at C6) lies 15 cm from the incisor teeth; this is the narrowest part of the esophagus.

</details>

---
### Q47 — Anterior part of interventricular septum is

Anterior part of interventricular septum is supplied by -

- A. Right coronary artery
- B. Left coronary artery
- C. Posterior descending coronary artery
- D. None

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Left coronary artery** — **B. Left coronary artery** — Anterior two-thirds of interventricular septum supplied by septal branches of LAD (a branch of left coronary); posterior 1/3 by PDA from RCA.

</details>

---
### Q48 — All veins open in sinus venarum

All veins open in sinus venarum except -

- A. SVC
- B. Coronary sinus
- C. Anterior cardiac vein
- D. Small cardiac vein

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Small cardiac vein** — **D. Small cardiac vein** — Sinus venarum (smooth part of right atrium) receives SVC, IVC, and coronary sinus; small cardiac vein drains into coronary sinus, not directly into sinus venarum.

</details>

---
### Q49 — True about atrioventricular groove are all

True about atrioventricular groove are all except ?

- A. Contains left anterior descending coronary artery
- B. Also called coronary sulcus
- C. Contains right coronary artery
- D. Contains circumflex branch of left coronary artery

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Contains left anterior descending coronary artery** — **A. Contains left anterior descending coronary artery** — AV (coronary) sulcus contains RCA, circumflex artery, and coronary sinus; LAD lies in the anterior interventricular groove, not AV groove.

</details>

---
### Q50 — Which vein is found at the

Which vein is found at the apex of the heart ?

- A. Great cardiac vein
- B. Coronary Sinus
- C. Anterior cardiac vein
- D. Middle cardiac vein

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Great cardiac vein** — **A. Great cardiac vein** — Great cardiac vein begins at the apex, ascends in the anterior interventricular groove alongside LAD, then drains into the coronary sinus.

</details>

---
### Q51 — Which of the following does not

Which of the following does not directly drain into right atrium ?

- A. Great cardiac vein
- B. Anterior cardiac vein
- C. Thebasian vein
- D. Venae cordis minimi

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Great cardiac vein** — **A. Great cardiac vein** — Great cardiac vein drains into the coronary sinus, which then drains into the right atrium; others (anterior cardiac, Thebesian, venae cordis minimi) drain directly.

</details>

---
### Q52 — Tributary of coronary sinus

Tributary of coronary sinus ?

- A. Anterior cardiac vein
- B. Thebesion vein
- C. Smallest cardiac vein
- D. Great cardiac vein

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Great cardiac vein** — **D. Great cardiac vein** — Coronary sinus tributaries: great, middle, small cardiac veins, posterior vein of LV, and oblique vein of Marshall; anterior cardiac veins drain directly into RA.

</details>

---
### Q53 — Which are segments of upper lobe

Which are segments of upper lobe of right lung ?

- A. Anterior, posterior, media
- B. Lateral, medial, superior
- C. Apical, anterior, posterior
- D. Basal, medial, lateral

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Apical, anterior, posterior** — **C. Apical, anterior, posterior** — Right upper lobe has three bronchopulmonary segments: apical, anterior, and posterior; left upper lobe has apicoposterior, anterior, and lingular segments.

</details>

---
### Q54 — What is the uppermost structure in

What is the uppermost structure in left lung hilum?

- A. Pulmonary artery
- B. Pulmonary vein
- C. Bronchial artery
- D. Left mainstem bronchus

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Pulmonary artery** — **A. Pulmonary artery** — Left lung hilum mnemonic 'PABV' from above downwards: Pulmonary artery (top), Bronchus, then pulmonary veins; on right, bronchus is uppermost (eparterial).

</details>

---
### Q55 — Base of the heart is formed

Base of the heart is formed by ?

- A. Right atrium
- B. Right ventricle
- C. Left atrium
- D. Left ventricle

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Left atrium** — **C. Left atrium** — Base of heart (posterior surface) is formed mainly by the left atrium with pulmonary vein openings; a small contribution from right atrium; faces the vertebral column.

</details>

---
### Q56 — Right border of heart is formed

Right border of heart is formed by ?

- A. Right ventricle
- B. Right atrium
- C. SVC
- D. IVC

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Right atrium** — **B. Right atrium** — Right border of heart is formed entirely by the right atrium; extends from the right 3rd costal cartilage to the right 6th costal cartilage on the sternum.

</details>

---
### Q57 — Sensory supply to tongue is by

Sensory supply to tongue is by all, EXCEPT?

- A. Lingual nerve
- B. Vagus nerve
- C. Glossopharyngeal nerve
- D. None of the above

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. None of the above** — **D. None of the above** — Tongue sensory: anterior 2/3 general by lingual (V3), taste by chorda tympani (VII); posterior 1/3 by glossopharyngeal (IX); posterior-most by vagus (X).

</details>

---
### Q58 — Internal anal sphincter is a apart

Internal anal sphincter is a apart of:

- A. Puborectalis muscle
- B. Deep perineal muscles
- C. Internal longitudinal fibers
- D. Internal circular fibers

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Internal circular fibers** — **D. Internal circular fibers** — Internal anal sphincter is an involuntary thickening of the inner circular smooth muscle layer of the rectum; external sphincter is voluntary skeletal muscle.

</details>

---
### Q59 — Shortest part of male urethra is

Shortest part of male urethra is :

- A. Prostatic
- B. Membranous
- C. Bulbar
- D. Penile

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Membranous** — **B. Membranous** — Membranous urethra is shortest (~1.5–2 cm), passing through urogenital diaphragm; narrowest and least dilatable; prone to injury in pelvic fracture.

</details>

---
### Q60 — Posterior relation of neck of pancreas

Posterior relation of neck of pancreas ?

- A. IVC
- B. Origin of postal vein
- C. Aorta
- D. Common bile duct

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Origin of postal vein** — **B. Origin of postal vein** — Portal vein forms posterior to the neck of pancreas by union of superior mesenteric and splenic veins; this is a key surgical landmark.

</details>

---
### Q61 — The right suprarenal vein drains into

The right suprarenal vein drains into the

- A. Inferior vena cava
- B. Right renal vein
- C. Right Gonadal vein
- D. Left Renal vein

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Inferior vena cava** — **A. Inferior vena cava** — Right suprarenal vein is short and drains directly into the IVC; left suprarenal vein drains into the left renal vein (similar to left gonadal pattern).

</details>

---
### Q62 — Suprarenal gland gets its blood supply

Suprarenal gland gets its blood supply from all of the following arteries except:

- A. Aorta
- B. Renal artery
- C. Inferior phrenic artery
- D. Superior mesentric artery

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Superior mesentric artery** — **D. Superior mesentric artery** — Adrenal blood supply: superior suprarenal (inferior phrenic), middle suprarenal (aorta), inferior suprarenal (renal artery); SMA does not supply suprarenals.

</details>

---
### Q63 — Inferior artery pancreaticoduodenal artery is a

Inferior artery? pancreaticoduodenal artery is a branch of which of the following

- A. Splenic artery
- B. Left gastric artery
- C. Gastroduodenal artery
- D. Superior mesenteric artery

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Superior mesenteric artery** — **D. Superior mesenteric artery** — Inferior pancreaticoduodenal artery branches from SMA, anastomoses with superior pancreaticoduodenal (from gastroduodenal) at foregut-midgut junction.

</details>

---
### Q64 — All of the following are branches

All of the following are branches of splenic artery, except?

- A. Hilar branches
- B. Short Gastric Artery
- C. Arteria Pancreatica Magna
- D. Right Gastroepiploic Artery

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Right Gastroepiploic Artery** — **D. Right Gastroepiploic Artery** — Splenic artery branches: pancreatic (arteria pancreatica magna), short gastric, left gastroepiploic, hilar branches; right gastroepiploic arises from gastroduodenal artery.

</details>

---
### Q65 — Esophageal varices occur in which portion

Esophageal varices occur in which portion of esophagus?

- A. Upper
- B. Middle
- C. Lower
- D. All sites

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Lower** — **C. Lower** — Lower esophageal varices occur at site of portosystemic anastomosis between left gastric vein (portal) and esophageal tributaries of azygos (systemic) in portal hypertension.

</details>

---
### Q66 — External oblique forms all except

External oblique forms all except?

- A. Lacunar ligament
- B. Pectineal ligament
- C. Conjoint tendon
- D. Inguinal ligament

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Conjoint tendon** — **C. Conjoint tendon** — External oblique aponeurosis forms inguinal, lacunar, pectineal (Cooper's), and reflected ligaments; conjoint tendon is formed by internal oblique and transversus abdominis.

</details>

---
### Q67 — Inferior epigastric artery forms the boundry

Inferior epigastric artery forms the boundry of ?

- A. Femoral triangle
- B. Hesselbach's triangle
- C. Adductor canal
- D. Popliteal triangle

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Hesselbach's triangle** — **B. Hesselbach's triangle** — Hesselbach's (inguinal) triangle boundaries: inferior epigastric artery (lateral), rectus abdominis (medial), inguinal ligament (inferior); site of direct inguinal hernia.

</details>

---
### Q68 — The boundaries of the interconnection between

The boundaries of the interconnection between greater sac and lesser sac of peritoneum known as 'Foramen of Winslow' are all, EXCEPT:

- A. Caudate lobe of liver
- B. Inferior vena cava
- C. Free border of lesser omentum
- D. 4th part of Duodenum

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. 4th part of Duodenum** — **D. 4th part of Duodenum** — Foramen of Winslow boundaries: caudate lobe (superior), 1st part of duodenum (inferior), IVC (posterior), free border of lesser omentum/portal triad (anterior).

</details>

---
### Q69 — Anorectal angle is formed due to

Anorectal angle is formed due to action of -

- A. Internal anal sphincter
- B. Circular muscle layer of smooth muscles
- C. Longitudinal muscle layer of smooth muscle
- D. Puborectalis

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Puborectalis** — **D. Puborectalis** — Puborectalis sling (part of levator ani) loops around anorectal junction, pulling it forward to create ~90° anorectal angle essential for fecal continence.

</details>

---
### Q70 — Prostate analogue in female is -

Prostate analogue in female is -

- A. Skene gland
- B. Bulbourethral gland
- C. Great vestibular gland
- D. Bartholin's gland

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Skene gland** — **A. Skene gland** — Skene's (paraurethral) glands are female homologues of the prostate; both develop from urogenital sinus and secrete PSA-like fluid into the urethra.

</details>

---
### Q71 — All are true about 1st part

All are true about 1st part of duodenum, except ?

- A. 5 cm long
- B. Is superior part
- C. Develops from foregut
- D. Supplied by superior mesenteric artery

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Supplied by superior mesenteric artery** — **D. Supplied by superior mesenteric artery** — First part of duodenum is foregut derivative, supplied by gastroduodenal/superior pancreaticoduodenal (from celiac); SMA supplies from major papilla distally (midgut).

</details>

---
### Q72 — Ventral pancreatic duct give rise to

Ventral pancreatic duct give rise to ?

- A. Body
- B. Tail
- C. Neck
- D. Uncinate process

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Uncinate process** — **D. Uncinate process** — Ventral pancreatic bud forms the uncinate process and inferior part of the head; dorsal bud forms body, tail, and superior head; ducts fuse to form main pancreatic duct.

</details>

---
### Q73 — The muscles attached to perineal body

The muscles attached to perineal body are A/E

- A. Ischiocavernosum
- B. Bulbospongiosm
- C. Superficial transverse perinea
- D. Deep transverse perinea

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Ischiocavernosum** — **A. Ischiocavernosum** — Ischiocavernosus attaches to the ischial ramus and crus, NOT the perineal body; perineal body receives bulbospongiosus, superficial and deep transverse perinei, external anal sphincter, and levator ani.

</details>

---
### Q74 — Anterior relation to upper part of

Anterior relation to upper part of rectum in male is-

- A. Rectovesical pouch
- B. Sacrum
- C. Seminal vesicle
- D. Ductus deference

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Rectovesical pouch** — **A. Rectovesical pouch** — Upper third of rectum is covered by peritoneum anteriorly, forming the rectovesical pouch (between rectum and bladder) in males; seminal vesicles and ductus deferens relate to the middle third.

</details>

---
### Q75 — FALSE for seminal vesicles

FALSE for seminal vesicles:

- A. Contains large amount of fructose
- B. Stores sperms
- C. Situated on either side near prostate
- D. Secretion of seminal vesicle gives mucoid consistency to

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Stores sperms** — **B. Stores sperms** — Seminal vesicles are secretory glands producing fructose-rich fluid (60% of semen volume); spermatozoa are stored in the epididymis and ductus deferens, not the seminal vesicles.

</details>

---
### Q76 — Sensory nerve supply of gall bladder

Sensory nerve supply of gall bladder is through -

- A. Vagus nerve
- B. Trigeminal nerve
- C. Parasympathetic nerve
- D. Facial nerve

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Vagus nerve** — **A. Vagus nerve** — Gallbladder receives parasympathetic (motor) and visceral sensory supply via the vagus nerve (CN X), with sympathetic supply from T7–T9 via the celiac plexus; referred pain goes to right shoulder tip via phrenic nerve.

</details>

---
### Q77 — Uvula vesicae seen in bladder is

Uvula vesicae seen in bladder is formed from the following structure ?

- A. Median lobe of prostate
- B. Lateral lobe of prostate
- C. Anterior lobe of prostate
- D. Posterior lobe of prostate

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Median lobe of prostate** — **A. Median lobe of prostate** — Uvula vesicae is an elevation at the internal urethral orifice formed by the median lobe of the prostate projecting into the bladder base, often enlarged in BPH causing urinary obstruction.

</details>

---
### Q78 — Floor of Petit triangle is formed

Floor of Petit triangle is formed by?

- A. Sacrospinalis
- B. Internal oblique
- C. Rectus abdominis
- D. Fascia Transversalis

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Internal oblique** — **B. Internal oblique** — Petit's (lumbar) triangle has the floor formed by the internal oblique muscle; its boundaries are the iliac crest inferiorly, latissimus dorsi posteriorly, and external oblique anteriorly — a site for lumbar hernia.

</details>

---
### Q79 — In which of the following vessels

In which of the following vessels transverse mesocolon seen ?

- A. Right colic artery
- B. Left colic artery
- C. Middle colic artery
- D. Iliocolic artery

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Middle colic artery** — **C. Middle colic artery** — The transverse mesocolon contains the middle colic artery (branch of superior mesenteric artery), which runs within its two peritoneal layers to supply the transverse colon.

</details>

---
### Q80 — Nerve suply of cremastric muscle

Nerve suply of cremastric muscle ?

- A. Pudendal nerve
- B. Femoral branch of genitofemoral
- C. Genital branch of genitofemoral nerve
- D. Ilioinguinal nerve

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Genital branch of genitofemoral nerve** — **C. Genital branch of genitofemoral nerve** — The cremaster muscle (derived from internal oblique) is supplied by the genital branch of the genitofemoral nerve (L1–L2); this reflex arc mediates the cremasteric reflex.

</details>

---
### Q81 — True about the anatomy of great

True about the anatomy of great saphenous vein:

- A. Starts as a continuation of medial marginal vein
- B. Ends of femoral vein 2.5 cm below the inguinal ligament
- C. There are 2 - 5 valves below the knee.
- D. Ascends 2.5 - 3 cm behind tibial malleolus

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Starts as a continuation of medial marginal vein** — **A. Starts as a continuation of medial marginal vein** — The great saphenous vein begins at the medial end of the dorsal venous arch (medial marginal vein) at the foot, ascends anterior to the medial malleolus, and drains into the femoral vein.

</details>

---
### Q82 — Posterior relation of right kidney are

Posterior relation of right kidney are all except -

- A. Diaphragm
- B. Subcostal nerve
- C. 11th rib
- D. Ilioinguinal nerve

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. 11th rib** — **C. 11th rib** — The right kidney is related posteriorly to the 12th rib (not 11th); both kidneys are related to the diaphragm, subcostal nerve, and iliohypogastric and ilioinguinal nerves; the 11th rib relates to the left kidney.

</details>

---
### Q83 — Not True about blood supply of

Not True about blood supply of kidney -

- A. Renal vein drains into IVC
- B. Renal artery is a branch of common iliac artery
- C. Right renal artery passes behind IVC
- D. Branches of renal artery are end arteries

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Renal artery is a branch of common iliac artery** — **B. Renal artery is a branch of common iliac artery** — The renal artery is a direct lateral branch of the abdominal aorta at L1–L2, not the common iliac; the right renal artery passes posterior to the IVC to reach the right kidney.

</details>

---
### Q84 — All pass through deep inguinal ring,

All pass through deep inguinal ring, EXCEPT?

- A. Spermatic cord
- B. Internal spermatic fascia
- C. Round ligament
- D. Ilioinguinal nerve

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Ilioinguinal nerve** — **D. Ilioinguinal nerve** — The ilioinguinal nerve (L1) enters the inguinal canal through a slit in the internal oblique muscle medial to the deep ring, traverses the canal, and exits through the superficial ring; it does NOT pass through the deep inguinal ring.

</details>

---
### Q85 — Superficial inguinal ring is a defect

Superficial inguinal ring is a defect in the:

- A. Internal oblique aponeurosis
- B. External oblique aponeurosis
- C. Transverse abdominis aponeurosis
- D. Internal oblique muscle

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. External oblique aponeurosis** — **B. External oblique aponeurosis** — The superficial inguinal ring is a triangular gap in the external oblique aponeurosis just above and lateral to the pubic tubercle, through which the spermatic cord (male) or round ligament (female) exits.

</details>

---
### Q86 — Superficial perineal space contains

Superficial perineal space contains ?

- A. Sphincter urethrae muscle
- B. Ischiocavernosus muscle
- C. Deep transverse perinei muscle
- D. Bulbourethral gland

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Ischiocavernosus muscle** — **B. Ischiocavernosus muscle** — The superficial perineal pouch (between perineal membrane and superficial perineal fascia) contains ischiocavernosus, bulbospongiosus, superficial transverse perinei muscles, and roots of the penis/clitoris.

</details>

---
### Q87 — 3d part of duodenum is not

3'd part of duodenum is not related -

- A. Superior mesenteric vessels
- B. Right ureter
- C. Head of pancreas
- D. Quadrate lobe of liver

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Quadrate lobe of liver** — **D. Quadrate lobe of liver** — The 3rd (horizontal) part of the duodenum crosses anterior to the aorta/IVC and right ureter, and is crossed anteriorly by the superior mesenteric vessels; the quadrate lobe of liver relates to the 1st part of duodenum.

</details>

---
### Q88 — Correct sequence of uterine blood flow

Correct sequence of uterine blood flow -

- A. Uterine A - Arcuate A Radial A Spiral A
- B. Uterine A Radial A - Arcuate A Spiral A
- C. Uterine A Spiral A Radial A Arcuate A
- D. Uterine A Arcuate A → Spiral A - Radial A

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Uterine A - Arcuate A Radial A Spiral A** — **A. Uterine A → Arcuate A → Radial A → Spiral A** — Uterine artery (branch of internal iliac) gives arcuate arteries in the myometrium, which give radial arteries penetrating inward, then spiral arteries supplying the endometrium and lost during menstruation.

</details>

---
### Q89 — Nerve supply of stapedius is

Nerve supply of stapedius is:

- A. 2nd nerve
- B. 3rd nerve
- C. 5th nerve
- D. 7th nerve

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. 7th nerve** — **D. 7th nerve** — Stapedius muscle is supplied by the nerve to stapedius, a branch of the facial nerve (CN VII) within the facial canal; its paralysis causes hyperacusis (abnormal sensitivity to loud sounds) in Bell's palsy.

</details>

---
### Q90 — Stapedius pulls stapes in which direction

Stapedius pulls stapes in which direction -

- A. Anterior
- B. Superior
- C. Inferior
- D. Posterior

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Posterior** — **D. Posterior** — Stapedius pulls the neck of the stapes posteriorly, tilting the footplate and reducing oscillation amplitude; this acoustic reflex (CN VII arc) protects the inner ear from loud sounds and is lost in facial nerve palsy causing hyperacusis.

</details>

---
### Q91 — Sternocleidomastoid muscle is examined by

Sternocleidomastoid muscle is examined by

- A. Turning the head towards the same side
- B. Turning the head towards opposite side
- C. Shrugging of shoulder
- D. Overhead abduction

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Turning the head towards opposite side** — **B. Turning the head towards opposite side** — SCM rotates the head to the contralateral side and flexes it ipsilaterally; to test, patient turns head against resistance toward the opposite side, making the contracting SCM prominent — tests CN XI (accessory nerve).

</details>

---
### Q92 — The nerve which has the longest

The nerve which has the longest intracranial course is:

- A. Fourth cranial nerve
- B. Third cranial nerve
- C. Sixth cranial nerve
- D. Fifth cranial nerve

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Fourth cranial nerve** — **A. Fourth cranial nerve** — The trochlear nerve (CN IV) has the longest intracranial course (~75 mm), exits dorsally from the midbrain, winds around the brainstem, and enters the orbit through the superior orbital fissure to supply superior oblique.

</details>

---
### Q93 — Largest cranial nerve is

Largest cranial nerve is:

- A. Trochlear
- B. Trigeminal
- C. Oculomotor
- D. Vagus

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Trigeminal** — **B. Trigeminal** — CN V (trigeminal) is the largest cranial nerve, with the thickest sensory root (portio major) carrying general sensation from the face, scalp, cornea, and meninges, plus a smaller motor root supplying muscles of mastication.

</details>

---
### Q94 — Olfactory Great cerebral vein of Galen

Olfactory Great cerebral vein of Galen drains into -

- A. Cavernous sinus
- B. Basal vein
- C. Internal cerebral vein
- D. Straight sinus

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Straight sinus** — **D. Straight sinus** — The great cerebral vein of Galen (formed by union of two internal cerebral veins) drains into the straight sinus at the junction with the inferior sagittal sinus; the straight sinus then drains into the confluence of sinuses.

</details>

---
### Q95 — Superficial middle cerebral vein drains into

Superficial middle cerebral vein drains into -

- A. Internal cerebral vein
- B. Cavernous sinus
- C. Great cerebral vein of Galen
- D. Straight sinus

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Cavernous sinus** — **B. Cavernous sinus** — The superficial middle cerebral vein runs along the lateral sulcus and drains into the cavernous sinus anteriorly; it connects superiorly to the superior sagittal sinus via the vein of Trolard and inferiorly via the vein of Labbé.

</details>

---
### Q96 — Which of the following structures seen

Which of the following structures seen in the cavernous sinus?

- A. Maxillary division of V nerve
- B. Mandibular division of V nerve
- C. Internal carotid artery
- D. Facial nerve

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Internal carotid artery** — **C. Internal carotid artery** — The internal carotid artery (with its sympathetic plexus) passes through the cavernous sinus; CN III, IV, V1, V2, and VI run in its walls or within it, making it a critical anatomical crossroads for clinical pathology.

</details>

---
### Q97 — First bone to start ossifying -

First bone to start ossifying -

- A. Femur
- B. Tibia
- C. Scapula
- D. Mandible

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Mandible** — **D. Mandible** — The mandible is the first bone to ossify, beginning in the 6th week of intrauterine life via intramembranous ossification from the membrane overlying Meckel's cartilage; clavicle ossifies simultaneously and is sometimes cited first.

</details>

---
### Q98 — What structure passes through the quandrangular

What structure passes through the quandrangular space ?

- A. Axillary nerve
- B. Radial nerve
- C. Median nerve
- D. Brachial Artery

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Axillary nerve** — **A. Axillary nerve** — The quadrangular space (bounded by teres major, teres minor, long head of triceps, and surgical neck of humerus) transmits the axillary nerve and posterior circumflex humeral vessels; damage causes deltoid paralysis and loss of shoulder abduction.

</details>

---
### Q99 — Inner plexus in GIT is

Inner plexus in GIT is ?

- A. Mucosal plexus
- B. Submucosal plexus
- C. Auerbach's plexus
- D. Myenteric plexus

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Submucosal plexus** — **B. Submucosal plexus** — Meissner's (submucosal) plexus is the inner enteric plexus lying in the submucosa, controlling secretion and blood flow; Auerbach's (myenteric) plexus lies between circular and longitudinal muscle layers controlling motility.

</details>

---
### Q100 — Relationship between insulin and glucose concentra

Relationship between insulin and glucose concentration?

- A. Linear
- B. Hyperbola
- C. Sigmoidal
- D. Bell Shaped

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Sigmoidal** — **C. Sigmoidal** — The insulin secretion vs. blood glucose concentration relationship follows a sigmoidal (S-shaped) curve: minimal response at low glucose, steep rise through the physiological range (4–10 mmol/L), then plateau at maximal secretion.

</details>

---
### Q101 — Drugs which are used in acute

Drugs which are used in acute asthma include?

- A. Budesonide
- B. Terbutaline
- C. Salbutamole
- D. Theophylline

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Theophylline** — **D. Theophylline** — IV aminophylline/theophylline (phosphodiesterase inhibitor causing bronchodilation via increased cAMP) is used in acute severe asthma unresponsive to beta-2 agonists; budesonide is inhaled preventer, not acute reliever.

</details>

---
### Q102 — Just before the birth which epiphysis

Just before the birth which epiphysis appears?

- A. Lower end of femur
- B. Upper end of humerus
- C. Lower end of fibula
- D. Upper end of tibia

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Lower end of femur** — **A. Lower end of femur** — The distal femoral epiphysis (Béclard's epiphysis) appears at 36 weeks gestation, just before birth, and is used in forensic medicine to estimate gestational age; its presence indicates a mature, near-term fetus.

</details>

---
### Q103 — Granuloma inguinale is caused by

Granuloma inguinale is caused by:

- A. H. ducreyi
- B. Chlamydia trachomatis
- C. Treponema pallidum
- D. Calymmatobacterium

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Calymmatobacterium** — **D. Calymmatobacterium** — Granuloma inguinale (donovanosis) is caused by Klebsiella granulomatis (formerly Calymmatobacterium granulomatis); diagnosed by Donovan bodies (bipolar-staining intracellular rods) in tissue smear; treated with azithromycin.

</details>

---
### Q104 — Sixth cranial nerve palsy causes of

Sixth cranial nerve palsy causes of left eye causes?

- A. Accomodation paresis in left gaze
- B. Ptosis of left eye
- C. Adduction weakness of left eye
- D. Diplopia in left gaze

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Diplopia in left gaze** — **D. Diplopia in left gaze** — CN VI (abducens) palsy paralyses the lateral rectus; the eye cannot abduct, causing uncrossed (homonymous) diplopia maximal in the direction of the paralysed muscle — left CN VI palsy causes diplopia on leftward gaze.

</details>

---
### Q105 — Which of the following is the

Which of the following is the common cause of respiratory failure type 2 ?

- A. Chronic bronchitis exacerbation
- B. Acute attack asthma
- C. ARDS
- D. Pneumonia

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Chronic bronchitis exacerbation** — **A. Chronic bronchitis exacerbation** — Type 2 (hypercapnic) respiratory failure involves elevated PaCO2 due to alveolar hypoventilation; COPD/chronic bronchitis with exacerbation is the classic cause, as opposed to Type 1 (hypoxaemic) seen in ARDS and pneumonia.

</details>

---
### Q106 — Gastric carcinoma is associated with all

Gastric carcinoma is associated with all EXCEPT ?

- A. Inactivation of p53
- B. Over expression of C-erb
- C. Over expression of C-met
- D. Activation of RAS

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Activation of RAS** — **D. Activation of RAS** — Gastric carcinoma is associated with p53 inactivation, c-erb B2 overexpression, and c-met overexpression; RAS activation is associated with colorectal, pancreatic, and lung cancers, not typically gastric adenocarcinoma.

</details>

### Q107 — Hellers myotomy is done for September

Heller's myotomy is done for: September 2007, 2009, 2010

- A. Esophageal carcinoma
- B. Pyloric hypertrophy
- C. Achalasia cardia
- D. Inguinal hernia

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Achalasia cardia** — **C. Achalasia cardia** — Heller's cardiomyotomy involves dividing the lower oesophageal sphincter muscle fibres to relieve functional obstruction in achalasia cardia, where absent Auerbach's plexus ganglia cause failure of LOS relaxation.

</details>

### Q108 — year old male is diagnosed with

A patient (age not captured in source) who is a year old male is diagnosed with carcinoma penis. The surgeon must look out for which lymphnodes

- A. Para aortic
- B. External iliac
- C. Internal Iliac
- D. Inguinal

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Inguinal** — **D. Inguinal** — Carcinoma of the penis drains primarily to superficial inguinal lymph nodes (first echelon), then deep inguinal, then external iliac nodes; sentinel node biopsy of inguinal nodes is standard for nodal staging.

</details>

### Q109 — year old male presenting with penile

A patient (age not captured in source) who is a year old male presenting with penile cancer extending upto the glans penis is treated with

- A. Partial Penectomy with 2 cm margin
- B. Circumcision
- C. Partial penectomy with Inguinal nodes exploration
- D. Partial penectomy with 4 cm margin

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Partial Penectomy with 2 cm margin** — **A. Partial Penectomy with 2 cm margin** — Penile carcinoma limited to the glans is treated with partial penectomy with a 2 cm clear surgical margin; total penectomy is reserved for proximal shaft involvement where a 2 cm margin cannot be achieved.

</details>

### Q110 — Most common site for bone marrow

Most common site for bone marrow aspiration in neonates is -

- A. Anterior superior iliac crest
- B. Posterior superior iliac crest
- C. Sternum
- D. Anteromedial tibia

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Anteromedial tibia** — **D. Anteromedial tibia** — In neonates and infants, the anteromedial surface of the proximal tibia is the preferred site for bone marrow aspiration/intraosseous access due to its flat, easily palpable surface and thin overlying cortex.

</details>

---
### Q111 — Puff of smoke appearance on cerebral

Puff of smoke appearance on cerebral angiography is seen in:

- A. ACA aneurysm
- B. Cavernous sinus thrombosis
- C. Moyamoya disease
- D. Vein of Galen malformation

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Moyamoya disease** — **C. Moyamoya disease** — Moyamoya disease shows bilateral progressive stenosis/occlusion of the terminal internal carotid arteries with extensive collateral network giving a 'puff of smoke' (moyamoya in Japanese) appearance on cerebral angiography.

</details>

### Q112 — K nail can be used for

K nail can be used for all of the following fractures excePt -

- A. Isthmic femur shaft fractures
- B. Intertrochanteric fractures
- C. Low subtrochanteric fractures
- D. Distal femur shaft fractures

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Intertrochanteric fractures** — **B. Intertrochanteric fractures** — Küntscher (K) nail is an intramedullary nail for diaphyseal femur fractures (isthmic, subtrochanteric, distal shaft); intertrochanteric fractures require a sliding hip screw or cephalomedullary nail (e.g., PFN), not a standard K nail.

</details>

---
### Q113 — Bone transport can be used in

Bone transport can be used in the management of -

- A. Gap non union
- B. Deformity Correction
- C. Communited shaft femur fracture
- D. Avscular Necrosis of Femoral Head

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Gap non union** — **A. Gap non union** — Bone transport (Ilizarov technique) uses distraction osteogenesis to gradually move a bone segment across a defect, filling gaps from non-union or after debridement of infected/comminuted bone, generating new bone in the regenerate zone.

</details>

---
### Q114 — Aeroplane splint is used in

Aeroplane splint is used in ?

- A. Radial nerve injury
- B. Ulnar nerve injury
- C. Brachial plexus injury
- D. Scoliosis

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Brachial plexus injury** — **C. Brachial plexus injury** — Aeroplane (shoulder abduction) splint maintains the arm at 90° abduction and slight forward flexion to prevent shoulder contracture and protect repaired nerves during healing in brachial plexus injuries, especially upper trunk (Erb's palsy).

</details>

---
### Q115 — Salter Harris classification is used for

Salter Harris classification is used for -

- A. Supracondylar humerus fractures in children
- B. Estimation of growth of the physes
- C. Physeal injuries
- D. Severity of degloving injuries to the limb

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Physeal injuries** — **C. Physeal injuries** — Salter-Harris classification (Types I–V) categorises fractures involving the growth plate (physis) in children; higher grades indicate greater physeal involvement and higher risk of growth disturbance and premature physeal closure.

</details>

---
### Q116 — Which of the following castssplints is

Which of the following casts/splints is used for fracture shaft humerus ?

- A. Hanging casts
- B. Knuckle bender splint
- C. Aeroplane Splint
- D. Above elbow cast

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Hanging casts** — **A. Hanging casts** — A hanging arm cast uses gravity traction (the weight of the cast) to reduce and maintain alignment of humeral shaft fractures in ambulant patients; it is applied below the elbow with the patient upright or semi-recumbent.

</details>

---
### Q117 — Lift off test is done to

Lift off test is done to assess the function of:

- A. Supraspinatus
- B. Infraspinatus
- C. Teres Minor
- D. Subscapularis

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Subscapularis** — **D. Subscapularis** — The lift-off test (Gerber's test): patient places dorsum of hand on lower back then lifts it off against resistance; inability to do so indicates subscapularis tear, as subscapularis is the primary internal rotator of the shoulder.

</details>

---
### Q118 — All are true about menisci of

All are true about menisci of knee joint except

- A. Lateral meniscus covers more articular surface of tibia-
- B. Lateral meniscus is more mobile
- C. Lateral meniscus is more prone to injury
- D. Lateral meniscus is semicircular

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Lateral meniscus is more prone to injury** — **C. Lateral meniscus is more prone to injury** — Medial meniscus is more prone to injury because it is firmly attached to the medial collateral ligament and joint capsule, limiting its mobility; lateral meniscus is more mobile and thus less frequently torn.

</details>

---
### Q119 — Three point bony relationship of the

Three point bony relationship of the elbow is disturbed in -

- A. Supracondylar Fracture of the humerus
- B. Fracture lateral condyle of the humerus
- C. Monteggia Fracture dislocation
- D. Fracture of Proximal Radius

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Fracture lateral condyle of the humerus** — **B. Fracture lateral condyle of the humerus** — The triangle of Hueter (medial epicondyle, lateral epicondyle, olecranon) is disturbed only when the epicondyles are displaced; lateral condyle fracture displaces one point of this triangle, unlike supracondylar fractures where the triangle is preserved.

</details>

---
### Q120 — Cubitus Valgus Deformity is commonly seen

Cubitus Valgus Deformity is commonly seen in which of the following conditions -

- A. Malunited Lateral Condylar fracture of Humerus
- B. Malunited Supracondylar Fracture of Humerus
- C. Posterior dislocation ofelbow
- D. Fracture medial condyle of humerus

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Malunited Lateral Condylar fracture of Humerus** — **A. Malunited Lateral Condylar fracture of Humerus** — Non-union or malunion of lateral condyle fractures in children leads to progressive cubitus valgus deformity, causing late tardy ulnar nerve palsy due to chronic traction on the nerve at the medial elbow.

</details>

---
### Q121 — AVN following transcervical neck femur fractures

AVN following transcervical neck femur fractures occurs due to damage to which of the following blood vessels ?

- A. Lateral retinacular branch of lateral circumflex femoral artery
- B. Lateral retinacular branch of medial circumflex femoral artery
- C. Medial retinacular branch of lateral circumflex femoral artery
- D. Obdurator artery

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Lateral retinacular branch of lateral circumflex femoral artery** — **A. Lateral retinacular branch of lateral circumflex femoral artery** — Wait — the dominant blood supply to the femoral head travels via retinacular vessels from the medial circumflex femoral artery (lateral retinacular branches); this is the vessel primarily damaged in transcervical fractures causing AVN.

</details>

---
### Q122 — Lockingoftheknee involves -

Lockingoftheknee involves -

- A. External rotation of femur with the foot offthe ground
- B. Internal rotation of the tibia with the foot on the ground
- C. Contraction of popliteus
- D. Internal rotation of femur with foot on the ground

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Internal rotation of femur with foot on the ground** — **D. Internal rotation of femur with foot on the ground** — Locking (full extension) of the knee occurs as the femur internally rotates on the fixed tibia in the closed kinetic chain (foot on ground), tightening cruciate and collateral ligaments to create a rigid locked joint.

</details>

---
### Q123 — Foot drop occurs due to the

Foot drop occurs due to the involvement of: September 2006, March 2013 (b, f g, h)

- A. Sciatic nerve
- B. Direct injury to the dorsiflexors
- C. Common peroneal nerve palsy
- D. All of the above

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. All of the above** — **D. All of the above** — Foot drop results from inability to dorsiflex the ankle; causes include common peroneal nerve palsy (most common), sciatic nerve injury, direct injury to dorsiflexors (tibialis anterior), or L4–L5 nerve root compression.

</details>

---
### Q124 — Fracture neck of femur in 80

Fracture neck of femur in 80 year old male sustained 1 weekback The treatment of choice is -

- A. Hemiarthroplasty
- B. ExcisionarthroPlastY
- C. Closed reduction and fixation with three cancellous screws
- D. Longitudinal skin traction for 6 weeks

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Hemiarthroplasty** — **A. Hemiarthroplasty** — In elderly patients (>70 years) with displaced intracapsular neck of femur fracture, hemiarthroplasty (replacing femoral head only) is preferred over internal fixation due to high AVN risk and poor bone quality precluding reliable screw fixation.

</details>

---
### Q125 — Which of the following fractures of

Which of the following fractures of the neck of femur are associated with maximal compromise in blood supply ?

- A. Intertrochantericfractures
- B. Basicervical fracture
- C. Trans cervical fracture
- D. Sub Capital fractures

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Sub Capital fractures** — **D. Sub Capital fractures** — Subcapital fractures occur immediately below the femoral head, disrupting the retinacular vessels (medial circumflex femoral artery branches) at their entry point, causing maximal compromise of blood supply and highest AVN risk.

</details>

---
### Q126 — AVN is seen in which type

AVN is seen in which type of # of femur:

- A. Intertrochantric #
- B. Subcapital #
- C. Trans cervical #
- D. B AND C

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. B AND C** — **D. B AND C** — AVN of the femoral head follows both subcapital and transcervical (intracapsular) fractures, as both disrupt the retinacular blood supply from medial circumflex femoral artery; intertrochanteric fractures are extracapsular and AVN risk is much lower.

</details>

---
### Q127 — Tinels sign is seen in -

Tinels sign is seen in -

- A. Avascular necrosisof scaPhoid
- B. Kienbock s Disease
- C. lst carpometacarpal joint arthritis
- D. Carpal tunnel syndrome

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Carpal tunnel syndrome** — **D. Carpal tunnel syndrome** — Tinel's sign is elicited by tapping over the carpal tunnel at the wrist, producing tingling/paraesthesia in the median nerve distribution (lateral 3.5 fingers); positive in median nerve compression (carpal tunnel syndrome).

</details>

### Q128 — Which of the following is used

Which of the following is used as a substitute for wrist extensors in radial nerve PalsY?

- A. Pronator Teres
- B. Palmaris Longus
- C. Flexor Digitorum SuPerficialis
- D. Flexor Digitorum Profundus

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Pronator Teres** — **A. Pronator Teres** — In radial nerve palsy causing wrist drop, pronator teres (median nerve, C6) is transferred to extensor carpi radialis brevis as a tendon transfer to restore wrist extension, while preserving elbow and forearm function.

</details>

### Q129 — year old carpenter with a blunt

A patient (age not captured in source) who is a year old carpenter with a blunt trauma to his arm sustained a fracture followingwhich he developedwrist drop, loss of extension at fingers and loss of

- A. Patient has an injury to the median nerve
- B. He should have also lost extention of the forearm
- C. Patient has injured the radial nerve in the spird groove
- D. There is combined involvement of the radial nerve and median

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Patient has injured the radial nerve in the spird groove** — **C. Patient has injured the radial nerve in the spiral groove** — Wrist drop with loss of finger extension but preserved elbow extension (triceps spared) indicates radial nerve injury at the spiral groove of the humerus, below the branch to triceps.

</details>

---
### Q130 — Wrist drop is due to injury

Wrist drop is due to injury to ?

- A. Radial nerve
- B. Ulnar nerve
- C. Median nerve
- D. Posterior interosseous nerve

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Radial nerve** — **A. Radial nerve** — Wrist drop (inability to extend wrist) results from radial nerve injury (C6–C8), most commonly at the spiral groove of the humerus (e.g., midshaft humeral fracture or 'Saturday night palsy'); all wrist and finger extensors are paralysed.

</details>

### Q131 — Blounts disease is associated with all

Blount's disease is associated with all of the following, EXCEPT:

- A. Genu varum
- B. Genu Recurvatum
- C. Internal Tibial Torsion
- D. External Tibial Torsion

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. External Tibial Torsion** — **D. External Tibial Torsion** — Blount's disease (tibia vara) is characterised by genu varum (bow legs), internal tibial torsion, and medial tibial metaphyseal beaking due to disturbed growth at the posteromedial proximal tibial physis; external torsion does NOT occur.

</details>

---
### Q132 — All of the following are true

All of the following are true regarding Pagets Disease except -

- A. Pelvis is the most common site
- B. Cranial nerve involvement may be seen
- C. High output cardiac failure is one of the complications
- D. It may progress to a secondary chondrosarcoma

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. It may progress to a secondary chondrosarcoma** — **D. It may progress to a secondary chondrosarcoma** — Paget's disease of bone undergoes sarcomatous transformation to osteosarcoma (most common, ~1%), fibrosarcoma, or rarely malignant fibrous histiocytoma; chondrosarcoma transformation does NOT occur in Paget's disease.

</details>

---
### Q133 — Commonest site of occurrence of chondrosarcoma

Commonest site of occurrence of chondrosarcoma is -

- A. Pelvis
- B. Ribs
- C. Femur
- D. Proximal tibia

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Pelvis** — **A. Pelvis** — Chondrosarcoma most commonly arises in the pelvis (ilium), followed by proximal femur and ribs; slow-growing cartilaginous malignancy, radioresistant, treated by wide resection.

</details>

### Q134 — Nerve damaged due to lunate dislocation

Nerve damaged due to lunate dislocation (in carpal tunnel):

- A. Median & ulnar
- B. Median
- C. Ulnar
- D. Radial

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Median** — **B. Median** — Lunate dislocates anteriorly into the carpal tunnel compressing the median nerve; presents with thenar weakness and sensory loss over lateral 3.5 fingers.

</details>

### Q135 — Anatomy: Cubital fossa medial boundary

Medial boundary of cubital fossa:

- A. Brachioradialis
- B. Pronator teres
- C. Supinator
- D. None

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Pronator teres** — Cubital fossa: lateral boundary = brachioradialis; medial boundary = pronator teres; floor = brachialis + supinator.

</details>

### Q136 — Anatomy: Anterior interosseous nerve

Anterior interosseous nerve is a branch of:

- A. Radial nerve
- B. Median nerve
- C. Ulnar nerve
- D. Axillary nerve

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Median nerve** — Anterior interosseous nerve (AIN) is a branch of median nerve; supplies FPL, lateral half of FDP, pronator quadratus.

</details>

### Q137 — Anatomy: Olecranon process

Olecranon process of ulna helps in formation of:

- A. Radial notch
- B. Trochlear notch
- C. Olecranon fossa
- D. Coronoid fossa

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Trochlear notch** — Trochlear (semilunar) notch = olecranon + coronoid processes of ulna articulating with trochlea of humerus.

</details>

---
### Q138 — Anatomy: Clavicle

True about clavicle:

- A. Endochondral ossification
- B. Vertical orientation
- C. No medullary cavity
- D. Rarely fractures

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. No medullary cavity** — Clavicle ossifies by intramembranous ossification (first bone to ossify); has no medullary cavity.

</details>

### Q139 — Anatomy: Clavipectoral fascia

Clavipectoral fascia is pierced by all except:

- B. Medial pectoral nerve
- C. Thoracoacromial vessels
- D. Cephalic vein

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Medial pectoral nerve** — Clavipectoral fascia pierced by: cephalic vein, thoracoacromial vessels, lateral pectoral nerve. Medial pectoral nerve pierces pectoralis minor to reach pectoralis major.

---
### Q140 — Anatomy: Supinator jerk root value

Root value of supinator jerk:

- A. C3, C4
- B. C4, C5
- C. C5, C6
- D. C8, T1

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. C5, C6** — Supinator jerk (brachioradialis reflex) = C5, C6; biceps = C5, C6; triceps = C7.

</details>

### Q141 — Anatomy: Small hand muscles nerve supply

Small muscles of hand are supplied by:

- A. C3
- B. C4
- C. C6
- D. C5–C7, C8 to T1

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. C5–C7, C8 to T1** — Most intrinsic hand muscles: ulnar C8, T1; thenar muscles (lateral): median C8, T1.

### Q142 — Anatomy: Bicipital aponeurosis

Bicipital aponeurosis lies over which structure in cubital fossa:

- B. Radial nerve
- C. Brachial artery
- D. Anterior interosseous artery

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Brachial artery** — Bicipital aponeurosis (lacertus fibrosus) passes medially to cover the brachial artery in the cubital fossa.

</details>

---
### Q143 — Anatomy: Over bicipital aponeurosis

Structure over bicipital aponeurosis in cubital fossa:

- A. Ulnar nerve
- B. Radial nerve
- C. Brachial artery
- D. Veins

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Veins** — Median cubital vein crosses superficial to the bicipital aponeurosis (important for venepuncture safety).

</details>

### Q144 — Anatomy: Radial nerve in spiral groove

Nerve running along with profunda brachii artery in spiral groove:

- A. Ulnar
- B. Median
- C. Radial
- D. None

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Radial** — Radial nerve and profunda brachii artery run together in the spiral groove (radial groove) of humerus.

</details>

### Q145 — Anatomy: Quadrilateral space

Boundaries of quadrilateral space include all except:

- A. Teres major
- B. Long head of triceps
- C. Neck of humerus
- D. Deltoid

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Deltoid** — Quadrilateral space boundaries: teres minor (superior), teres major (inferior), long head of triceps (medial), surgical neck of humerus (lateral). Axillary nerve and posterior circumflex humeral artery pass through.

</details>

### Q146 — Anatomy: Axillary artery

Axillary artery is divided into three parts by:

- A. 1st rib
- B. Clavicle
- C. Pectoralis minor muscle
- D. Teres minor muscle

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Pectoralis minor muscle** — Pectoralis minor divides axillary artery into 3 parts (1 branch, 2 branches, 3 branches respectively).

### Q147 — Anatomy: Posterior wall of axilla

Posterior wall of axilla is formed by:

- A. Pectoralis major
- B. Pectoralis minor
- C. Subscapularis
- D. Intercostal muscles

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Subscapularis** — Posterior wall of axilla: subscapularis, teres major, latissimus dorsi. Anterior wall: pectoralis major and minor.

### Q148 — Anatomy: Anterior axillary fold

Anterior axillary fold is due to which muscle:

- A. Pectoralis major
- B. Pectoralis minor
- C. Subscapularis
- D. Teres major

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Pectoralis major** — Anterior axillary fold = pectoralis major; posterior axillary fold = latissimus dorsi + teres major.

</details>

### Q149 — Anatomy: Intracapsular extrasynovial structure

Intracapsular but extrasynovial:

- A. Long head of triceps
- B. Long head of biceps
- C. Short head of biceps
- D. Medial head of biceps

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Long head of biceps** — Long head of biceps tendon is intracapsular (within shoulder joint capsule) but extrasynovial.

### Q150 — Anatomy: Lactiferous ducts

How many lactiferous ducts open in nipple:

- A. 0–10
- B. 15–20

**B. 15–20** — Each lactiferous duct opens separately at nipple; corresponds to 15–20 lobes.

### Q151 — Anatomy: Deltopectoral groove

Structure related to deltopectoral groove:

### Q152 — Anatomy: Common interosseous artery

Common interosseous artery is a branch of:

- A. Brachial artery

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

### Q153 — Anatomy: Scaphoid blood supply

True about blood supply of scaphoid:

- D. Proximal supply in anterograde fashion

**C. Major supply from dorsal surface** — Scaphoid blood supply mainly enters dorsally; proximal pole is supplied retrogradely, predisposing to AVN after fracture.

### Q154 — Anatomy: Foot lumbricals nerve supply

3rd and 4th lumbricals of foot are supplied by:

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

### Q155 — Anatomy: Short saphenous vein

### Q156 — Anatomy: Inferior extensor retinaculum

- C. Inferior slip attached to deep fascia of sole

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

### Q157 — Anatomy: Popliteus

### Q158 — Anatomy: Semitendinosus

- B. Proximal fleshy, distal thin

### Q159 — Anatomy: Foot dorsiflexor

### Q160 — Anatomy: Oblique popliteal ligament artery

Artery piercing the oblique popliteal ligament of knee:

**C. Middle genicular** — Middle genicular (azygos articular) artery pierces the oblique popliteal ligament.

### Q161 — Anatomy: Lateral patellar dislocation prevention

Lateral dislocation of patella is prevented by:

### Q162 — Anatomy: Hunter's canal

Hunter's canal is seen in:

### Q163 — Anatomy: Iliotibial tract

---
### Q164 — Anatomy: Talus support ligament

Ligament supporting the talus:

- A. Spring ligament
- B. Deltoid ligament
- C. LCL
- D. Cervical ligament

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Spring ligament** — Plantar calcaneonavicular (spring) ligament supports head of talus and the medial longitudinal arch.

</details>

---

---
### Q165 — Anatomy: Tibia nutrient artery

False about tibia-fibula:

- A. Nutrient artery of tibia is from posterior tibial artery
- B. Nutrient artery of fibula is from peroneal artery
- C. Proximal end of tibia is related to common peroneal nerve
- D. Tibia is most common site of osteomyelitis

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Proximal end of tibia is related to common peroneal nerve** — Common peroneal nerve winds around neck of fibula, not tibia. So the false statement is C.

</details>

---

---
### Q166 — Anatomy: Lumbar plexus branches

All are branches of lumbar plexus except:

- A. Iliohypogastric nerve
- B. Ilioinguinal nerve
- C. Obturator nerve
- D. Subcostal nerve

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Subcostal nerve** — Subcostal nerve (T12) is NOT part of lumbar plexus; it's the ventral ramus of T12.

</details>

---

---
### Q167 — Anatomy: Sacral articular surface in males

Articular surface of sacrum extends up to how many vertebrae in males:

- A. 1 to 1½
- B. 2 to 2½
- C. 3 to 3½
- D. 4 to 4½

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. 3 to 3½** — In males, sacroiliac articular surface extends over first 3–3.5 sacral segments; in females, 2–2.5 segments.

</details>

---

---
### Q168 — Anatomy: Sacroiliac joint lower limit in females

Lower limit of sacroiliac joint in females:

- A. 1 to 1½
- B. 2 to 2½
- C. 3 to 3½
- D. 4 to 4½

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. 2 to 2½** — Sacroiliac joint extends over S1–S2 (or 2–2.5 vertebrae) in females.

</details>

---

---
### Q169 — Anatomy: First rib relations

First rib is not related to:

- A. Sympathetic chain
- B. Scalenus anterior
- C. Suprapleureal membrane
- D. T2 nerve

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. T2 nerve** — First rib is related to T1 nerve (not T2). The T2 nerve passes over the 2nd rib.

</details>

---

---
### Q170 — Anatomy: Anterior intercostal artery

True about anterior intercostal artery:

- A. Present in 1st to 11th intercostal space
- B. Each intercostal space has two anterior intercostal arteries
- C. Branch of internal thoracic artery
- D. Branch of aorta

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Branch of internal thoracic artery** — Anterior intercostal arteries (1st–6th) are from internal thoracic artery; 7th–9th from musculophrenic artery.

</details>

---

---
### Q171 — Anatomy: Right principal bronchus

True about right principal bronchus:

- A. Narrower
- B. Horizontal
- C. Shorter
- D. All are true

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Shorter** — Right principal bronchus: wider, shorter, more vertical (hence more likely to aspirate foreign bodies into right lung).

</details>

---

---
### Q172 — Anatomy: Thoracic duct formation

Thoracic duct is formed by:

- A. Union of left subclavian and left internal jugular vein
- B. Union of brachiocephalic vein and internal jugular vein
- C. Continuation of upper end of cisterna chyli
- D. None of the above

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Continuation of upper end of cisterna chyli** — Thoracic duct is the upward continuation of cisterna chyli, starting at T12.

</details>

---

---
### Q173 — Anatomy: Thoracic duct termination

Thoracic duct opens into:

- A. Subclavian vein
- B. Internal jugular vein
- C. Right brachiocephalic vein
- D. Left brachiocephalic vein

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Left brachiocephalic vein** — Thoracic duct opens into the junction of left internal jugular and left subclavian veins (= left brachiocephalic vein).

</details>

---

---
### Q174 — Anatomy: Hilum of lung posterior relation

Posterior relation of hilum of lung:

- A. Azygous vein
- B. SVC
- C. Vagus nerve
- D. Arch of aorta
