<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Vagus nerve** — Vagus passes posterior to lung hilum; phrenic passes anterior.

</details>

---

---
### Q175 — Anatomy: Right lung hilum not related to

Not related to hilum of right lung:

- A. Azygous vein
- B. Vagus nerve
- C. SVC
- D. Arch of aorta

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Arch of aorta** — Arch of aorta is on the left side. Right lung hilum is related to SVC, azygous vein, right vagus.

</details>

---

---
### Q176 — Anatomy: Xiphoid fusion age

Xiphoid fuses with sternum by what age:

- A. 30 years
- B. 35 years
- C. 40 years
- D. 45 years

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. 40 years** — Xiphoid process fuses with the sternal body around age 40.

</details>

---

---
### Q177 — Anatomy: Bronchopulmonary segments

Bronchopulmonary segments in right and left lungs respectively:

- A. 9, 11
- B. 11, 9
- C. 10, 10
- D. 8, 10

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. 10, 10** — Both right and left lungs have 10 bronchopulmonary segments each.

</details>

---

---
### Q178 — Anatomy: Arch of aorta nerve relations

At the level of arch of aorta, relationship of left vagus and left phrenic nerve:

- A. Phrenic nerve anterior, vagus nerve posterior
- B. Phrenic nerve posterior, vagus nerve anterior
- C. Both in same plane anteroposteriorly
- D. Variable relationship

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Phrenic nerve anterior, vagus nerve posterior** — At arch of aorta level: phrenic nerve is anterior (crosses laterally), vagus is posterior (to form left RLN).

</details>

---

---
### Q179 — Anatomy: Transverse pericardial sinus

Posterior to transverse pericardial sinus:

- A. Aorta
- B. Pulmonary trunk
- C. SVC
- D. Left atrium

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. SVC** — Transverse pericardial sinus: anterior = aorta and pulmonary trunk; posterior = SVC and pulmonary veins.

</details>

---

---
### Q180 — Anatomy: Cremasteric reflex

True about cremasteric reflex:

- A. Afferent: genital branch of genitofemoral nerve
- B. Efferent: genital branch of genitofemoral nerve
- C. Efferent: femoral branch of genitofemoral nerve
- D. Afferent: pudendal nerve

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Efferent: genital branch of genitofemoral nerve** — Cremasteric reflex: afferent = femoral branch (L1, L2); efferent = genital branch of genitofemoral nerve (L1, L2).

</details>

---

---
### Q181 — Anatomy: Urethral crest

Urethral crest is an elevation seen in urethra due to:

- A. Prostatic glands
- B. Insertion of detrusor muscle
- C. Insertion of trigone
- D. Pre-prostatic internal sphincter

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Prostatic glands** — Urethral crest (verumontanum) is a longitudinal ridge in the prostatic urethra where prostatic utricle and ejaculatory ducts open.

</details>

---

---
### Q182 — Anatomy: Pyramidalis nerve supply

Pyramidalis is supplied by:

- A. Subcostal nerve
- B. Ilioinguinal nerve
- C. Iliohypogastric nerve
- D. Genitofemoral nerve

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Subcostal nerve** — Pyramidalis muscle is supplied by the subcostal nerve (T12).

</details>

---

---
### Q183 — Anatomy: Appendices epiploicae

Appendices epiploicae is a feature of:

- A. Duodenum
- B. Stomach
- C. Colon
- D. Jejunum

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Colon** — Appendices epiploicae are fat-filled peritoneal pouches on the surface of the large intestine.

</details>

---

---
### Q184 — Anatomy: Appendices epiploicae distribution

Appendices epiploicae are seen in all parts of large intestine except:

- A. Sigmoid colon
- B. Ascending colon
- C. Caecum
- D. Transverse colon

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Caecum** — Caecum has no appendices epiploicae; the appendix and rectum also lack them.

</details>

---

---
### Q185 — Anatomy: Inferior rectal artery

Inferior rectal artery is a branch of:

- A. Inferior mesenteric artery
- B. Superior mesenteric artery
- C. Coeliac trunk
- D. Internal pudendal artery

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Internal pudendal artery** — Inferior rectal artery = branch of internal pudendal artery (supplies external anal sphincter).

</details>

---

---
### Q186 — Anatomy: Cremasteric artery

Cremasteric artery is a branch of:

- A. Internal pudendal artery
- B. External pudendal artery
- C. Inferior epigastric artery
- D. Superior epigastric artery

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Inferior epigastric artery** — Cremasteric artery is a branch of the inferior epigastric artery.

</details>

---

---
### Q187 — Anatomy: Superficial epigastric artery

Superficial epigastric artery is a branch of:

- A. Internal pudendal artery
- B. External pudendal artery
- C. Internal iliac artery
- D. Femoral artery

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Femoral artery** — Superficial epigastric artery is a branch of the femoral artery (just below inguinal ligament).

</details>

---

---
### Q188 — Anatomy: Inferior epigastric vein

Inferior epigastric vein drains into:

- A. Femoral vein
- B. External iliac vein
- C. Internal iliac vein
- D. Internal pudendal vein

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. External iliac vein** — Inferior epigastric vein drains into the external iliac vein.

</details>

---

---
### Q189 — Anatomy: Superior rectal vein

Superior rectal vein drains into:

- A. Inferior mesenteric vein
- B. External iliac vein
- C. Internal iliac vein
- D. Internal pudendal vein

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Inferior mesenteric vein** — Superior rectal vein → inferior mesenteric vein → portal system. Important portosystemic anastomosis site.

</details>

---

---
### Q190 — Anatomy: Accessory spleen location

Most common location of accessory spleen:

- A. Hilum of spleen
- B. Greater omentum
- C. Lesser omentum
- D. None

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Hilum of spleen** — Accessory spleen most commonly found at splenic hilum (~80% of cases).

</details>

---

---
### Q191 — Anatomy: Posterior vaginal wall

Length of posterior vaginal wall:

- A. Variable
- B. Same as anterior vaginal wall
- C. Less than anterior vaginal wall
- D. More than anterior vaginal wall

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. More than anterior vaginal wall** — Posterior vaginal wall (~9 cm) is longer than anterior vaginal wall (~7 cm) due to posterior fornix depth.

</details>

---

---
### Q192 — Anatomy: Bare area of liver

Bare area of liver is related to:

- A. Aorta
- B. Hepatic vein
- C. Portal vein
- D. Gallbladder

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Hepatic vein** — Bare area of liver (not covered by peritoneum) is related to the IVC and right hepatic vein.

</details>

---

---
### Q193 — Anatomy: Circumcaval ureter

True about circumcaval ureter:

- A. Developmental anomaly of ureter
- B. Ureter passes in front of IVC from lateral to medial
- C. Mostly involves right ureter
- D. Type 2 is more common

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Mostly involves right ureter** — Retrocaval (circumcaval) ureter = right ureter passes behind IVC (due to persistence of right posterior cardinal vein). Mostly right ureter.

</details>

---

---
### Q194 — Anatomy: Retroperitoneal structure

Which of the following is a retroperitoneal structure:

- A. Ileum
- B. Jejunum
- C. Ureter
- D. Appendix

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Ureter** — Ureter is retroperitoneal. SAD PUCKER mnemonic: Suprarenal glands, Aorta/IVC, Duodenum (2nd–4th), Pancreas (except tail), Ureters, Colon (ascending/descending), Kidneys, Esophagus, Rectum.

</details>

---

---
### Q195 — Anatomy: Falciform ligament content

Falciform ligament contains:

- A. Ligamentum venosus
- B. Ligamentum teres
- C. Linorenal ligament
- D. None of the above

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Ligamentum teres** — Falciform ligament contains ligamentum teres hepatis (obliterated left umbilical vein).

</details>

---

---
### Q196 — Anatomy: Colon without mesentery

Part of colon with no mesentery:

- A. Transverse colon
- B. Sigmoid colon
- C. Ascending colon
- D. Rectum

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Ascending colon** — Ascending and descending colon are retroperitoneal (no mesentery). Transverse and sigmoid have mesentery.

</details>

---

---
### Q197 — Anatomy: Trigone of bladder

False regarding trigone of bladder:

- A. Lined by transitional epithelium
- B. Mucosa smooth and firmly adherent
- C. Internal urethral orifice lies at lateral angle of base
- D. Developed from mesonephric duct

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Internal urethral orifice lies at lateral angle of base** — Internal urethral orifice is at the APEX (lower angle) of the trigone, not lateral angles. Ureteric orifices are at lateral angles.

</details>

---

---
### Q198 — Anatomy: Trigone development

Trigone of urinary bladder develops from:

- A. Mesoderm
- B. Ectoderm
- C. Endoderm of urachus
- D. None of the above

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Mesoderm** — Trigone develops from the absorbed mesonephric (Wolffian) ducts = mesodermal origin.

</details>

---

---
### Q199 — Anatomy: Large intestine watershed zone

Watershed zone of large intestine:

- A. Cecum
- B. Ascending colon
- C. Rectosigmoid
- D. Transverse colon

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Rectosigmoid** — Rectosigmoid junction (Sudeck's critical point) is the watershed zone between IMA territory (sigmoid) and superior rectal artery territory; prone to ischemia.

</details>

---

---
### Q200 — Anatomy: Transverse cervical ligament

Ligament extending from cervix and vagina to lateral pelvic wall:

- A. Broad ligament
- B. Pubocervical ligament
- C. Round ligament
- D. Transverse cervical ligament

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Transverse cervical ligament** — Transverse cervical (cardinal/Mackenrodt's) ligament supports cervix and upper vagina, attaches to lateral pelvic wall.

</details>

---

---
### Q201 — Anatomy: Scarpa's fascia

True about Scarpa's fascia:

- A. Deep fascia of anterior abdominal wall
- B. Also called Buck's fascia
- C. Attached to iliotibial tract
- D. Forms suspensory ligament of penis

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Forms suspensory ligament of penis** — Scarpa's fascia (membranous layer of superficial fascia) forms the suspensory ligament of penis and dartos fascia.

</details>

---

---
### Q202 — Anatomy: Cave of Retzius

Where is the Cave of Retzius present:

- A. Between urinary bladder and rectum
- B. Between urinary bladder and cervix
- C. In front of the bladder
- D. Between cervix and rectum

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. In front of the bladder** — Retropubic space (space of Retzius/Cave of Retzius) is between pubic symphysis and urinary bladder.

</details>

---

---
### Q203 — Anatomy: Deep inguinal ring nerve

Nerve entering the inguinal canal through deep inguinal ring:

- A. Ilioinguinal nerve
- B. Pudendal nerve
- C. Genital branch of genitofemoral nerve
- D. Superior rectal nerve

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Genital branch of genitofemoral nerve** — Genital branch of genitofemoral nerve enters through deep inguinal ring with spermatic cord.

</details>

---

---
### Q204 — Anatomy: Renal artery development

Initially, renal arteries are branches of:

- A. Internal pudendal artery
- B. External iliac artery
- C. Common iliac artery
- D. Aorta

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Common iliac artery** — During early development, kidney is in pelvis; initially supplied by branches of common iliac artery.

</details>

---

---
### Q205 — Anatomy: Neonatal kidney supply

In a neonate, kidney is supplied by:

- A. Internal pudendal artery
- B. External iliac artery
- C. Common iliac artery
- D. Aorta

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Aorta** — After ascent to adult position, kidney is supplied by renal arteries directly from abdominal aorta (at L1–L2).

</details>

---

---
### Q206 — Anatomy: Meckel's cave

Meckel's cave is related to:

- A. Submandibular ganglion
- B. Trigeminal ganglion
- C. Otic ganglion
- D. Pterygopalatine ganglion

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Trigeminal ganglion** — Meckel's cave (cavum trigeminale) is a dural recess near apex of petrous bone containing the trigeminal (Gasserian) ganglion.

</details>

---

---
### Q207 — Anatomy: Longest spinous process

Longest spinous process is seen in:

- A. C2
- B. C4
- C. C5
- D. C7

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. C7** — C7 (vertebra prominens) has the longest spinous process in the cervical spine; palpable at base of neck.

</details>

---

---
### Q208 — Anatomy: Sweat gland near lid margin

Sweat gland near the lid margins:

- A. Moll
- B. Zeis
- C. Meibomian
- D. Krause

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Moll** — Glands of Moll = modified sweat (apocrine) glands at eyelid margin. Zeis = modified sebaceous glands. Meibomian = tarsal glands (sebaceous).

</details>

---

---
### Q209 — Anatomy: Anterior nasal lymph drainage

Anterior lymphatics from the nose drain into:

- A. Pretracheal nodes
- B. Submandibular nodes
- C. Sublingual nodes
- D. Superficial cervical nodes

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Submandibular nodes** — Anterior nasal cavity drains to submandibular nodes; posterior nasal cavity to retropharyngeal and deep cervical nodes.

</details>

---

---
### Q210 — Anatomy: Killian's dehiscence

Killian's dehiscence is seen in:

- A. Oropharynx
- B. Nasopharynx
- C. Cricopharynx
- D. Vocal cords

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Cricopharynx** — Killian's dehiscence = triangle of weakness between thyropharyngeus and cricopharyngeus; site of pharyngeal (Zenker's) diverticulum formation.

</details>

---

---
### Q211 — Anatomy: Fossa incudis

Fossa incudis is related to:

- A. Head of malleus
- B. Long process of incus
- C. Short process of incus
- D. Foot process of stapes

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Short process of incus** — Fossa incudis is a recess in the posterior epitympanum that lodges the short process of the incus.

</details>

---

---
### Q212 — Anatomy: Unpaired laryngeal cartilage

Unpaired laryngeal cartilage:

- A. Arytenoid
- B. Corniculate
- C. Cuneiform
- D. Epiglottis

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Epiglottis** — Unpaired laryngeal cartilages: thyroid, cricoid, epiglottis. Paired: arytenoid, corniculate, cuneiform.

</details>

---

---
### Q213 — Anatomy: Dorsal cranial nerve exit

Only nerve which exits brainstem on dorsal side:

- A. Facial
- B. Trigeminal
- C. Trochlear
- D. Abducent

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Trochlear** — CN IV (trochlear) is the only cranial nerve to exit dorsally from the brainstem (posterior midbrain); also the smallest.

</details>

---

---
### Q214 — Anatomy: Putamen blood supply

Blood supply of putamen includes all except:

- A. Medial striate arteries
- B. Lateral striate arteries
- C. Anterior choroidal artery
- D. Posterior communicating artery

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Posterior communicating artery** — Putamen is supplied by medial and lateral striate arteries (lenticulostriate from MCA) and anterior choroidal artery.

</details>

---

---
### Q215 — Anatomy: Medulla blood supply

Medulla is supplied by all except:

- A. Basilar artery
- B. Anterior spinal artery
- C. Vertebral artery
- D. Posterior cerebral artery

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Posterior cerebral artery** — Medulla blood supply: vertebral artery, anterior spinal, posterior inferior cerebellar (PICA), and basilar (lower portion). PCA supplies midbrain and occipital lobe.

</details>

---

---
### Q216 — Anatomy: Eustachian tube length

Length of Eustachian tube:

- A. 12 mm
- B. 24 mm
- C. 36 mm
- D. 48 mm

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. 36 mm** — Eustachian (auditory) tube is approximately 35–36 mm long in adults.

</details>

---

---
### Q217 — Anatomy: Lacrimal gland parasympathetic supply

Parasympathetic supply to lacrimal glands passed through:

- A. Lesser petrosal nerve
- B. Chorda tympani
- C. Greater petrosal nerve
- D. Lingual nerve

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Greater petrosal nerve** — Greater petrosal nerve (from facial/geniculate ganglion) → nerve of pterygoid canal (Vidian) → pterygopalatine ganglion → zygomatic → lacrimal nerve → lacrimal gland.

</details>

---

---
### Q218 — Anatomy: Onodi cells

Onodi cells are seen in:

- A. Sphenoid sinus
- B. Maxillary sinus
- C. Anterior ethmoidal sinus
- D. Posterior ethmoidal sinus

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Posterior ethmoidal sinus** — Onodi cells are posterior ethmoid air cells that extend superolaterally around the optic nerve; risk in sinus surgery.

</details>

---

---
### Q219 — Anatomy: Haller cells

Haller cells are seen in:

- A. Roof of nose
- B. Orbital floor
- C. Lateral nasal wall
- D. Maxillary sinus

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Orbital floor** — Haller cells (infraorbital cells) = anterior ethmoid cells that pneumatize the orbital floor medial to infraorbital canal.

</details>

---

---
### Q220 — Anatomy: Nasolacrimal duct valve

Which valve is present at opening of nasolacrimal duct:

- A. Hasner's valve
- B. Heister valve
- C. Spiral valve
- D. None

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Hasner's valve** — Valve of Hasner (plica lacrimalis) is at the lower opening of the nasolacrimal duct in the inferior meatus.

</details>

---

---
### Q221 — Anatomy: Cribriform plate function

Cribriform plate forms:

- A. Roof of olfactory region
- B. Floor of olfactory region
- C. Nasal septum
- D. All of the above

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Roof of olfactory region** — Cribriform plate of ethmoid = roof of olfactory region (nasal cavity); olfactory nerve bundles pass through its foramina.

</details>

---

---
### Q222 — Anatomy: Galen's anastomosis

Galen's anastomosis is between:

- A. Recurrent laryngeal nerve and external laryngeal nerve
- B. Recurrent laryngeal nerve and internal laryngeal nerve
- C. Internal laryngeal nerve and external laryngeal nerve
- D. None of the above

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Recurrent laryngeal nerve and internal laryngeal nerve** — Galen's anastomosis connects the recurrent laryngeal nerve (motor) with the internal laryngeal nerve (sensory branch of SLN) in the posterior larynx.

</details>

---

---
### Q223 — Anatomy: Lamina papyracea

Lamina papyracea is between:

- A. Optic nerve and orbit
- B. Maxillary sinus and orbit
- C. Ethmoid sinus and orbit
- D. Cranial cavity and orbit

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Ethmoid sinus and orbit** — Lamina papyracea = paper-thin medial orbital wall = orbital plate of ethmoid bone; separates orbit from ethmoid air cells.

</details>

---

---
### Q224 — Anatomy: Sternocleidomastoid

False about sternocleidomastoid:

- A. Arises from sternum and clavicle
- B. Inserted on mastoid process
- C. Motor supply by spinal accessory nerve
- D. Tilts the head on opposite side

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Tilts the head on opposite side** — SCM tilts (laterally flexes) the head to the SAME side while rotating the face to the OPPOSITE side.

</details>

---

---
### Q225 — Anatomy: Scalenus anterior

The key to the root of the neck is scalenus anterior. True statement about scalenus anterior:

- A. Not pierced by phrenic nerve
- B. Attached to scalene tubercle on 2nd rib
- C. Separates subclavian artery from subclavian vein
- D. Pierced by phrenic nerve

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Not pierced by phrenic nerve** — Phrenic nerve descends on the ANTERIOR surface of scalenus anterior (not through it). The muscle separates subclavian artery (posterior) from subclavian vein (anterior). Attaches to 1st rib scalene tubercle.

</details>

---

---
### Q226 — Anatomy: Tongue and occipital myotome

Tongue muscle not developed from occipital myotome:

- A. Styloglossus
- B. Hyoglossus
- C. Genioglossus
- D. Palatoglossus

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Palatoglossus** — Palatoglossus is the exception — it is derived from the pharyngeal mesoderm (not occipital myotomes) and is innervated by vagus nerve, not hypoglossal.

</details>

---

---
### Q227 — Anatomy: Korner's septum

Korner's septum is seen in:

- A. Petrosquamous suture
- B. Temporosquamous suture
- C. Petromastoid suture
- D. Frontozygomatic suture

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Petrosquamous suture** — **A. Petrosquamous suture** — Körner's septum is a bony plate at the petrosquamous suture line within the mastoid; it can mislead surgeons into thinking mastoidectomy is complete while disease persists in the deeper petrous portion; a surgical pitfall.

</details>

---

---
### Q228 — Anatomy: Chorda tympani fibers

True about chorda tympani:

- A. Postganglionic sympathetic
- B. Preganglionic sympathetic
- C. Preganglionic parasympathetic
- D. Postganglionic parasympathetic

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Preganglionic parasympathetic** — Chorda tympani carries preganglionic parasympathetic fibers (synapsing in submandibular ganglion) and taste from anterior 2/3 tongue.

</details>

---

---
### Q229 — Anatomy: Vidian nerve formation

Vidian nerve is formed by union of:

- A. Superficial petrosal nerve and deep petrosal nerve
- B. Greater petrosal nerve and superficial petrosal nerve
- C. Greater petrosal nerve and deep petrosal nerve
- D. Greater petrosal nerve and external petrosal nerve

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Greater petrosal nerve and deep petrosal nerve** — Vidian nerve (nerve of pterygoid canal) = greater petrosal nerve (pre-ganglionic parasympathetic from facial) + deep petrosal nerve (sympathetic from ICA plexus).

</details>

---

---
### Q230 — Anatomy: Woodruff's area

Woodruff's area is located at:

- A. Anteroinferior part of nasal septum
- B. Posteroinferior part of nasal septum
- C. Superior part of nasal septum
- D. Posteroinferior part of lateral nasal wall

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Posteroinferior part of lateral nasal wall** — Woodruff's plexus is on the posteroinferior lateral nasal wall (nasopharyngeal end of inferior turbinate); common bleeding site in hypertensives.

</details>

---

---
### Q231 — Anatomy: Scutum in middle ear

Scutum is present in middle ear:

- A. Roof
- B. Lateral wall
- C. Medial wall
- D. Floor

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Lateral wall** — Scutum (spur) = sharp bony projection of the lateral wall of attic (epitympanum); erosion suggests cholesteatoma.

</details>

---

---
### Q232 — Anatomy: Bony labyrinth

Not a part of bony labyrinth:

- A. Cochlea
- B. Vestibule
- C. Utricle
- D. Semicircular canal

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Utricle** — Utricle and saccule are parts of the membranous labyrinth (inside bony vestibule). Bony labyrinth = cochlea, vestibule, semicircular canals.

</details>

---

---
### Q233 — Anatomy: Thyroid lymph drainage

Lymphatic drainage of thyroid gland is mainly:

- A. Sublingual nodes
- B. Submandibular nodes
- C. Deep cervical nodes
- D. Submental nodes

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Deep cervical nodes** — Thyroid lymph drains mainly to deep cervical nodes (and paratracheal nodes).

</details>

---

---
### Q234 — Anatomy: Organ of Corti location

Organ of Corti is situated in:

- A. Basilar membrane
- B. Utricle
- C. Saccule
- D. None of the above

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Basilar membrane** — Organ of Corti sits on the basilar membrane within the scala media (cochlear duct) of the cochlea.

</details>

---

---
### Q235 — Anatomy: 4th ventricle floor

Floor of 4th ventricle has:

- A. Infundibulum
- B. Vagal triangle
- C. Mammillary body
- D. Tuber cinereum

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Vagal triangle** — Floor of 4th ventricle (rhomboid fossa) contains: hypoglossal triangle, vagal triangle, facial colliculus, sulcus limitans, locus coeruleus.

</details>

---

---
### Q236 — Anatomy: Middle meningeal artery foramen

Middle meningeal artery passes through:

- A. Foramen ovale
- B. Foramen lacerum
- C. Foramen rotundum
- D. Foramen spinosum

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Foramen spinosum** — Middle meningeal artery enters the middle cranial fossa through foramen spinosum.

</details>

---

---
### Q237 — Anatomy: Nerve looping submandibular duct

Nerve which loops around submandibular duct:

- A. Mandibular nerve
- B. Lingual nerve
- C. Hypoglossal nerve
- D. Recurrent laryngeal nerve

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Lingual nerve** — Lingual nerve winds around the submandibular duct (Wharton's duct), crossing from lateral to medial to reach tongue tip.

</details>

---

---
### Q238 — Anatomy: Medulla oblongata derivation

Medulla oblongata is derived from:

- A. Telencephalon
- B. Diencephalon
- C. Mesencephalon
- D. Myelencephalon

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Myelencephalon** — Myelencephalon → medulla oblongata. Metencephalon → pons + cerebellum. Mesencephalon → midbrain.

</details>

---

---
### Q239 — Embryology: Morula cell count

Morula is how many celled:

- A. 4
- B. 8
- C. 12
- D. 16

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. 16** — Morula (= mulberry) is a solid ball of ~16 cells formed by day 3 after fertilization.

</details>

---

---
### Q240 — Embryology: Dental papilla derivatives

Dental papilla gives rise to:

- A. Enamel
- B. Dental cuticle
- C. Tooth pulp
- D. None

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Tooth pulp** — Dental papilla (mesenchyme) → dentine and pulp. Dental organ (enamel organ) → enamel.

</details>

---

---
### Q241 — Embryology: Optic cup derivatives

Optic cup gives rise to:

- A. Lens
- B. Retina
- C. Cornea
- D. Sclera

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Retina** — Inner layer of optic cup → neural retina; outer layer → retinal pigment epithelium. Lens = surface ectoderm.

</details>

---

---
### Q242 — Embryology: Kidney excretory system

Excretory system of kidney is derived from:

- A. Ureteric bud
- B. Mesonephros
- C. Metanephros
- D. None

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Metanephros** — Metanephric mesoderm (induced by ureteric bud) → nephrons (excretory tubules, glomeruli).

</details>

---

---
### Q243 — Embryology: Ureteric bud origin

Ureteric bud arises from:

- A. Paramesonephric duct
- B. Mullerian duct
- C. Mesonephric duct
- D. Mesonephric tubule

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Mesonephric duct** — Ureteric bud is an outgrowth from the caudal mesonephric (Wolffian) duct.

</details>

---

---
### Q244 — Embryology: Vitelline vein derivative

Derivative of vitelline vein:

- A. IVC
- B. SVC
- C. Ligamentum venosus
- D. Ligamentum teres

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. IVC** — Vitelline veins contribute to formation of IVC (hepatic segments), portal vein, and hepatic sinusoids.

</details>

---

---
### Q245 — Embryology: Ovary development

Not true about development of ovary:

- A. Develops in genital ridge
- B. Sex cords are derived from coelomic epithelium
- C. Oocytes are mesodermal in origin
- D. At birth ovary contains 2 million follicles

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Oocytes are mesodermal in origin** — Oocytes (primordial germ cells) originate from the wall of the yolk sac (endoderm), not mesoderm.

</details>

---

---
### Q246 — Embryology: Testis at deep inguinal ring

Testis lies at deep inguinal ring up to:

- A. 4 months
- B. 5 months
- C. 7 months
- D. 9 months

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. 7 months** — Testis reaches deep inguinal ring at 7 months; enters scrotum at 8–9 months. Descent involves gubernaculum.

</details>

---

---
### Q247 — Embryology: Testis position at 24–28 weeks

Position of testis at 24–28 weeks of intrauterine life:

- A. Inguinal canal
- B. Lumbar region
- C. Superficial inguinal ring
- D. Deep inguinal ring

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Deep inguinal ring** — At 24–28 weeks (7 months), testis is at the deep inguinal ring prior to entering the inguinal canal.

</details>

---

---
### Q248 — Embryology: Notochord

True about notochord — all except:

- A. Defines axis of embryo
- B. Serves as primary inductor
- C. Derived from hypoblast
- D. Remains as nucleus pulposus

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Derived from hypoblast** — Notochord is derived from the primitive node/epiblast (NOT hypoblast); it is a mesodermal structure.

</details>

---

---
### Q249 — Embryology: Notochord remnant

Remnant of notochord:

- A. Annulus fibrosus
- B. Nucleus pulposus
- C. Ligamentum flavum
- D. Intertransverse ligament

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Nucleus pulposus** — Nucleus pulposus (central gelatinous core of intervertebral disc) is the remnant of the notochord.

</details>

---

---
### Q250 — Embryology: Second part of duodenum

2nd part of duodenum is derived from:

- A. Foregut
- B. Midgut
- C. Both foregut and midgut
- D. Hindgut

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Both foregut and midgut** — 1st part and upper 2nd part = foregut (celiac trunk supply); lower 2nd part to 2/3 transverse colon = midgut (SMA supply). The ampulla of Vater marks the foregut/midgut junction.

</details>

---

---
### Q251 — Embryology: Corneal stroma development

Stroma of cornea develops from:

- A. Neural ectoderm
- B. Surface ectoderm
- C. Mesoderm
- D. Neural crest

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Mesoderm** — Corneal stroma derives from periocular mesoderm/neural crest (Note: different sources vary; most standard teaching = neural crest/mesenchyme).

</details>

---

---
### Q252 — Embryology: Female genital tract

Female genital tract develops from:

- A. Mesonephric duct
- B. Mesonephric tubules
- C. Mullerian duct
- D. None

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Mullerian duct** — Paramesonephric (Mullerian) duct → fallopian tubes, uterus, upper vagina in females.

</details>

---

---
### Q253 — Embryology: Glomus cells

Glomus cells are derived from:

- A. Surface ectoderm
- B. Neuroectoderm
- C. Mesoderm
- D. Endoderm

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Neuroectoderm** — Glomus (type I) cells of the carotid body are derived from neural crest (neuroectoderm).

</details>

---

---
### Q254 — Embryology: Vaginal epithelium

Epithelium of vagina arises from:

- A. Ectoderm
- B. Wolffian duct
- C. Mesoderm
- D. Mesonephric duct

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Mesoderm** — Vaginal epithelium is derived from urogenital sinus (endoderm) per most sources, but some texts say mesoderm. The answer given here is C (mesoderm).

</details>

---

---
### Q255 — Embryology: First arch derivatives

Which of the following is derived from 1st arch:

- A. Frontonasal process
- B. Maxillary process
- C. Mandibular process
- D. Both maxillary and mandibular processes

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Both maxillary and mandibular processes** — First branchial arch has two processes: maxillary process (superior) and mandibular process (inferior).

</details>

---

---
### Q256 — Embryology: First arch skeletal derivatives

Skeletal derivative of 1st arch:

- A. Stapes
- B. Hyoid
- C. Maxilla
- D. Laryngeal cartilages

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Maxilla** — 1st arch: maxilla, mandible, malleus, incus, sphenomandibular ligament. Stapes = 2nd arch. Hyoid = 2nd + 3rd.

</details>

---

---
### Q257 — Embryology: Styloid process derivation

Styloid process is derived from:

- A. 1st arch
- B. 2nd arch
- C. 3rd arch
- D. 4th arch

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. 2nd arch** — **B. 2nd arch** — The styloid process is derived from Reichert's cartilage (2nd pharyngeal/hyoid arch); the 2nd arch also gives rise to stapes, stylohyoid ligament, lesser horn and upper body of hyoid bone (mnemonic: Stapedius-Styloid-Superior hyoid).

</details>

---

---
### Q258 — Embryology: Pharyngeal muscles arch

Pharyngeal muscles are derived from which pharyngeal arch:

- A. 1st
- B. 2nd
- C. 3rd
- D. 5th (4th)

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. 3rd** — Stylopharyngeus = 3rd arch (CN IX). Most pharyngeal constrictors = 4th arch (vagus, CN X).

</details>

---

---
### Q259 — Anatomy: Sternochondral joint type

Sternochondral joint is:

- A. Primary cartilaginous
- B. Secondary cartilaginous
- C. Fibrous
- D. Synovial

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Primary cartilaginous** — 1st sternochondral joint = primary cartilaginous (synchondrosis). 2nd–7th = synovial joints.

</details>

---

---
### Q260 — Anatomy: Innervated joint structures

Innervated structures of joints are all except:

- A. Synovium
- B. Capsule
- C. Articular cartilage
- D. Ligaments

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Articular cartilage** — Articular cartilage is avascular and aneural; this is why cartilage damage is painless until other structures are involved.

</details>

---

---
### Q261 — Anatomy: Submucosal plexus

Submucosal plexus is:

- A. Myenteric plexus
- B. Auerbach's plexus
- C. Meissner's plexus
- D. Tympanic plexus

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Meissner's plexus** — Submucosal (Meissner's) plexus controls secretion; myenteric (Auerbach's) plexus controls motility.

</details>

---

---
### Q262 — Histology: Hassall's corpuscles

Hassall's corpuscles are found in:

- A. Lymph nodes
- B. Spleen
- C. Liver
- D. Thymus

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Thymus** — Hassall's corpuscles are whorls of epithelial cells in thymic medulla; markers of thymus on histology.

</details>

---

---
### Q263 — Histology: Duct of Bellini

Duct of Bellini is present in:

- A. Pancreas
- B. Liver
- C. Kidney
- D. Salivary gland

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Kidney** — Duct of Bellini = papillary collecting ducts that open at renal papilla into minor calyces.

</details>

---

---
### Q264 — Histology: Breast gland type

Breast is a:

- A. Endocrine gland
- B. Modified sweat gland
- C. Modified sebaceous gland
- D. Holocrine gland

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Modified sweat gland** — Mammary gland is a modified apocrine sweat gland.

</details>

---

---
### Q265 — Histology: Valve of Heister

Valve of Heister is seen in:

- A. Cystic duct
- B. Common bile duct
- C. Common hepatic duct
- D. Pancreatic duct

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Cystic duct** — Heister's valves (spiral valves of cystic duct) are mucosal folds in the cystic duct that regulate bile flow.

</details>

---

## Biochemistry

### Q266 — Function of myenteric plexus is to *(2015)*

Function of myenteric plexus is to regulate ?

- A. GI secretion
- B. Local blood flow
- C. Motility
- D. All of the above

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Motility**

</details>

---
### Q267 — important intracellular buffer *(2015)*

Most important intracellular buffer ?

- A. Bicarbonate
- B. Albumin
- C. Phosphate
- D. Ammonia

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Phosphate**

</details>

---
### Q268 — Example of allosteric inhibition *(2015)*

Example of allosteric inhibition

- A. Inactivation of glycogen synthase by phosphorylation
- B. Decreased synthesis of glucokinase by glucagon
- C. Inhibition of PFK-1 by citrate
- D. All of the above

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Inhibition of PFK-1 by citrate**

</details>

---
### Q269 — Chylomicrons core is formed by *(2015)*

Chylomicrons core is formed by ?

- A. Triglyceride
- B. Triglyceride and Cholesterol
- C. Triglyceride, Cholesterol and Phospholipids
- D. Free fatty acids

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Triglyceride and Cholesterol**

</details>

---
### Q270 — Pyridoxine is required in - *(2015)*

Pyridoxine is required in -

- A. Glycolysis
- B. TCA cycle
- C. Glycogenesis
- D. Glycogenolysis

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Glycogenolysis**

</details>

---
### Q271 — Bile salts help in absorption of *(2015)*

Bile salts help in absorption of fat by -

- A. Micelles formation
- B. Activation of transporter protein
- C. Creation of concentration gradient
- D. All of the above

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Micelles formation**

</details>

---
### Q272 — Caseating necrosis occurs in *(2015)*

Caseating necrosis occurs in

- A. Brain
- B. liver
- C. kidney
- D. lung

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. lung**

</details>

---
### Q273 — Hypophosphatemic rickets is *(2015)*

Hypophosphatemic rickets is

- A. AR
- B. AD
- C. XR
- D. XD

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. XD**

</details>

---
### Q274 — Chymotrypsinogen is activated into chymotrypsin by

Chymotrypsinogen is activated into chymotrypsin by:

- A. Trypsin
- B. Pepsin
- C. Renin
- D. HCl

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Trypsin** — Trypsin cleaves chymotrypsinogen (zymogen) into active chymotrypsin; trypsin itself is activated from trypsinogen by enteropeptidase (enterokinase) in duodenum.

</details>

---
### Q275 — Example of exopeptidase is

Example of exopeptidase is ?

- A. Trypsin
- B. Chymotrypsin
- C. Elastase
- D. Carboxy peptidases

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Carboxy peptidases** — Carboxypeptidase A/B (pancreatic) cleaves terminal amino acids from C-end; trypsin, chymotrypsin, elastase are endopeptidases cleaving internal peptide bonds.

</details>

---
### Q276 — Secretion of bile out of hepactocytes

Secretion of bile out of hepactocytes occurs via?

- A. Passive diffusion
- B. Facilitated diffusion
- C. Osmosis
- D. Active transport

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Active transport** — Bile secretion into canaliculi uses ATP-dependent active transport via BSEP/MRP2 transporters; concentration gradient against canalicular lumen requires energy.

</details>

---
### Q277 — Absorption of calcium ion is affected

Absorption of calcium ion is affected mostly by ?

- A. Calcitriol
- B. Parathormone
- C. Glucocorticoids
- D. ACTH

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Calcitriol** — Calcitriol (1,25-dihydroxyvitamin D3) upregulates calbindin and TRPV6 in enterocytes, driving active transcellular calcium absorption in duodenum.

</details>

---
### Q278 — Vitamin D absorption is decreased by

Vitamin D absorption is decreased by ?

- A. Proteins
- B. Acid
- C. Lactose
- D. Fat malabsorption

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Fat malabsorption** — Vitamin D is fat-soluble; requires bile salts and micelle formation. Fat malabsorption (steatorrhea, cholestasis, celiac) impairs its intestinal uptake.

</details>

---
### Q279 — Effect of cholecystokinin on GIT

Effect of cholecystokinin on GIT ?

- A. Increases gastric acid secretion
- B. Increases small intestinal peristlasis
- C. Increases gastric motility
- D. Relaxes gall bladder

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Increases small intestinal peristlasis** — CCK contracts gallbladder, relaxes sphincter of Oddi, stimulates pancreatic enzyme secretion, and increases small intestinal peristalsis while delaying gastric emptying.

</details>

---
### Q280 — Small intestinal peristalsis is controlled by

Small intestinal peristalsis is controlled by :

- A. Myentric plexus
- B. Meissners plexus
- C. Vagus nerve
- D. Para sympathetic system

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Myentric plexus** — Auerbach's (myenteric) plexus between longitudinal and circular muscle layers controls GI motility/peristalsis; Meissner's submucosal plexus regulates secretion.

</details>

---
### Q281 — How is folic acid absorbed in

How is folic acid absorbed in proximal jejunum?

- A. Facilitated diffusion
- B. Active transport
- C. Passive transport
- D. Both active & passive transport

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Both active & passive transport** — Folate absorbed in proximal jejunum via PCFT (proton-coupled active transport) at low concentrations and passive diffusion at high concentrations.

</details>

---
### Q282 — Colipase is

Colipase is:

- A. Is secreted by Oxyntic cells
- B. Is secreted in the active form
- C. Helps gastric lipase
- D. Encoded by the gene CLPS

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Encoded by the gene CLPS** — Colipase, encoded by CLPS gene, is secreted as procolipase by pancreas; trypsin activates it to anchor pancreatic lipase to bile-salt-coated lipid droplets.

</details>

---
### Q283 — Cholecystokinin is produced from

Cholecystokinin is produced from:

- A. Hepatocyte
- B. Gastric mucosa
- C. Duodenal mucosa
- D. Epithelial cells of distal common bile duct

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Duodenal mucosa** — CCK is secreted by I-cells of duodenal and jejunal mucosa in response to luminal fatty acids and amino acids.

</details>

---
### Q284 — Somatomedin - C deficiency causes

Somatomedin - C deficiency causes?

- A. Achondroplasia
- B. Cretinism
- C. Growth retardation
- D. Juvenile DM

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Growth retardation** — Somatomedin-C (IGF-1) mediates GH's growth effects; its deficiency (Laron dwarfism, GH receptor defect) causes proportionate growth retardation.

</details>

---
### Q285 — All are about adiponectin except

All are about adiponectin except ?

- A. Secreted by adipose tissue
- B. Lowers glucose
- C. Increases FFA oxidation
- D. Positive Correlation with BMI

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Positive Correlation with BMI** — Adiponectin is inversely correlated with BMI (decreased in obesity); it enhances insulin sensitivity, lowers glucose, and stimulates fatty acid oxidation via AMPK.

</details>

---
### Q286 — Hormone responsible for galactopoiesis

Hormone responsible for galactopoiesis ?

- A. Growth hormone
- B. Insulin
- C. Oxytocin
- D. Prolactin

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Prolactin** — Prolactin maintains established milk production (galactopoiesis); oxytocin causes milk ejection/letdown via myoepithelial contraction.

</details>

---
### Q287 — Ionotropic effect of thyroid hormone is

Ionotropic effect of thyroid hormone is by ?

- A. Membrane receptors
- B. cAMP
- C. Potentiation of Catecholamines
- D. cGMP

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Potentiation of Catecholamines** — T3 upregulates beta-adrenergic receptors on myocardium, potentiating catecholamine action; increases Na/K-ATPase and SERCA expression for positive inotropy.

</details>

---
### Q288 — 3 - subumit of insulin receptor

3 - subumit of insulin receptor binds to ?

- A. Guanylyl cyclase
- B. Adenylyl cyclase
- C. IP3-DAG
- D. Tyrosine kinase

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Tyrosine kinase** — Insulin receptor beta-subunit has intrinsic tyrosine kinase activity; autophosphorylation upon insulin binding recruits IRS-1 and downstream PI3K/Akt signaling.

</details>

---
### Q289 — Insulin mediated transport of glucose is

Insulin mediated transport of glucose is ?

- A. Seen in adipose tissue
- B. Via GLUT-2
- C. Main Mechanism in RBCs
- D. All are true

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Seen in adipose tissue** — Insulin-dependent GLUT-4 mediates glucose uptake in adipose tissue and skeletal muscle; GLUT-2 (liver/beta cells) and GLUT-1 (RBC) are insulin-independent.

</details>

---
### Q290 — Acrosome reaction is seen in

Acrosome reaction is seen in?

- A. Spermatogenesis
- B. Oogenesis
- C. Fertilization
- D. Mensturation

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Fertilization** — Acrosome reaction occurs at fertilization when capacitated sperm contacts zona pellucida; releases hyaluronidase and acrosin to penetrate the oocyte.

</details>

---
### Q291 — Iron is conserved by

Iron is conserved by?

- A. Hemopexin
- B. Hepicidin
- C. Hemomedins
- D. None

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Hemopexin** — Hemopexin binds free heme released from hemoglobin and delivers it to hepatocytes, conserving iron; haptoglobin binds free hemoglobin similarly.

</details>

---
### Q292 — Primitive red cells first originates in

Primitive red cells first originates in the early embryonic life is in:

- A. Liver
- B. Yolk sac
- C. Bone marrow
- D. Spleen

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Yolk sac** — Mesoblastic phase: primitive nucleated erythrocytes arise in yolk sac blood islands at 3rd week; later hepatic then medullary phases take over.

</details>

---
### Q293 — Converging point of both pathway in

Converging point of both pathway in coagulation is at:

- A. Factor VIII
- B. Stuart factor X
- C. Factor IX
- D. Factor VII

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Stuart factor X** — Factor X (Stuart-Prower) is activated by both intrinsic (IXa/VIIIa) and extrinsic (VIIa/TF) pathways, marking start of common pathway converting prothrombin to thrombin.

</details>

---
### Q294 — Penile erection is mediated by

Penile erection is mediated by?

- A. Parasympathetic system via muscarinic receptors
- B. Parasympathetic system via nicotinic receptors
- C. Sympathetic system via a-receptors
- D. Sympathetic system via (3-receptors

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Parasympathetic system via muscarinic receptors** — Parasympathetic S2-S4 nerves release ACh and NO; activates muscarinic receptors and guanylate cyclase, raising cGMP, relaxing cavernosal smooth muscle.

</details>

---
### Q295 — Inhibition of heart by vagus is

Inhibition of heart by vagus is mediated by which receptors -

- A. M1
- B. M2
- C. NN
- D. NM2

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. M2** — Vagal ACh acts on cardiac M2 muscarinic receptors (SA/AV node) causing bradycardia via Gi-coupled inhibition of adenylyl cyclase and K+ channel opening.

</details>

---
### Q296 — Inhibition of heart by vagus by

Inhibition of heart by vagus by M2 receptors is medicated by which mechanism?

- A. cAMP
- B. Cat'
- C. DAG
- D. None

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. cAMP** — M2 receptor couples to Gi, inhibiting adenylyl cyclase, reducing cAMP; decreases pacemaker funny current (If) and slows SA node depolarization.

</details>

---
### Q297 — Glucagon activates which enzyme

Glucagon activates which enzyme ?

- A. Pepsinogen
- B. Trypsinogen
- C. Adenylyl cyclase
- D. None

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Adenylyl cyclase** — Glucagon binds Gs-coupled hepatic receptor, activating adenylyl cyclase to raise cAMP, which activates PKA driving glycogenolysis and gluconeogenesis.

</details>

---
### Q298 — Size of platelets is

Size of platelets is ?

- A. 1 A°
- B. 2 A°
- C. 1µm
- D. 2µm

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. 2µm** — Platelets (thrombocytes) are anucleate fragments of megakaryocytes, measuring 2-4 micrometers in diameter; lifespan about 8-10 days.

</details>

---
### Q299 — If HbAl C is 8, What

If HbAl C is 8%, What will be the value of blood glucose [mg/d1] ?

- A. 100
- B. 150
- C. 200
- D. 300

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. 200** — By ADAG formula: mean glucose = 28.7 x HbA1c - 46.7; HbA1c of 8% corresponds to estimated average blood glucose around 180-200 mg/dL.

</details>

---
### Q300 — Preaccelerin is

Preaccelerin is ?

- A. Eater II
- B. Facter V
- C. Facter VII
- D. Facter X

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Facter VII** — Proaccelerin is Factor V (labile factor); proconvertin is Factor VII. Confusion in question, but proaccelerin classically refers to Factor V cofactor of Xa.

</details>

---
### Q301 — Conversion of prekallikrein to kallikrein requires

Conversion of prekallikrein to kallikrein requires which clotting factor -

- A. XIII
- B. XII
- C. XI
- D. X

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. XII** — Factor XIIa (Hageman factor, activated by contact with negatively charged surfaces) cleaves prekallikrein to kallikrein in intrinsic pathway initiation.

</details>

---
### Q302 — Caissons disease is associated with

Caisson's disease is associated with?

- A. Rapid descend in aircraft
- B. Rapid descend of deep sea divers
- C. Underwater construction workers
- D. All of the above

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Underwater construction workers** — Caisson disease (decompression sickness) affects underwater construction workers and divers ascending rapidly; dissolved nitrogen forms bubbles in tissues/joints.

</details>

---
### Q303 — Following is true about oculocardiac reflex

Following is true about oculocardiac reflex except ?

- A. It is also called aschner phenomenon
- B. It is mediated by occulomotor and vagus nerve
- C. It is characterized by bradycardia following traction on extra-
- D. Reflex is more sensitive in neonates

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. It is mediated by occulomotor and vagus nerve** — Oculocardiac (Aschner) reflex: trigeminal (ophthalmic V1, afferent) to vagus (efferent) causes bradycardia on globe traction; oculomotor nerve not involved.

</details>

---
### Q304 — Blood testis barrier in testis is

Blood testis barrier in testis is formed by?

- A. Sertoli cells
- B. Leydig cells
- C. Granulosa cells
- D. None

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Sertoli cells** — Sertoli cells form tight junctions between adjacent cells in seminiferous tubules, creating the blood-testis barrier that protects developing spermatogenic cells.

</details>

---
### Q305 — Which of the following is false

Which of the following is false about peripheral nerve injury?

- A. Neuropraxia is irreversible
- B. Epineurium is intact in axonotmesis
- C. Neurotmesis is the most severe form of injury
- D. Wellerian generation starts in axonotmesis

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Neuropraxia is irreversible** — Neuropraxia is reversible — it is a transient conduction block from focal demyelination with intact axon and endoneurium, recovering fully in days to weeks.

</details>

---
### Q306 — First to occur after a sharp

First to occur after a sharp nerve cut ?

- A. Chromatolysis
- B. Polymorphic arrangement
- C. Increased protein synthesis
- D. Macrophage activation

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Chromatolysis** — Chromatolysis is the earliest neuronal cell-body response — Nissl substance dissolves and nucleus shifts peripherally within 24-48 hours after axotomy.

</details>

---
### Q307 — LDH has how many isoenzymes

LDH has how many isoenzymes

- A. 3, based on B and M polypeptide subunits
- B. 5, based on B and M polypeptide subunits
- C. 7, based on H and M polypeptide subunits
- D. 5, based on H and M polypeptide subunits

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. 5, based on H and M polypeptide subunits** — Lactate dehydrogenase has 5 tetrameric isoenzymes (LDH1-5) formed by combinations of H (heart) and M (muscle) polypeptide subunits.

</details>

---
### Q308 — Which of the following is known

Which of the following is known as suicidal enzyme?

- A. Lipoxygenase
- B. Cyclooxygenase
- C. Thromboxane synthatase
- D. 5' nucleotidase

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Cyclooxygenase** — Cyclooxygenase (COX) self-inactivates after producing ~400 prostaglandin molecules due to oxidative damage from its own peroxide intermediate, hence 'suicide enzyme'.

</details>

---
### Q309 — Rate limiting enzyme in bile acid

Rate limiting enzyme in bile acid synthesis ?

- A. Desmolase
- B. 21 α-hydroxylase
- C. 7α-hydroxylase
- D. 12α-hydroxylase

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. 7α-hydroxylase** — Cholesterol 7-alpha-hydroxylase (CYP7A1) is rate-limiting in the classical bile acid synthesis pathway, converting cholesterol to 7-alpha-hydroxycholesterol in hepatocytes.

</details>

---
### Q310 — Enzyme activity is expressed as

Enzyme activity is expressed as:

- A. Millimoles /lit?
- B. Milli gm/lit?
- C. Mg/ dl
- D. Micromoles/min

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Micromoles/min** — Enzyme activity unit (IU) equals micromoles of substrate converted per minute under standard conditions; SI katal is moles per second.

</details>

---
### Q311 — Lysyl oxidase requires which cofactor -

Lysyl oxidase requires which cofactor -

- A. Zn
- B. Cu
- C. Se
- D. Fe

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Cu** — Lysyl oxidase is a copper-dependent amine oxidase that cross-links lysine residues in collagen and elastin; deficiency causes Menkes and occipital horn syndromes.

</details>

---
### Q312 — Mechanism of conversion of trypsinogen to

Mechanism of conversion of trypsinogen to trypsin -

- A. Hydrolysis
- B. Phosphorylation
- C. Removal of part of protein
- D. Removal of Carboxyl group

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Removal of part of protein** — Enterokinase cleaves an N-terminal hexapeptide from trypsinogen — limited proteolysis (partial protein removal) activates the zymogen to trypsin.

</details>

---
### Q313 — Immediate source of energy is

Immediate source of energy is ?

- A. Cori's cycle
- B. HMP
- C. ATP
- D. TCA cycle

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. ATP** — ATP hydrolysis to ADP and inorganic phosphate releases ~7.3 kcal/mol; it is the universal immediate energy currency for cellular work.

</details>

---
### Q314 — True about NADP -

True about NADP -

- A. Acts as coenzyme form of Niacin
- B. Involved in HMP shunt
- C. Not involved in glycolysis
- D. All are true

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. All are true** — NADP is the phosphorylated coenzyme form of niacin (vitamin B3); NADPH is generated by the HMP shunt, while glycolysis uses NAD not NADP.

</details>

---
### Q315 — In malate shuttle, NADH Produces how

In malate shuttle, NADH Produces how many ATPs

- A. 1
- B. 1-5
- C. 2
- D. 2.5

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. 2.5** — Malate-aspartate shuttle transfers cytosolic NADH equivalents into mitochondria as NADH, yielding 2.5 ATP via complex I of the electron transport chain.

</details>

---
### Q316 — Not a substrate for gluconeogenesis -

Not a substrate for gluconeogenesis -

- A. Glycerol
- B. Leucine
- C. Lactate
- D. Propionate

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Leucine** — Leucine is a purely ketogenic amino acid degraded to acetyl-CoA/acetoacetate; mammals cannot convert acetyl-CoA to glucose, so leucine cannot enter gluconeogenesis.

</details>

---
### Q317 — Pyruvate dehydrogenase requires all cofactors exce

Pyruvate dehydrogenase requires all cofactors except

- A. Thiamin
- B. Riboflavin
- C. Niacin
- D. Pyridoxin

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Pyridoxin** — PDH complex requires TPP (thiamine), FAD (riboflavin), NAD (niacin), lipoic acid, and coenzyme A — not pyridoxine (B6).

</details>

---
### Q318 — Lactate produced anaerobically is used by

Lactate produced anaerobically is used by

- A. Gluconeogenesis & Glycolysis
- B. Cori cycle & gluconeogenesis
- C. TCA cycle & Glycogenolysis
- D. Cori cycle only

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Cori cycle & gluconeogenesis** — Muscle lactate enters Cori cycle — transported to liver where gluconeogenesis converts it back to glucose via lactate dehydrogenase and pyruvate.

</details>

---
### Q319 — Citrate synthase is inhibited by -

Citrate synthase is inhibited by -

- A. ATP
- B. ADP
- C. Insulin
- D. Glucagon

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. ATP** — ATP allosterically inhibits citrate synthase (along with NADH, succinyl-CoA, citrate), slowing TCA cycle when cellular energy charge is high.

</details>

---
### Q320 — Fumarate is formed from which amino

Fumarate is formed from which amino acid

- A. Methionine
- B. Valine
- C. Histidine
- D. Tyrosine

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Tyrosine** — Tyrosine degradation via homogentisate and fumarylacetoacetate produces fumarate and acetoacetate; phenylalanine also enters this pathway after hydroxylation.

</details>

---
### Q321 — The major role of 2, 3

The major role of 2, 3 bisphosphoglycerate in RBCs is -

- A. Acid-base balance
- B. Reversal of glycolysis
- C. Release of oxygen
- D. Binding of oxygen

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Release of oxygen** — 2,3-BPG binds deoxyhemoglobin in the central cavity, stabilizing T-state and shifting oxygen dissociation curve right, promoting O2 release to tissues.

</details>

---
### Q322 — Immediate metabolic products during conversion of

Immediate metabolic products during conversion of Fructus 1-6 bisphosphate to 2 molecules of pyruvate -

- A. Glyceraldehyde-3-phosphate and 1,3-bisphosphoglycerate
- B. Dihydroxyacetone phosphate and 1,3 bisphos​phoglycerate
- C. Glyceraldehyde-3-phosphate and dihydroxy-acetone phosphate
- D. 3-phosphoglycerate and 1,3 bisphosglycerate

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Glyceraldehyde-3-phosphate and dihydroxy-acetone phosphate** — Aldolase cleaves fructose-1,6-bisphosphate into glyceraldehyde-3-phosphate and dihydroxyacetone phosphate; triose phosphate isomerase then interconverts them.

</details>

---
### Q323 — In conversion of glucose to glucose-6-

In conversion of glucose to glucose-6- phosphate in glycolysis true is

- A. Glucokinase has low km
- B. Hexokinase is found only in liver
- C. Glucokinase is induced by insulin
- D. Hexokinase is not specific for glucose

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Glucokinase is induced by insulin** — Hepatic glucokinase has high Km (~10 mM), is glucose-specific, and is induced by insulin; hexokinase has low Km and broad specificity.

</details>

---
### Q324 — Most abundant source of fuel in

Most abundant source of fuel in starvation -

- A. Liver glycogen
- B. Muscle glycogen
- C. Adipose tissue
- D. Blood glucose

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Adipose tissue** — Adipose triglycerides (~15 kg, ~135,000 kcal) vastly exceed glycogen (~0.5 kg) — fatty acid oxidation sustains energy during prolonged starvation.

</details>

---
### Q325 — Major metabolism of saturated fatty acids

Major metabolism of saturated fatty acids in the mitochondria is called as -

- A. β-oxidation
- B. α-oxidation
- C. ω-oxidation
- D. None of the above

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. β-oxidation** — Beta-oxidation occurs in the mitochondrial matrix — sequential removal of 2-carbon acetyl-CoA units from saturated fatty acyl-CoA, generating FADH2 and NADH.

</details>

---
### Q326 — Bile acids are synthesized from

Bile acids are synthesized from ?

- A. Heme
- B. Cholesterol
- C. Ribulose
- D. Arachidonic acid

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Cholesterol** — Bile acids (cholic, chenodeoxycholic) are synthesized from cholesterol in hepatocytes; cholesterol 7-alpha-hydroxylase is the rate-limiting step.

</details>

---
### Q327 — Which method is used to separate

Which method is used to separate a mixture of lipids -

- A. Electrophoresis
- B. Chromatography
- C. Isoelectric focusing
- D. PAGE

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Chromatography** — Thin-layer or column chromatography separates lipids based on polarity differences with non-polar solvents; electrophoresis/PAGE separate charged molecules like proteins.

</details>

---
### Q328 — Transport of lipids from the intestine

Transport of lipids from the intestine to other tissues is by -

- A. Chylomicrons
- B. LDL
- C. HDL
- D. VI DL

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Chylomicrons** — Chylomicrons assembled in enterocytes transport dietary triglycerides and cholesterol from intestine via lymphatics to peripheral tissues for lipoprotein lipase hydrolysis.

</details>

---
### Q329 — (β-oxidation of palmitic acid yields

(β-oxidation of palmitic acid yields

- A. 3 acetyl CoA
- B. 129 ATP net
- C. 131 ATP net
- D. 16 Acetyl CoA

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. 129 ATP net** — Palmitate (C16) yields 8 acetyl-CoA, 7 FADH2, 7 NADH = 131 ATP gross, minus 2 ATP for activation = 129 ATP net.

</details>

---
### Q330 — All are true about Niemann- Pick

All are true about Niemann- Pick disease except -

- A. Due to deficiency of sphingomyelinase
- B. CNS symptoms in type A
- C. Histiocytes showing PAS positive inclusions and Type B is less
- D. None

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. None** — Niemann-Pick disease — sphingomyelinase deficiency (types A/B) causes sphingomyelin accumulation; type A has severe CNS involvement, foam cells are PAS-positive.

</details>

---
### Q331 — Plasminogen domain resembles

Plasminogen domain resembles

- A. Fibrinogen
- B. LDL receptor
- C. Apolipoprotein (a)
- D. Prothrombin

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Apolipoprotein (a)** — Apolipoprotein(a) of Lp(a) contains kringle domains homologous to plasminogen, competing with plasminogen binding and contributing to thrombogenic atherosclerosis risk.

</details>

---
### Q332 — Acetyl CoA Carboxylase is stimulated by

Acetyl CoA Carboxylase is stimulated by -

- A. Starvation
- B. Glucagon
- C. Citrate
- D. All of the above

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Citrate** — Citrate allosterically activates acetyl-CoA carboxylase (rate-limiting in fatty acid synthesis); insulin activates and glucagon/starvation inhibit via phosphorylation.

</details>

---
### Q333 — Cholesterol is not a precursor for

Cholesterol is not a precursor for synthesis of -

- A. Vitamin D
- B. Progesterone
- C. Bile acids
- D. Lipocortin

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Lipocortin** — Lipocortin (annexin-1) is a phospholipase A2-inhibitory protein induced by glucocorticoids — it is a protein, not a cholesterol derivative.

</details>

---
### Q334 — HDL is called good cholesterol because

HDL is called good cholesterol because -

- A. Removes cholesterol from extrahepatic tissues
- B. Causes transport of cholesterol to extrahepatic tissues
- C. Stimulate hepatic TGs synthesis
- D. Activates lipoprotein lipase

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Removes cholesterol from extrahepatic tissues** — HDL performs reverse cholesterol transport via ApoA-1/LCAT, removing cholesterol from peripheral tissues back to liver; deficiency causes Tangier disease.

</details>

---
### Q335 — Non- essential amino-acids are all except

Non- essential amino-acids are all except -

- A. Basic amino acids
- B. Acidic amino acids
- C. Neutral amino acids
- D. None of the above

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Basic amino acids** — Basic amino acids (lysine, arginine, histidine) are essential/semi-essential; deficiency impairs growth, urea cycle (arginase) and histamine synthesis.

</details>

---
### Q336 — Amino acid which can be used

Amino acid which can be used in both gluconeogenesis and ketogenesis -

- A. Threonine
- B. Valine
- C. Tyrosine
- D. Arginine

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Tyrosine** — Tyrosine is mixed gluco-ketogenic (via fumarate + acetoacetate); enzyme fumarylacetoacetate hydrolase deficiency causes tyrosinemia type I.

</details>

---
### Q337 — In phenylketonuria, diet restriction is advised

In phenylketonuria, diet restriction is advised for -

- A. Tyrosine
- B. Phenylalanine
- C. Maize
- D. All

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Phenylalanine** — PKU is phenylalanine hydroxylase deficiency (BH4 cofactor); restrict dietary phenylalanine to prevent mental retardation and musty urine odor.

</details>

---
### Q338 — Guanidinium group is associated with -

Guanidinium group is associated with -

- A. Tyrosine
- B. Arginine
- C. Histidine
- D. Lysine

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Arginine** — Arginine's side chain bears the guanidinium group (pKa ~12.5); substrate for arginase in urea cycle and NO synthase.

</details>

---
### Q339 — Creatinine is formed from -

Creatinine is formed from -

- A. Glycine
- B. Lysine
- C. Leucine
- D. Histamine

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Glycine** — Creatine synthesized from glycine + arginine + SAM (methyl donor); creatine phosphate spontaneously cyclizes to creatinine, excreted renally.

</details>

---
### Q340 — Coenzyme for phenylalanine hydroxylase is -

Coenzyme for phenylalanine hydroxylase is -

- A. Tetrahydrofolate
- B. Pyridoxal phosphate
- C. S-adenosyl methionine
- D. Tetrahydrobiopterin

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Tetrahydrobiopterin** — Phenylalanine hydroxylase uses tetrahydrobiopterin (BH4) as cofactor; BH4 deficiency causes malignant/atypical PKU unresponsive to phenylalanine restriction.

</details>

---
### Q341 — Glycine is required in formation of

Glycine is required in formation of all except ?

- A. Heme
- B. Purines
- C. Glutathione
- D. Thyroxine

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Thyroxine** — Glycine contributes to heme (ALA synthase), purines (C4,C5,N7), glutathione, creatine; thyroxine derives from tyrosine + iodine via thyroid peroxidase.

</details>

---
### Q342 — Which of the following is required

Which of the following is required in the synthesis of acetylcholine -

- A. Inositol
- B. Carnitine
- C. Glycine
- D. Choline

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Choline** — Acetylcholine synthesized from choline + acetyl-CoA by choline acetyltransferase; choline deficiency causes fatty liver and cholinergic dysfunction.

</details>

---
### Q343 — Most important factor which causes lactic

Most important factor which causes lactic acidosis in alcoholics -

- A. Pruduction of NADH
- B. Formation of acetaldehyde
- C. Production of acetate
- D. None of the above

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Pruduction of NADH** — Alcohol dehydrogenase + ALDH generate excess NADH, shifting pyruvate to lactate via LDH; also causes hypoglycemia and ketoacidosis.

</details>

---
### Q344 — Insulin dependant cells are -

Insulin dependant cells are -

- A. Pituitocytes
- B. Myocytes
- C. Adipocytes
- D. RBCs

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Myocytes** — Myocytes and adipocytes use insulin-dependent GLUT4 transporter; brain (GLUT1/3), RBC (GLUT1), liver (GLUT2) are insulin-independent.

</details>

---
### Q345 — Energy source used by brain in

Energy source used by brain in later days of Starvation is

- A. Glucose
- B. Ketone bodies
- C. Glycogen
- D. Fatty acids

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Ketone bodies** — After ~3 days starvation, brain shifts to ketone bodies (beta-hydroxybutyrate) via HMG-CoA synthase; spares muscle protein and glucose.

</details>

---
### Q346 — Which of the following enzyme activity

Which of the following enzyme activity decreases in fasting?

- A. Hormone sensitive lipase
- B. Glycogen phosphorylase
- C. Acetyl CoA Carboxylase
- D. Phosphofructokinase I

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Phosphofructokinase I** — PFK-1 (glycolysis rate-limiting) is inhibited in fasting by low fructose-2,6-bisphosphate and high glucagon; gluconeogenesis dominates instead.

</details>

---
### Q347 — Vitamin formed in the body -

Vitamin formed in the body -

- A. B 1
- B. B 3
- C. B 6
- D. B 12

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. B 12** — Vitamin B12 (cobalamin) synthesized by colonic bacteria; also niacin from tryptophan, vitamin D from cholesterol, vitamin K by gut flora.

</details>

---
### Q348 — Pruritis Itching is caused by deficiency

Pruritis [Itching] is caused by deficiency of -

- A. HMB synthase
- B. 5-ALA dehydratase
- C. Uroporphyrinogen - I synthase
- D. Uroporphyrinogen - III synthase

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Uroporphyrinogen - III synthase** — Congenital erythropoietic porphyria from uroporphyrinogen III synthase deficiency causes photosensitivity, pruritus, red urine, hemolytic anemia.

</details>

---
### Q349 — Vitamin acting on intranuclear receptors -

Vitamin acting on intranuclear receptors -

- A. Vitamin K
- B. Vitamin D
- C. Vitamin E
- D. Thiamine

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Vitamin D** — Vitamin D (calcitriol) binds VDR nuclear receptor (steroid superfamily); deficiency causes rickets/osteomalacia and impaired calcium absorption.

</details>

---
### Q350 — Which of the following has antioxidant

Which of the following has antioxidant property?

- A. Selenium
- B. Copper
- C. Zinc
- D. All

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. All** — Selenium (GSH peroxidase cofactor), copper/zinc (SOD cofactors) all act as antioxidants; deficiencies cause Keshan, Menkes, acrodermatitis enteropathica.

</details>

---
### Q351 — Niacin acts as coenzyme -

Niacin acts as coenzyme -

- A. TPP
- B. FAQ
- C. NAD
- D. CoA (pantothenic acid)

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. NAD** — Niacin (B3) forms NAD+/NADP+ coenzymes for redox reactions; deficiency causes pellagra (dermatitis, diarrhea, dementia); seen in Hartnup disease.

</details>

---
### Q352 — Coenzyme not required in formation of

Coenzyme not required in formation of glutamate-

- A. Thiamine pyrophosphate
- B. Pyridoxial phosphate
- C. Niacin
- D. None of the above

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Thiamine pyrophosphate** — Glutamate formation uses pyridoxal phosphate (transamination) and NAD(P)H (glutamate dehydrogenase); TPP (B1) is for oxidative decarboxylation, not glutamate.

</details>

---
### Q353 — Not a metabolic product of urea

Not a metabolic product of urea cycle -

- A. Citrulline
- B. Ornithine
- C. Alanine
- D. Arginine

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Alanine** — Urea cycle intermediates: ornithine, citrulline, argininosuccinate, arginine; alanine is glucose-alanine cycle product, not urea cycle.

</details>

---
### Q354 — Which amino acid is not involved

Which amino acid is not involved in transamination -

- A. Alanine
- B. Aspartate
- C. Lysine
- D. Histidine

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Lysine** — Lysine and threonine are the only amino acids that do not undergo transamination; transaminases require PLP (vitamin B6) cofactor.

</details>

---
### Q355 — Boiled cabbage or rancid butter smelling

Boiled cabbage or rancid butter smelling urine is seen in

- A. Phenylketonuria
- B. Tyrosinemia
- C. Isovaleric Acidaemia
- D. Multiple carboxylase deficiency

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Tyrosinemia** — Tyrosinemia type I (fumarylacetoacetate hydrolase deficiency) causes cabbage-like urine odor, liver failure, renal Fanconi syndrome, hepatocellular carcinoma.

</details>

---
### Q356 — Apo B48 is synthesized in -

Apo B48 is synthesized in -

- A. Liver
- B. Kidney
- C. Intestine
- D. RBCs

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Intestine** — ApoB-48 synthesized in intestine via mRNA editing (APOBEC-1); component of chylomicrons; ApoB-100 made in liver for VLDL/LDL.

</details>

---
### Q357 — Allantoin is the end product of

Allantoin is the end product of metabolism of ?

- A. Glycogen
- B. Purine
- C. Pyrimidine
- D. Histidine

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Purine** — Purines degrade via xanthine oxidase to uric acid; in non-primate mammals uricase converts urate to allantoin; humans lack uricase causing gout.

</details>

---
### Q358 — True about sigma factor

True about sigma factor?

- A. Subunit of 50s ribosome
- B. Subunit of DNA polymerase
- C. Subunit of RNA polymerase
- D. Initiates DNA replication

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Subunit of RNA polymerase** — Sigma factor is detachable subunit of prokaryotic RNA polymerase holoenzyme; recognizes promoter (-10, -35 boxes) to initiate transcription.

</details>

---
### Q359 — Replication and transcription are similar processe

Replication and transcription are similar processes in mechanistic terms because both :

- A. Use RNA primers for initiation.
- B. Use deoxyribonucleotides as precursors.
- C. Are semi conserved events
- D. Involve phosphodiester bond formation with elongation

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Involve phosphodiester bond formation with elongation** — Both replication (DNA pol) and transcription (RNA pol) elongate in 5' to 3' direction via phosphodiester bond formation, releasing pyrophosphate.

</details>

---
### Q360 — What are okazzaki fragments -

What are okazzaki fragments -

- A. Long pieces of DNA on lagging strand
- B. Long pieces of DNA on leading strand
- C. Short pieces of DNA on lagging strand
- D. Short pieces of DNA on leading strand

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Short pieces of DNA on lagging strand** — Okazaki fragments: short (100-200 nt eukaryotes, 1000-2000 prokaryotes) DNA pieces on lagging strand; joined by DNA ligase after RNA primer removal.

</details>

---
### Q361 — First purine nucleotide, which is synthesized

First purine nucleotide, which is synthesized in purine biosynthesis ?

- A. AMP
- B. GMP
- C. IMP
- D. UMP

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. IMP** — IMP (inosine monophosphate) is first purine nucleotide formed from PRPP; branches to AMP (via adenylosuccinate) and GMP (via xanthosine monophosphate).

</details>

---
### Q362 — C4, C5, N7 in purine ring

C4, C5, N7 in purine ring are derived from -

- A. Aspartate
- B. Glycine
- C. Glutamine
- D. CO

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Glutamine** — Glycine contributes C4, C5, N7 of purine ring as intact unit; N1 from aspartate, N3/N9 from glutamine, C2/C8 from formyl-THF, C6 from CO2.

</details>

---
### Q363 — Most important tool used in genetic

Most important tool used in genetic engineering

- A. Halicase
- B. Topoisomerase
- C. DNA Ligase
- D. Restriction endonuclease

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Restriction endonuclease** — Restriction endonucleases (Type II) cut DNA at specific palindromic sequences producing sticky/blunt ends; essential for recombinant DNA cloning.

</details>

---
### Q364 — The gaps between segments of DNA

The gaps between segments of DNA on the lagging strand produced by restriction enzymes are rejoined sealed by:

- A. DNA Ligases
- B. DNA Helicase
- C. DNA topoisomerase
- D. DNA phosphorylase

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. DNA Ligases** — **A. DNA Ligases** — DNA ligase seals the phosphodiester bond gaps between Okazaki fragments on the lagging strand by catalysing nick ligation using NAD+ or ATP as cofactor.

</details>

---
### Q365 — Which is not a chaperon protein

Which is not a chaperon protein -

- A. Calnexin
- B. Protein disulfide isomerase
- C. Calreticulin
- D. Calbindin

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Calbindin** — **D. Calbindin** — Calbindin is a calcium-binding protein; calnexin, calreticulin, and protein disulfide isomerase are ER-resident chaperones assisting in glycoprotein folding and quality control.

</details>

---
### Q366 — All are functions of glycosaminglycans except

All are functions of glycosaminglycans except -

- A. Lubrication
- B. Wound healing
- C. Anticoagulant
- D. Transport of lipids

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Transport of lipids** — **D. Transport of lipids** — Glycosaminoglycans (GAGs) function in lubrication (hyaluronic acid), wound healing (heparan sulfate), and anticoagulation (heparin); lipid transport is performed by lipoproteins, not GAGs.

</details>

---
### Q367 — a-aminolevulinic acid is a metabolic product

a-aminolevulinic acid is a metabolic product in synthesis of -

- A. Tryptophan
- B. Collagen
- C. Glycosaminoglycans
- D. Heme

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Heme** — **D. Heme** — δ-Aminolevulinic acid (ALA) is the first committed intermediate in heme biosynthesis, formed from succinyl-CoA and glycine by ALA synthase in mitochondria.

</details>

---
### Q368 — Which is an inhibitor of ferrochelatase

Which is an inhibitor of ferrochelatase ?

- A. Lead
- B. Mercury
- C. Iron
- D. Arsenic

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Lead** — **A. Lead** — Lead (Pb) inhibits ferrochelatase (the enzyme that inserts Fe²⁺ into protoporphyrin IX to form heme), causing accumulation of zinc protoporphyrin and producing microcytic anaemia.

</details>

---
### Q369 — Bile acids consist of all except

Bile acids consist of all except -

- A. Lithocholic acid
- B. Taurocholic acid
- C. Deoxycholic acid
- D. Chendeoxycholic acid

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Taurocholic acid** — **B. Taurocholic acid** — Taurocholic acid is a conjugated bile salt (bile acid + taurine), not a free bile acid; lithocholic, deoxycholic, and chenodeoxycholic acids are the primary free bile acids.

</details>

---
### Q370 — True about cAMP and cGMP -

True about cAMP and cGMP -

- A. Second messengers
- B. Act on membrane receptors
- C. Act by post-translational modification
- D. All of the above

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. All of the above** — **D. All of the above** — cAMP and cGMP are second messengers that bind intracellular receptors, act via post-translational phosphorylation through PKA/PKG, and are generated downstream of membrane receptor activation.

</details>

---
### Q371 — Spectroscopy is used for interaction of

Spectroscopy is used for interaction of -

- A. Electromagnetic radiation
- B. Protons
- C. Alpha particles
- D. Positrons

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Electromagnetic radiation** — **A. Electromagnetic radiation** — Spectroscopy studies the interaction of electromagnetic radiation (UV, visible, IR, NMR) with matter to determine molecular structure, concentration, and bonding characteristics.

</details>

---
### Q372 — Abnormal proteins which are bound to

Abnormal proteins which are bound to ubiquitin are degraded in -

- A. Proteosomes
- B. Golgi apparatus
- C. Smooth ER
- D. Lysosomes

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Proteosomes** — **A. Proteosomes** — Poly-ubiquitinated abnormal proteins are recognised and degraded by the 26S proteasome (ATP-dependent multicatalytic protease complex) in the cytoplasm and nucleus.

</details>

---
### Q373 — Enzyme degradation is caused by -

Enzyme degradation is caused by -

- A. Ubiquitin
- B. RNAse
- C. Zymase
- D. Chaperone

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Ubiquitin** — **A. Ubiquitin** — Ubiquitin tags misfolded or damaged enzymes for proteolytic degradation via the 26S proteasome; multiple ubiquitin molecules form a poly-ubiquitin chain that serves as the degradation signal.

</details>

---
### Q374 — G1cNAc-P-P- oligosacharride is -

G1cNAc-P-P- oligosacharride is -

- A. Proteoglycan
- B. Glycoprotein
- C. Collagen
- D. Phospholipid

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Glycoprotein** — **B. Glycoprotein** — GlcNAc-P-P-dolichol (dolichol pyrophosphate-oligosaccharide) is the lipid-linked precursor used in N-linked glycosylation of glycoproteins in the ER.

</details>

---
### Q375 — Prolyl hydroxylase require which cofactor -

Prolyl hydroxylase require which cofactor -

- A. Sc
- B. Vitamin C
- C. Mo
- D. Vitamin K

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Vitamin C** — **B. Vitamin C** — Prolyl hydroxylase (and lysyl hydroxylase) require ascorbic acid (Vitamin C) as a cofactor to hydroxylate proline residues, essential for stable triple-helix collagen formation; deficiency causes scurvy.

</details>

---
### Q376 — Which of following is not a

Which of following is not a free radical -

- A. H202
- B. (M?
- C. Superoxide anion
- D. HOC1-

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. HOC1-** — **D. HOCl⁻** — Hypochlorite (HOCl) is a reactive oxygen species but not a free radical as it has no unpaired electrons; H₂O₂ and superoxide anion are also ROS, but only superoxide is a true radical.

</details>

---
### Q377 — Not an irreversible injury-

Not an irreversible injury-

- A. Pyknosis
- B. Karyorrhexis
- C. Karyolysis
- D. Bleb formation

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Bleb formation** — **D. Bleb formation** — Cell membrane blebbing is a reversible early injury change; pyknosis (nuclear condensation), karyorrhexis (nuclear fragmentation), and karyolysis (nuclear dissolution) all indicate irreversible cell death.

</details>

---
### Q378 — Hypertrophy is -

Hypertrophy is -

- A. Increase in cell number
- B. Increase in cell size
- C. Decrease in cell number
- D. Decrease in cell size

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Increase in cell size** — **B. Increase in cell size** — Hypertrophy is an increase in cell size (not number) due to increased synthesis of structural proteins, seen in cardiac muscle and skeletal muscle under increased workload.

</details>

---
### Q379 — Hyaline degeneration is found in -

Hyaline degeneration is found in -

- A. MI
- B. Parkinson's' disease
- C. Yellow fever hepatitis
- D. Basophilic cell of pituitary

<details><summary>Answer</summary>
