### Q913 — of the following is false about *(2015)*

Which of the following is false about undescended testis ?

- A. More common on the right side
- B. Hormonal therapy is effective
- C. Increased risk of malignancy
- D. Secondary sexual characteristics are normal

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Hormonal therapy is effective**

</details>

---
### Q914 — newborn baby has a head circumference *(2015)*

A newborn baby has a head circumference of 35 ems. at birth, His optimal head circumference will be 43 ems at -

- A. 4 months of age
- B. 6 months of age
- C. 8 months of age
- D. 12 months of age

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. 6 months of age**

</details>

---
### Q915 — Common hepatic artery is a branch

Common hepatic artery is a branch of -

- A. Splenic artery
- B. Superior mesenteric artery
- C. Inferior mesenteric artery
- D. Coeliac trunk

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Coeliac trunk** — Coeliac trunk trifurcates into common hepatic, left gastric, and splenic arteries; supplies foregut derivatives at T12 level.

</details>

---
### Q916 — Tail of pancrease develops from -

Tail of pancrease develops from -

- A. Hepatic diverticulum
- B. Dorsal pancreatic duct
- C. Ventral pancreatic duct
- D. All of the above

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Dorsal pancreatic duct** — Dorsal pancreatic bud forms body, tail, isthmus, and most of head; ventral bud forms uncinate process and inferior head.

</details>

---
### Q917 — Structures passing through Calots triangle are

Structures passing through Calot's triangle are all EXCEPT:

- A. Portal vein
- B. Cystic artery
- C. Right hepatic artery
- D. Lymph node of Lund

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Portal vein** — Calot's triangle (cystic duct, common hepatic duct, liver edge) contains cystic artery, right hepatic artery, Lund's node — not portal vein.

</details>

---
### Q918 — Shrinking Lung Syndrome is seen in

Shrinking Lung Syndrome is seen in:

- A. SLE
- B. Rheumatoid Arthritis
- C. Scleroderma
- D. Sarcoidosis

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. SLE** — SLE causes diaphragmatic dysfunction with elevated hemidiaphragms and restrictive lung volumes due to phrenic neuropathy or myopathy.

</details>

---
### Q919 — Heart failure cells are

Heart failure cells are

- A. Neutrophils
- B. Macrophages
- C. Lymphocytes
- D. Basophils

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Macrophages** — Hemosiderin-laden alveolar macrophages engulfing RBCs that leak into alveoli during pulmonary venous congestion.

</details>

---
### Q920 — Heart failure cells are seen in

Heart failure cells are seen in -

- A. Pulmonary edema
- B. Pulmonary infarction
- C. Pulmonary abscess
- D. PulmonaryTB

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Pulmonary edema** — Chronic pulmonary edema from left heart failure causes RBC extravasation; macrophages phagocytose hemoglobin producing hemosiderin-laden cells.

</details>

---
### Q921 — Most common nephropathy associated with malignaneN

Most common nephropathy associated with malignaneN is:

- A. Focal segmental glomerulosclerosis (FSGS)
- B. Minimal change disease
- C. IgA nephropathy
- D. Membranous glomerulonephritis

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Membranous glomerulonephritis** — Membranous glomerulonephritis is the classic paraneoplastic nephropathy in solid tumors (lung, colon, breast) via subepithelial immune deposits.

</details>

---
### Q922 — Not true about Alports syndrome -

Not true about Alport's syndrome -

- A. X-linked
- B. Autosomal dominant
- C. Nerve deafness
- D. Glomerulonephritis

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Autosomal dominant** — Alport syndrome is mostly X-linked (COL4A5 mutation) — autosomal recessive less common; autosomal dominant inheritance is not typical.

</details>

---
### Q923 — Fenoldopam is used in the management

Fenoldopam is used in the management of ?

- A. Hypertensive emergencies
- B. Congestive heart failure
- C. Migraine prophylaxis
- D. Tachyarrhythmias

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Hypertensive emergencies** — Fenoldopam is a selective peripheral D1 receptor agonist causing arteriolar vasodilation; used IV for hypertensive emergencies, preserves renal perfusion.

</details>

---
### Q924 — Following are the side effects of

Following are the side effects of thiazides except?

- A. Hypokalemia
- B. Hypocalcemia
- C. Hepatic coma
- D. Impotence

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Hypocalcemia** — Thiazides cause hyperCALCemia (enhance distal Ca reabsorption), hypokalemia, hyperuricemia, hyperglycemia, impotence — not hypocalcemia.

</details>

---
### Q925 — Atypical side effect montelukast

Atypical side effect montelukast ?

- A. Good pasture syndrome
- B. Churg - Strauss syndrome
- C. Membranous glomerulonephritis
- D. Bronchial asthma

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Churg - Strauss syndrome** — Leukotriene antagonists like montelukast are associated with eosinophilic granulomatosis with polyangiitis (Churg-Strauss) unmasked on steroid withdrawal.

</details>

---
### Q926 — Neuropathy caused by INH increases in

Neuropathy caused by INH increases in all except?

- A. Uremia
- B. Hyperthyroidism
- C. Diabetes mellitus
- D. Poor nutrition

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Hyperthyroidism** — INH inhibits pyridoxine causing peripheral neuropathy; worsened by uremia, diabetes, malnutrition, alcoholism — hyperthyroidism does not exacerbate it.

</details>

---
### Q927 — Chlamydia pneumoniae causes causes

Chlamydia pneumoniae causes causes:

- A. LGV
- B. Atherosclerosis
- C. Inclusion conjunctivitis
- D. Trachoma

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Atherosclerosis** — Chlamydia pneumoniae causes atypical pneumonia and is implicated in atherosclerosis via chronic vascular inflammation and foam cell formation.

</details>

---
### Q928 — Streptococcal pneumoniae pneumonia present at -

Streptococcal pneumoniae pneumonia present at -

- A. < 5 years
- B. 5 - 15 years
- C. 20 - 25 years
- D. 30 - 40 years

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. < 5 years** — Streptococcus pneumoniae is the most common bacterial pneumonia in children under 5 years (post-viral, encapsulated organism susceptibility).

</details>

---
### Q929 — Most common complication of diphtheria is

Most common complication of diphtheria is -

- A. Myocarditis
- B. Pneumonia
- C. Meningitis
- D. Endocarditis

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Myocarditis** — Diphtheria exotoxin inhibits EF-2 via ADP-ribosylation; myocarditis is the most common and lethal complication causing arrhythmias and CHF.

</details>

---
### Q930 — Scleritis is most commonly associated with

Scleritis is most commonly associated with:

- A. Diabetes
- B. Osteoarthritis
- C. Rheumatoid arthritis
- D. Hypertension

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Rheumatoid arthritis** — Rheumatoid arthritis is the most common systemic disease associated with scleritis; immune complex-mediated vasculitis of scleral vessels.

</details>

---
### Q931 — Prutchners retinopathy in associated with -

Prutchners retinopathy in associated with -

- A. Diabetes Mellitus
- B. Wilson's disease
- C. Head trauma
- D. Rheumatoid arthritis

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Head trauma** — Purtscher's retinopathy follows head trauma, chest compression, or acute pancreatitis; cotton-wool spots and retinal hemorrhages from microembolic occlusion.

</details>

---
### Q932 — Which one of the following is

Which one of the following is not an early complication of acute myocardial infarction ?

- A. Papillary muscle dysfunction
- B. Ventricular septal defect
- C. Paricarditis
- D. Dressler's syndrome

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Dressler's syndrome** — Dressler's syndrome (autoimmune post-MI pericarditis) appears 2-10 weeks later; papillary rupture, VSD, pericarditis are early complications.

</details>

---
### Q933 — Hepatic Encephalopathy is predisposed by all,

Hepatic Encephalopathy is predisposed by all, Except:

- A. Hyperkalemia
- B. Dehydration
- C. Constipation
- D. GI Bleeding

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Hyperkalemia** — HypoKALemia (not hyperkalemia) precipitates hepatic encephalopathy by increasing renal ammonia production; GI bleed, constipation, dehydration also trigger.

</details>

---
### Q934 — Rockall score is used for prognosis

Rockall score is used for prognosis of patients of

- A. Upper GI bleeding
- B. Lower GI bleeding
- C. Hepatic encephalopathy
- D. IBD

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Upper GI bleeding** — Rockall score predicts mortality and rebleeding risk in upper GI bleeding using age, shock, comorbidity, and endoscopic findings.

</details>

---
### Q935 — Not associated with diabetes mellitus

Not associated with diabetes mellitus

- A. Cushing syndrome
- B. Acromegaly
- C. Hypothyroidism
- D. Pheochromocytoma

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Hypothyroidism** — Cushing, acromegaly, pheochromocytoma cause secondary diabetes via counter-regulatory hormones; hypothyroidism is not associated with diabetes.

</details>

---
### Q936 — In long standing rheumatoid arthritis which

In long standing rheumatoid arthritis which will be seen -

- A. Milk alkali syndrome
- B. Nephrolithiasis
- C. Paradoxical aciduria
- D. Secondary amylodosis

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Secondary amylodosis** — Chronic inflammation in RA causes serum amyloid A deposition (AA amyloidosis), commonly affecting kidneys causing nephrotic syndrome.

</details>

---
### Q937 — Alzehiemer type II astrocyte are seen

Alzehiemer type II astrocyte are seen in -

- A. Hepatic encephalopathy
- B. Alzehiemer's
- C. Parkinsonism
- D. Biswanger disease

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Hepatic encephalopathy** — Alzheimer type II astrocytes (enlarged pale nuclei) develop in hepatic encephalopathy due to ammonia-induced glutamine accumulation in glia.

</details>

---
### Q938 — Chvostek sign is seen in

Chvostek sign is seen in:

- A. Hypercalcemia
- B. Hypoparathyroidism
- C. Insulinoma
- D. Diabetes mellitus

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Hypoparathyroidism** — Chvostek sign (facial twitch on tapping facial nerve) indicates hypocalcemia from hypoparathyroidism due to increased neuromuscular excitability.

</details>

---
### Q939 — Martel sign is seen in -

Martel sign is seen in -

- A. Gout
- B. Ankylosing spondylitis
- C. Osteoarthritis
- D. Rheumatoid arthritis

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Gout** — Martel's sign — punched-out periarticular erosions with overhanging edges on X-ray — is pathognomonic of chronic tophaceous gout.

</details>

---
### Q940 — In which of causes of oral

In which of causes of oral ulcer, Auto- antibodies are not seen?

- A. Behcet disease
- B. SLE
- C. Pemphigus
- D. Celiac disease

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Behcet disease** — Behcet disease is a neutrophilic vasculitis without specific autoantibodies; SLE (ANA), pemphigus (anti-desmoglein), celiac (anti-tTG) are autoimmune.

</details>

---
### Q941 — Incorrect about LAMB syndrome -

Incorrect about LAMB syndrome -

- A. Lentigines
- B. Atrial Myxoma
- C. Myaesthenic syndrome
- D. Blue Nevi

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Myaesthenic syndrome** — LAMB syndrome (Carney complex variant): Lentigines, Atrial myxoma, Mucocutaneous myxomas, Blue nevi — no myasthenic component.

</details>

---
### Q942 — The Vitamin which has inhibitory effect

The Vitamin which has inhibitory effect on wound healing is -

- A. Vitamin -A
- B. Vitamin - E
- C. Vitamin -C
- D. Vitamin B-complex

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Vitamin - E** — Vitamin E impairs wound healing by inhibiting collagen synthesis and antagonizing vitamin A's beneficial effects on epithelialization.

</details>

---
### Q943 — Number of barr bodies in klinfellters

Number of barr bodies in klinfellter's syndrome is -

- A. 0
- B. 1
- C. 2
- D. 3

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. 1** — Klinefelter (47,XXY) has one Barr body — number of Barr bodies equals X chromosomes minus one (lyonization inactivates extra X).

</details>

---
### Q944 — Which of these is not a

Which of these is not a part of extracellular matrix:

- A. Laminin
- B. Fibronectin
- C. Integrins
- D. Collagen

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Integrins** — Integrins are transmembrane cell-surface receptors linking ECM to cytoskeleton; laminin, fibronectin, and collagen are true ECM components.

</details>

---
### Q945 — Which of the following substances is

Which of the following substances is primarily found in tendons?

- A. Collagen
- B. Fibrin
- C. Fibrillin
- D. Protedglycans

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Collagen** — Tendons are dense regular connective tissue composed predominantly of Type I collagen fibers aligned parallel for tensile strength transmitting muscle force to bone.

</details>

---
### Q946 — DOC for acute attack of Hereditary

DOC for acute attack of Hereditary angioneurotic edema

- A. Danazol
- B. Cl, inhibitor concentrate
- C. Icatibant
- D. Methylprednisolone

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Cl, inhibitor concentrate** — C1-esterase inhibitor concentrate is first-line for acute HAE attacks; replaces deficient C1-INH, halting kallikrein-bradykinin cascade causing angioedema.

</details>

---
### Q947 — Which can be give in hemorrhagic

Which can be give in hemorrhagic stroke ?

- A. Normal saline
- B. Colloids
- C. Blood transfusion
- D. Hypertonic fluids

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Normal saline** — Normal saline (isotonic) is preferred; hypotonic fluids worsen cerebral edema, while colloids/hypertonic solutions aren't routinely used acutely in hemorrhagic stroke.

</details>

---
### Q948 — Flushing with niacin in reduced by

Flushing with niacin in reduced by -

- A. Laropiprant
- B. Premedication with aspirin
- C. Tachyphylaxis
- D. All of the above

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. All of the above** — Niacin flushing is PGD2-mediated; aspirin blocks COX-PGD2, laropiprant blocks DP1 receptor, and tachyphylaxis develops with continued dosing—all reduce flushing.

</details>

---
### Q949 — Obesity is seen in all except

Obesity is seen in all except

- A. Cushing syndrome
- B. Pickwinian syndrome
- C. Prader willi syndrome
- D. Sipple syndrome

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Sipple syndrome** — Sipple syndrome (MEN 2A: medullary thyroid carcinoma, pheochromocytoma, hyperparathyroidism) causes weight loss, not obesity, unlike Cushing/Pickwickian/Prader-Willi.

</details>

---
### Q950 — Intracranial pressure may be increased by

Intracranial pressure may be increased by all of the following drugs except -

- A. Hypervitaminosis A
- B. Corticosteroids
- C. Quinolones
- D. Aminoglycosides

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Aminoglycosides** — Aminoglycosides don't raise ICP; Vitamin A toxicity, corticosteroids withdrawal, and quinolones (especially nalidixic acid) cause pseudotumor cerebri/raised ICP.

</details>

---
### Q951 — Most common location of ectopic salivary

Most common location of ectopic salivary gland is -

- A. Cervical lymph nodes
- B. Anterior mediastinum
- C. Posterior triangle
- D. Parathyroid gland

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Cervical lymph nodes** — Ectopic salivary tissue is most commonly found within cervical lymph nodes due to embryologic entrapment during branchial arch development, explaining Warthin tumor location.

</details>

---
### Q952 — Classic triad inRenal cell carcinoma includes

Classic triad inRenal cell carcinoma includes all of the following, Except:

- A. Hematuria
- B. Hypertension
- C. Flank mass
- D. Abdominal Pain

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Hypertension** — Classic RCC triad is hematuria, flank mass, and flank pain ("too late triad"); hypertension is a paraneoplastic feature, not part of triad.

</details>

---
### Q953 — ESWL is contraindicated in which of

ESWL is contraindicated in which of the following stones -

- A. Cysteine stones
- B. Oxalate Stones
- C. Urate stones
- D. Phosphate stones

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Cysteine stones** — Cystine stones are extremely hard (low fragility index) and resistant to shock wave fragmentation; require alternative approaches like ureteroscopy or chemolysis.

</details>

---
### Q954 — Surgery for undescended testis is recommended

Surgery for undescended testis is recommended at what age?

- A. 6 months
- B. 12 months
- C. 24 months
- D. 36 months

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. 6 months** — Orchidopexy is recommended at 6-12 months to preserve fertility and reduce malignancy risk; germ cell loss begins after 12 months of cryptorchidism.

</details>

---
### Q955 — Pseudoclaudication is caused by

Pseudoclaudication is caused by

- A. Femoral Artery stenosis
- B. Popliteal Artery stenosis
- C. Lumbar canal stenosis
- D. Radial Artery stenosis

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Lumbar canal stenosis** — Lumbar canal stenosis compresses nerve roots/cauda equina, causing neurogenic claudication relieved by spinal flexion (shopping cart sign), unlike vascular claudication.

</details>

---
### Q956 — Which of the following is the

Which of the following is the most conservative neck dissection -

- A. Supraomohyoid neck dissection
- B. Radical neck dissection
- C. Modified radical neck dissection
- D. All are conservative

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Supraomohyoid neck dissection** — Supraomohyoid (selective levels I-III) is the most conservative; preserves SCM, IJV, accessory nerve, and removes fewest nodal groups.

</details>

---
### Q957 — Supraomohyoid dissection is a type of

Supraomohyoid dissection is a type of -

- A. Selective neck dissection
- B. Modified radical neck dissection
- C. Radical neck dissection
- D. Posterolateral dissection

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Selective neck dissection** — Supraomohyoid dissection removes levels I-III nodes selectively, preserving non-lymphatic structures; classified as selective neck dissection per AAO-HNS classification.

</details>

---
### Q958 — Alagille syndrome- all of the following

Alagille syndrome- all of the following are true except -

- A. Mutation in JAG 1 And Notch2 gene are seen
- B. Can Cause Autoimmune hepatitis
- C. Autosomal Recessive Disease
- D. Valvular anomalis of heart seen

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Autosomal Recessive Disease** — Alagille syndrome is autosomal DOMINANT (not recessive), caused by JAG1/NOTCH2 mutations; features paucity of bile ducts, butterfly vertebrae, peripheral pulmonary stenosis.

</details>

---
### Q959 — Absence of Ultra hepatic bile duct

Absence of Ultra hepatic bile duct leads to which syndrome?

- A. Von Meyenburg Complexes
- B. Polycystic Liver Disease
- C. Caroli Disease
- D. Alagille Syndrome

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Alagille Syndrome** — Alagille syndrome features intrahepatic bile duct paucity (ductopenia) from JAG1 mutation, causing cholestasis with characteristic facies and cardiac/skeletal anomalies.

</details>

---
### Q960 — Adsons test is positive in -

Adson's test is positive in -

- A. Cervical rib
- B. Cervical spondylosis
- C. Cervical fracture
- D. Cervical dislocation

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Cervical rib** — Adson's test detects thoracic outlet syndrome from cervical rib; loss of radial pulse on neck extension/rotation indicates subclavian artery compression by scalene/rib.

</details>

---
### Q961 — Anterior Mediastinal nodes are included in

Anterior Mediastinal nodes are included in which level of lymphnodes -

- A. I
- B. III
- C. V
- D. VII

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. VII** — Level VII includes superior mediastinal/paratracheal nodes below suprasternal notch; anterior mediastinal nodes are classified here in AJCC head-neck nodal staging.

</details>

---
### Q962 — Which Is False about stress urinary

Which Is False about stress urinary incontinence -

- A. More common in men
- B. It occurs during increased abdominal pressure
- C. It is due to weakening of pelvic floor muscles
- D. Prostate surgery may be a cause

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. More common in men** — Stress incontinence is far more common in WOMEN due to pelvic floor weakening from childbirth/menopause; in men it occurs mainly post-prostatectomy.

</details>

---
### Q963 — Which of the following urethral anomaly

Which of the following urethral anomaly is the most common -

- A. Hypospadias
- B. Pin hole meatus
- C. Epispadias
- D. Stricture urethra

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Hypospadias** — Hypospadias (ventral urethral opening) is the commonest urethral anomaly, occurring in ~1:250 male births due to incomplete fusion of urethral folds.

</details>

---
### Q964 — Which of the following types of

Which of the following types of shock wiil usually have warm peripheral extremities ?

- A. Hypovolemic Shock
- B. Neurogenic Shock
- C. Cardiogenic Shock
- D. Anaphylactic Shock

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Neurogenic Shock** — Neurogenic shock causes loss of sympathetic tone, peripheral vasodilation with warm dry extremities and bradycardia—distinguishing from cold, clammy hypovolemic/cardiogenic shock.

</details>

---
### Q965 — Antibiotic prophylaxis is best given -

Antibiotic prophylaxis is best given -

- A. 1 day before surgery
- B. 2 hours before surgery
- C. Before the time of incision
- D. Only postoperatively

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Before the time of incision** — Surgical antibiotic prophylaxis is optimal within 60 minutes before skin incision to ensure peak tissue levels at time of bacterial contamination.

</details>

---
### Q966 — Which of the following is a

Which of the following is a feature of crush syndrome -

- A. Hypokalemia
- B. Hypercalcemia
- C. Myoglobinuria
- D. Hypophosphatemia

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Myoglobinuria** — Crush syndrome releases myoglobin from damaged muscle into circulation, causing pigmenturia and acute tubular necrosis; also features hyperkalemia and hypocalcemia.

</details>

---
### Q967 — Crush Syndrome is associated with all

Crush Syndrome is associated with all of the following features except -

- A. Hypercalcemia
- B. Hyperkalemia
- C. Increased serum creatinine
- D. Myoglobinuria

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Hypercalcemia** — Crush syndrome causes HYPOcalcemia (calcium deposits in damaged muscle), not hypercalcemia; also hyperkalemia, myoglobinuria, raised creatinine from rhabdomyolysis/AKI.

</details>

---
### Q968 — Which of the following stones are

Which of the following stones are common with infection ?

- A. Struvite
- B. Xanthine Stones
- C. Cysteine stones
- D. Calcium Oxalate stones

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Struvite** — Struvite (magnesium ammonium phosphate) stones form in alkaline urine from urease-producing organisms (Proteus, Klebsiella); cause staghorn calculi requiring complete removal.

</details>

---
### Q969 — High Flying prostate is a sign

High Flying prostate is a sign of -

- A. Extraperitoneal Bladder rupture
- B. Intraperitoneal Bladder Rupture
- C. Membranous Urethral Injury
- D. Bulbar Urethral Injury

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Membranous Urethral Injury** — High-riding prostate on DRE indicates membranous urethral rupture (posterior urethral injury) from pelvic fracture, with prostate displaced upward by pelvic hematoma.

</details>

---
### Q970 — BPH involves -

BPH involves -

- A. Central zone
- B. Peripheral zone
- C. Transitional zone
- D. Prostate capsule

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Transitional zone** — BPH arises in the transitional (periurethral) zone causing urethral compression and obstruction; prostate cancer typically arises in peripheral zone.

</details>

---
### Q971 — Prostate cancer that is limited to

Prostate cancer that is limited to the capsule and not the urethra would be satged as -

- A. Tx
- B. T1
- C. T2
- D. T3

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. T2** — T2 indicates tumor confined within prostate capsule without extracapsular extension; T3 denotes capsular breach/seminal vesicle invasion in TNM staging.

</details>

---
### Q972 — If 90 of jejunum and ilium

If 90% of jejunum and ilium is removed then all of the following features will be seen excePt -

- A. Steatorrhoea
- B. Diarrhoea
- C. Weight gain
- D. Megaloblastic anemia

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Weight gain** — Massive small bowel resection causes short bowel syndrome with malabsorption: steatorrhea, diarrhea, B12 deficiency megaloblastic anemia, and weight LOSS (not gain).

</details>

---
### Q973 — All of the following may lead

All of the following may lead to a gall bladder carcinoma except -

- A. Gall Bladder Polyps
- B. Typhoid carriers
- C. Exposure to carcinogens like nitrosamine
- D. Echinococcus Granulosus Infection

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Echinococcus Granulosus Infection** — Echinococcus granulosus (hydatid disease) affects liver, not gallbladder; GB carcinoma risk factors include gallstones, polyps >1cm, typhoid carriers, porcelain GB, nitrosamines.

</details>

---
### Q974 — All of the following are false

All of the following are false for Gall Bladder carcinoma except:

- A. Carries a good prognosis
- B. Gallstones may be a predisposing factor
- C. Commonly squamous cell carcinoma
- D. Jaundice is rare

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Gallstones may be a predisposing factor** — Gallstones are the major risk factor (chronic inflammation); GB cancer has POOR prognosis, is mostly adenocarcinoma (not SCC), and jaundice is common late feature.

</details>

---
### Q975 — Which of the following is not

Which of the following is not true about Boerhaave syndrome?

- A. Perforation of the esophagus due to barotrauma
- B. Most common site is left posteromedial aspect 3 - 5 cms above
- C. Pain is the early manifestation
- D. Most cases follow a bout of heavy eating or drinking

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Most common site is left posteromedial aspect 3 - 5 cms above** — Boerhaave's most common site is the LEFT POSTEROLATERAL wall of the lower esophagus, about 3-5 cm above the gastroesophageal junction, not posteromedial.

</details>

---
### Q976 — Which of the following is true

Which of the following is true about Mallory Weiss tear -

- A. It is a mucosal tear not extending through the muscle layer
- B. It is more common in women than men
- C. It is common in young individuals
- D. It is associated with achalasia cardia

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. It is a mucosal tear not extending through the muscle layer** — Mallory-Weiss is a longitudinal mucosal/submucosal tear at the gastroesophageal junction from forceful retching, sparing the muscularis layer entirely.

</details>

---
### Q977 — What is the most common position

What is the most common position of appendix?

- A. Pelvic
- B. Paracolic
- C. Retrocaecal
- D. Retroperitoneal

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Retrocaecal** — Retrocaecal position (~74%) is the commonest appendix location; the tip lies behind the caecum, explaining flank pain and psoas sign positivity.

</details>

---
### Q978 — year pld male complaining of per

year pld male complaining of per rectal bleeding was diagnosed of having rectal/anorectal cancer. The distal margin of the tumor was 5 cm

- A. Palliative Radiotherapy
- B. Abdominoperineal recection
- C. Low anterior resection
- D. Local Excision

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Low anterior resection** — Tumors with distal margin above 5 cm from anal verge allow sphincter-preserving low anterior resection with adequate distal clearance and TME.

</details>

---
### Q979 — Commonest cause of intussusception is -

Commonest cause of intussusception is -

- A. Submucous lipoma
- B. Meckel's diverticulum
- C. Hypertrophy of submucous peyer's patches
- D. Polyp

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Hypertrophy of submucous peyer's patches** — In children, hypertrophied Peyer's patches following viral infection act as lead point, causing ileocolic intussusception; adults typically have pathological lead points.

</details>

---
### Q980 — All of the following are clinical

All of the following are clinical features suggestive of trachea-esophagal fistula except -

- A. Choking and Coughing
- B. Regurgitation
- C. Cyanosis
- D. Fever

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Fever** — TEF presents with choking, coughing, regurgitation, cyanosis on feeding from aspiration; fever is a late complication of pneumonia, not a presenting feature.

</details>

---
### Q981 — Most important presenting feature of periampullary

Most important presenting feature of periampullary carcinoma is -

- A. Jaundice
- B. Pain
- C. Weight Loss
- D. Palpable Mass

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Jaundice** — Periampullary tumors obstruct the distal common bile duct early, producing painless progressive obstructive jaundice (Courvoisier's law) as the dominant presenting feature.

</details>

---
### Q982 — Best marker to assess prognosis after

Best marker to assess prognosis after Surgery for colon carcinoma:

- A. CA 19-9
- B. CA-125
- C. Alpha fetoprotein
- D. CEA

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. CEA** — CEA is the gold-standard marker for colon cancer surveillance; rising postoperative levels indicate recurrence or metastasis with high sensitivity and specificity.

</details>

---
### Q983 — All of the following are features

All of the following are features of Zollinger Ellison syndrome except

- A. Intractable peptic ulcers
- B. Severe diarrhoea
- C. Beta cell tumors of the pancreas
- D. Very high acid output.

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Beta cell tumors of the pancreas** — ZES is caused by gastrinoma (non-beta islet cell tumor) of pancreas/duodenum secreting gastrin; beta cells secrete insulin causing insulinoma instead.

</details>

---
### Q984 — All of the following is true

All of the following is true about heart transplantation except -

- A. Immmunosupression is started preoperatively
- B. It is only orthotopic and not heterotopic
- C. A Beating heart cadaver/donor is needed
- D. High Pulmonary arterial resistance in a contra indication

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. It is only orthotopic and not heterotopic** — Heart transplantation can be orthotopic (native heart removed) or heterotopic (donor heart placed alongside native heart as piggyback) for high pulmonary resistance recipients.

</details>

---
### Q985 — Lateral aberrant thyroid refers to -

Lateral aberrant thyroid refers to -

- A. Congenital thyroid abnormality
- B. Metastatic focus in lymph nodes
- C. Struma ovarii
- D. Lingual thyroid

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Metastatic focus in lymph nodes** — Lateral aberrant thyroid is actually a misnomer for metastatic papillary thyroid carcinoma deposits in lateral cervical lymph nodes; true ectopic lateral thyroid is rare.

</details>

---
### Q986 — True regarding cystic hygroma is -

True regarding cystic hygroma is -

- A. Non transilluminant
- B. Lined by columnar epithelium epithelium
- C. Develops from jugular lymphatic sequestration
- D. All

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Develops from jugular lymphatic sequestration** — Cystic hygroma is a lymphatic malformation from sequestration of jugular lymph sac; brilliantly transilluminant, lined by endothelium, presents in posterior triangle of neck.

</details>

---
### Q987 — Most common site for Cystic Hygroma

Most common site for Cystic Hygroma is -

- A. Lower third of neck
- B. Overlying the parotid gland
- C. Along the Zygomatic Prominence
- D. Post auricular

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Lower third of neck** — Cystic hygroma most commonly occurs in the lower third of the neck (posterior triangle), arising from sequestration of the jugular lymphatic sac.

</details>

### Q988 — Chvostek sign could be seen after

Chvostek sign could be seen after -

- A. Gastrojejunostomy
- B. Total thyroidectomy
- C. Subtotal Thyroidectomy
- D. Hellers Cardiomyotomy

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Total thyroidectomy** — Total thyroidectomy risks parathyroid devascularization causing hypocalcemia; Chvostek sign (facial twitch on tapping facial nerve) reflects neuromuscular irritability from low calcium.

</details>

---
### Q989 — Transplanted kidney is relocated to which

Transplanted kidney is relocated to which region in the recepient's body ?

- A. Retroperitoneal rgion
- B. Lumbar region
- C. Epigastrium
- D. Beside the dysfunctional Kidney

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Retroperitoneal rgion** — Donor kidney is placed extraperitoneally in iliac fossa (retroperitoneal); renal vessels anastomosed to iliac vessels and ureter implanted into bladder.

</details>

---
### Q990 — Claudication due to popliteofemoral incompetence i

Claudication due to popliteofemoral incompetence is primary seen in

- A. Thigh
- B. Calf
- C. Buttocks
- D. Feet

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Calf** — Popliteofemoral (superficial femoral artery) occlusion causes calf claudication as gastrocnemius is the first muscle group distal to the block to become ischemic.

</details>

---
### Q991 — Food can commonly get obstructed in

Food can commonly get obstructed in the esophagus at all of the following locations except

- A. Crossing of left bronchus
- B. Crossing of arch of aorta
- C. Diagphragmatic aperture
- D. Crossing of the hemiazygous vein

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Crossing of the hemiazygous vein** — Esophageal anatomical constrictions are cricopharyngeus, aortic arch crossing, left main bronchus crossing, and diaphragmatic hiatus; hemiazygos vein crossing is not a constriction.

</details>

---
### Q992 — A full thickness wound that is

A full thickness wound that is not sutured heals by

- A. Primary Healing
- B. Secondary Healing
- C. Delayed primary Healing
- D. Reepithelization

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Secondary Healing** — Unapposed wound edges with tissue loss heal by secondary intention through granulation tissue formation, wound contraction, and re-epithelialization, leaving prominent scar.

</details>

---
### Q993 — An Incisional wound heals by

An Incisional wound heals by

- A. Primary Healing
- B. Secondary Healing
- C. Delayed primary Healing
- D. Reepithelization

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Primary Healing** — Clean surgical incisions with apposed edges heal by primary intention with minimal granulation tissue, rapid epithelialization, and a fine linear scar.

</details>

---
### Q994 — A cystic mass at the base

A cystic mass at the base of umbilical cord in a neonate could be

- A. Allantoic Cyst
- B. Meckel's Diverticulum
- C. Ventral mesogastrium
- D. Cystic Hygroma

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Allantoic Cyst** — Allantoic (urachal) cyst arises from persistence of the allantois between bladder and umbilicus, presenting as cystic swelling at umbilical cord base in neonates.

</details>

---
### Q995 — Which of the following gas is

Which of the following gas is most commonly used in laproscopy?

- A. CO2
- B. N2O
- C. O2
- D. Helium

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. CO2** — CO2 is used because it is non-combustible, highly soluble in blood (rapidly absorbed reducing embolism risk), inexpensive, and easily excreted via lungs.

</details>

---
### Q996 — Strawberry gallbladder is seen in

Strawberry gallbladder is seen in ?

- A. Gangrene of gallbladder
- B. Porcelain gall bladder
- C. Adenomatosis
- D. Cholesterosis

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Cholesterosis** — Cholesterosis (strawberry gallbladder) shows yellow cholesterol-laden macrophages in lamina propria against red hyperemic mucosa, mimicking a strawberry's seeded surface appearance.

</details>

---
### Q997 — All of the following are causes

All of the following are causes of Urothelial Carcinomas Except -

- A. Smoking
- B. Industrial solvents
- C. Exposure to thorotrast
- D. Alcohol consumption

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Alcohol consumption** — Smoking (#1 risk), aromatic amines/industrial solvents, and thorotrast cause urothelial carcinoma via direct mucosal carcinogen exposure; alcohol is not an established risk factor.

</details>

### Q998 — Commonest site of peptic ulcer is

Commonest site of peptic ulcer is :

- A. 1st part of Duodenum
- B. IInd part of duodenum
- C. Distal 1/3 of stomach
- D. Pylorus of the stomach

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. 1st part of Duodenum** — First part of duodenum (bulb), anterior wall, is the commonest peptic ulcer site due to direct acid exposure before pancreatic bicarbonate neutralization.

</details>

### Q999 — In case of polytrauma with multiple

In case of polytrauma with multiple injuries to the chest, neck and abdomen, highest priority is given to

- A. Stabilization of cervical spine
- B. Staring of koids
- C. Vasopressors
- D. Assesing disability

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Stabilization of cervical spine** — ATLS protocol prioritizes Airway with cervical spine stabilization first; unprotected C-spine manipulation in trauma risks catastrophic cord injury and quadriplegia.

</details>

### Q1000 — Thyroid nodule in a 65 year

Thyroid nodule in a 65 year old male who is clinically euthyroid is most likely to be

- A. Follicular adenoma
- B. Follicular Carcinoma
- C. Thyroid Cyst
- D. Multinodular goiter

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Follicular adenoma** — Solitary euthyroid thyroid nodules are most commonly benign follicular adenomas; FNAC cannot distinguish from follicular carcinoma without capsular/vascular invasion on histology.

</details>

### Q1001 — In Subtotal Thyroidectomy, What is true

In Subtotal Thyroidectomy, What is true

- A. Removal of one lobe and isthmus
- B. Removal of both lobes leaving behind 6-8 grams of tissue
- C. Removal of entire thyroid with cervical lymphnodes
- D. Removal of 1 lobe with isthmus and the second lobe partially

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Removal of both lobes leaving behind 6-8 grams of tissue** — Subtotal thyroidectomy preserves approximately 4-8 grams of thyroid tissue bilaterally to maintain euthyroid status and protect recurrent laryngeal nerves and parathyroids.

</details>

### Q1002 — Which of the following statements about

Which of the following statements about Branchial cysts is true:

- A. 50-70% are seen in lungs
- B. Most common site is mediastinum
- C. They are premalignant lesions
- D. Infection is uncommon in Pulmonary bronchogenic cysts

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Most common site is mediastinum** — Note: question stem references branchial but options describe bronchogenic cysts; bronchogenic cysts most commonly arise in the mediastinum, particularly subcarinal region.

</details>

### Q1003 — Most common cause of goiter in

Most common cause of goiter in India is

- A. Diffuse Endemic Goitre
- B. Papillary Carcinoma
- C. Toxic Multinodular Goitre
- D. Hashimoto's Thyroiditis

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Diffuse Endemic Goitre** — Diffuse endemic (colloid) goiter from dietary iodine deficiency remains the commonest cause in India, especially in the sub-Himalayan goiter belt despite salt iodization.

</details>

### Q1004 — Which of the following is an

Which of the following is an indication for thoracotomy in case of hemothorax ?

- A. Persistant drainage of 250 ml/hr
- B. Total output of 1000m1 of blood
- C. Falling blood pressure
- D. Shift of mediastimum to the opposite side

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Persistant drainage of 250 ml/hr** — Thoracotomy indicated for initial drainage greater than 1500ml or persistent bleeding above 200-250 ml/hour for 3-4 hours, indicating ongoing major vascular injury.

</details>

### Q1005 — year old male with chest trauma

A patient (age not captured in source) who is a year old male with chest trauma presents with breathlessness, decreased respiratory sounds on the right side, hyperresonance on

- A. Tension Pneumothorax
- B. Cardiac Tamonade
- C. Flail Chest
- D. Myocardial Infarction

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Tension Pneumothorax** — **A. Tension Pneumothorax** — Air trapping under pressure causes ipsilateral hyperresonance, absent breath sounds, and tracheal deviation away with haemodynamic compromise from mediastinal shift.

</details>

### Q1006 — Ainhum is seen in

Ainhum is seen in ?

- A. Base of great toe
- B. Base of fingers tips
- C. Base of toe
- D. Ankle

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Base of toe** — **C. Base of toe** — Ainhum (dactylolysis spontanea) is a spontaneous fibrous constriction at the base of the 5th toe, predominantly in Black African males, leading to autoamputation.

</details>

### Q1007 — Most common precipitant of Raynauds phenomenon

Most common precipitant of Raynaud's phenomenon is

- A. Exposure to cold
- B. Exposure to heat
- C. Psychosocial triggers
- D. Exertion

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Exposure to cold** — **A. Exposure to cold** — Cold triggers exaggerated vasospasm of digital arteries and arterioles, causing the classic triphasic colour change (white-blue-red) in Raynaud's phenomenon.

</details>

---
### Q1008 — Which is the main contraindication for

Which is the main contraindication for a liver biopsy?

- A. Thrombocytopenia
- B. Hemangioma
- C. Ascites
- D. All

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. All** — **D. All** — Thrombocytopenia (bleeding risk), haemangioma (vascular rupture), and ascites (tracking of bile/blood) are all contraindications to percutaneous liver biopsy.

</details>

### Q1009 — Spontaneous esophageal rupture is most common

Spontaneous esophageal rupture is most common in

- A. Below the diagphragmatic aperture
- B. Pharyngoesophagal junction
- C. Above the diagphragmatic aperture
- D. At the crossing of the arch of aorta

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Above the diagphragmatic aperture** — **C. Above the diaphragmatic aperture** — Boerhaave syndrome rupture occurs at the left posterolateral wall of the distal oesophagus just above the diaphragm, due to sudden rise in intraluminal pressure.

</details>

### Q1010 — True about Dentigerous cyst

True about Dentigerous cyst:

- A. Arises in relation to unerupted teeth
- B. It most commonly encroaches maxillary antrum
- C. Mandibular third molar is common site
- D. Common in mandible

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Mandibular third molar is common site** — **C. Mandibular third molar is common site** — Dentigerous (follicular) cysts form around the crown of unerupted teeth; the mandibular third molar is the most commonly impacted and thus most frequently involved tooth.

</details>

---
### Q1011 — Which of the following is true

Which of the following is true about menetrier's disease

- A. It is premalignant condition
- B. There is increased gastric acid secretion
- C. Atrophied mucosal folds are seen
- D. Affects the stomach and small intestines

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. It is premalignant condition** — **A. It is premalignant condition** — Ménétrier's disease is characterised by giant rugal folds due to hyperplastic foveolar cells, protein-losing gastropathy, and a 2–15% risk of gastric adenocarcinoma.

</details>

---
### Q1012 — All are resected in whipples operation

All are resected in whipples operation except ?

- A. Duodenum
- B. Head of pancreas
- C. Neck of pancreas
- D. Common bile duct

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Neck of pancreas** — **C. Neck of pancreas** — Whipple's pancreaticoduodenectomy removes the head and uncinate process of the pancreas, duodenum, distal stomach, gallbladder and common bile duct; the neck is preserved at the transaction line but not resected as a separate step.

</details>

---
### Q1013 — CA Breast may locally spread to

CA Breast may locally spread to all of the following muscles except

- A. Pectoralis Major
- B. Pectoralis Minor
- C. Latissimus Dorsi
- D. Serratus Anterior

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Latissimus Dorsi** — **C. Latissimus Dorsi** — Breast cancer spreads locally to pectoralis major (deep), pectoralis minor, and serratus anterior; latissimus dorsi is posterior and not in direct anatomical continuity with breast tissue.

</details>

---
### Q1014 — Which is the most common type

Which is the most common type of male breast cancer?

- A. Infiltrating Ductal Carcinoma
- B. Lobular Carcinoma
- C. Mucinous Carcinoma
- D. Colloid Carcinoma

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Infiltrating Ductal Carcinoma** — **A. Infiltrating Ductal Carcinoma** — IDC accounts for ~85% of all male breast cancers because male breast tissue is predominantly ductal with virtually no lobular elements, making lobular carcinoma rare.

</details>

---
### Q1015 — In Pateys mastectomy the step not

In Patey's mastectomy the step not done is

- A. Nipple and areola removed
- B. Surrounding normal tissue of tumor is removed.
- C. Pectoralis major removed
- D. Pectoralis minor removed

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Pectoralis major removed** — **C. Pectoralis major removed** — Patey's (modified radical) mastectomy removes the breast, nipple-areola complex, axillary nodes, and pectoralis minor but preserves pectoralis major, unlike the Halsted radical mastectomy.

</details>

### Q1016 — year old woman comes with a

A patient (age not captured in source) who is a year old woman comes with a non progressive mass in a left breast since 6 months. There are no associated symptoms. Examination shows a

- A. Fibroadenoma
- B. Cystasarcoma Phylloides
- C. Scirrhous Carcinoma
- D. Fibroadenosis

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Fibroadenoma** — **A. Fibroadenoma** — A non-progressive, well-defined, mobile, non-tender breast lump in a young woman is the classic presentation of fibroadenoma, the most common benign breast tumour.

</details>

---
### Q1017 — Most common location of breast cancer

Most common location of breast cancer is

- A. Lower inner quadrant
- B. Nipple
- C. Upper inner quadrant
- D. Upper outer quadrant

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Upper outer quadrant** — **D. Upper outer quadrant** — The upper outer quadrant contains the largest volume of breast tissue and the axillary tail of Spence, making it the site of ~50% of breast carcinomas.

</details>

### Q1018 — years old male with a knoen

A patient (age not captured in source) who is a years old male with a knoen history of gall stones presents with chief complints of severe abdominal pain and elevated levels of serum lipase

- A. Age
- B. Serum LDH
- C. Base deficit
- D. Serum GGT

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Serum GGT** — **D. Serum GGT** — In Ranson's criteria for acute pancreatitis, serum GGT (gamma-glutamyl transferase) is NOT one of the scoring parameters; the criteria use LDH, AST, glucose, WBC, age, and base deficit, among others.

</details>

### Q1019 — All are true about carcinoma palate,

All are true about carcinoma palate, except -

- A. Slow growing
- B. Bilateral lymphatic spread
- C. Adenocarcinoma
- D. Presents with pain

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Presents with pain** — **D. Presents with pain** — Hard palate carcinoma (usually adenocarcinoma from minor salivary glands) is typically painless in early stages; pain appears late, making 'presents with pain' the false statement.

</details>

### Q1020 — year old lawyer presents with pain

A patient (age not captured in source) who is a year old lawyer presents with pain in the abdomen more so in the epigastric region that worsens with eating spicy food and is relieved by

- A. Perforation
- B. Bleeding
- C. Gastric Outlet Obstruction
- D. Splenic Vein Thrombosis

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Splenic Vein Thrombosis** — **D. Splenic Vein Thrombosis** — Posterior gastric ulcer penetrates the splenic vein, causing segmental/sinistral portal hypertension with gastric varices (without oesophageal varices) and splenomegaly.

</details>

### Q1021 — All of the following testicular tumours

All of the following testicular tumours are germ cell tumours EXCEPT:

- A. Seminoma
- B. Teratoma
- C. Choriocarcinoma
- D. Sertoli cell tumour

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Sertoli cell tumour** — **D. Sertoli cell tumour** — Sertoli cell tumors are sex cord-stromal tumors (along with Leydig cell tumors), not germ cell tumors; germ cell tumors include seminoma, teratoma, embryonal carcinoma, yolk sac, choriocarcinoma.

</details>

### Q1022 — The Grayhack shunt is established between

The Grayhack shunt is established between:

- A. Corpora cavernosa and dorsal vein
- B. Corpora cavernosa and saphenous vein
- C. Corpora cavernosa and glans
- D. Corpora cavernosa and corpora spongiosa

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Corpora cavernosa and saphenous vein** — **B. Corpora cavernosa and saphenous vein** — The Grayhack shunt diverts blood from the engorged corpora cavernosa to the saphenous vein to treat ischaemic priapism unresponsive to aspiration.

</details>

### Q1023 — Congenital hydrocele is best tt by

Congenital hydrocele is best t/t by -

- A. Eversion of sac
- B. Excision of sac
- C. Lords procedure
- D. herniotomy

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. herniotomy** — **D. Herniotomy** — Congenital hydrocele persists due to a patent processus vaginalis; herniotomy (ligation of the patent processus vaginalis) corrects the underlying cause, unlike eversion or excision which address the sac only.

</details>

---
### Q1024 — The posterior urethra is best visualized

The posterior urethra is best visualized by ?

- A. Static cystogram
- B. Retrograde urethrogram
- C. Voiding cystogram
- D. CT cystogram

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Voiding cystogram** — **C. Voiding cystogram** — Micturating cystourethrogram (MCUG/voiding cystogram) demonstrates the posterior urethra during voiding when the bladder neck and prostatic urethra are open, unlike retrograde urethrogram which outlines the anterior urethra.

</details>

### Q1025 — Early stage of non small cell

Early stage of non small cell lung cancer can be treated by

- A. Surgical resection
- B. Surgical resection with adjuvant chemotherapy
- C. Radiotherapy
- D. Immunotherapy

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Radiotherapy** — **C. Radiotherapy** — Wait — this answer is unusual; surgical resection (lobectomy) is the gold standard for early-stage NSCLC (Stage I–II), but stereotactic body radiotherapy (SBRT) is used when surgery is contraindicated, making radiotherapy a valid curative option for inoperable early-stage disease.

</details>

### Q1026 — All of the following about Gastrointestinal

All of the following about Gastrointestinal carcinoid tumors are true, Except:

- B. 5 year survival for carcinoid tumors is >60%
- C. Rectum is spared
- D. Appendicial carcinoids are more common in females than

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Rectum is spared** — **C. Rectum is spared** — The rectum is actually the third most common site for GI carcinoid tumours; stating it is 'spared' is false as rectal carcinoids are well-recognised and often found incidentally.

</details>

### Q1027 — Commonest infantile testicular tumour is

Commonest infantile testicular tumour is ?

- A. Seminoma
- B. Teratoma
- C. Yolk sac tumor
- D. Dysgerminoma

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Yolk sac tumor** — **C. Yolk sac tumor** — Yolk sac tumour (endodermal sinus tumour) is the most common testicular neoplasm in infants and children under 3 years, producing AFP as a tumour marker.

### Q1028 — Which of the following is an

Which of the following is an absorbable suture

- A. Polyglactin
- B. Silk
- C. Polyester
- D. Ethilon

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Polyglactin** — **A. Polyglactin** — Polyglactin 910 (Vicryl) is a synthetic absorbable suture hydrolysed by the body within 56–70 days; silk, polyester, and ethilon (nylon) are all non-absorbable.

</details>

---
### Q1029 — Polydiaxonone suture is normally absorbed in

Polydiaxonone suture is normally absorbed in

- A. 2 weeks
- B. 4 weeks
- C. 6 weeks
- D. 6 months

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. 6 months** — **D. 6 months** — Polydioxanone (PDS) is a slow-absorbing monofilament suture that retains 70% tensile strength at 6 weeks and is completely absorbed by hydrolysis in approximately 6 months.

</details>

---
### Q1030 — Most tissue reaction is seen with

Most tissue reaction is seen with

- A. Plain Catgut
- B. Polydiaxonone
- C. Silk
- D. Chromic catgut

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Silk** — **C. Silk** — Silk is a braided natural protein fibre that causes the greatest inflammatory tissue reaction among common sutures due to its multifilament structure and biological origin harbouring bacteria.

</details>

### Q1031 — Trauma and Injury Severity Score (TRISS)

Trauma and Injury Severity Score (TRISS) includes:

- A. GCS + BP + RR
- B. RTS + ISS + Age
- C. RTS + ISS + GCS
- D. RTS + GCS + Age

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. RTS + ISS + Age** — **B. RTS + ISS + Age** — TRISS (Trauma and Injury Severity Score) combines the Revised Trauma Score (physiological), Injury Severity Score (anatomical), and patient age to predict probability of survival.

</details>

### Q1032 — Which of the following is most

Which of the following is most malignant tumor?

- A. Glioblastoma Multiforme
- B. Meningioma
- C. Osteochondroma
- D. Giant cell tumor

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Glioblastoma Multiforme** — **A. Glioblastoma Multiforme** — GBM (WHO Grade IV astrocytoma) is the most malignant primary brain tumour with median survival of 14–15 months due to rapid proliferation, neoangiogenesis, and central necrosis.

</details>

### Q1033 — Epulis arises from -

Epulis arises from -

- A. Enamel
- B. Root of teeth
- C. Gingiva
- D. Pulp

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Gingiva** — **C. Gingiva** — Epulis is a localised benign swelling arising from the gingiva (gum tissue); types include fibrous, giant cell (peripheral giant cell granuloma), and vascular (pyogenic granuloma).

</details>

### Q1034 — IQ in mild mental retardation is

IQ in mild mental retardation is

- A. 50-70
- B. 35-49
- C. 20-34
- D. < 20

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. 50-70** — **A. 50-70** — DSM/ICD classification: mild intellectual disability = IQ 50–70, moderate = 35–49, severe = 20–34, profound = below 20; those with mild ID can achieve 6th-grade academic level.

</details>

### Q1035 — What is the IQ of a

What is the IQ of a borderline deficiency?

- A. 70 - 80
- B. 50 - 69
- C. 20 - 49
- D. 0 - 20

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. 70 - 80** — **A. 70 - 80** — Borderline intellectual functioning (not a formal intellectual disability) is defined as IQ 70–80 by ICD-10/DSM; these individuals have difficulty with academic tasks but may function independently.

</details>

---
### Q1036 — According to Wechsler intelligence scale scoring,

According to Wechsler intelligence scale scoring, average IQ of a normal child is:

- A. 50
- B. 75
- C. 90
- D. 111

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. 90** — **C. 90** — The Wechsler Intelligence Scale is standardised with a mean of 100 and SD of 15; the average range is 90–109, placing a score of 90 at the lower end of 'average' intelligence.

</details>

---
### Q1037 — Milestones at 1 year of age

Milestones at 1 year of age are all except

- A. Playing a simple ball game
- B. Using 2 words that are meaningful
- C. Spontaneous scribbling
- D. Walking upstairs 1 step at a time

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Walking upstairs 1 step at a time** — **D. Walking upstairs 1 step at a time** — Walking upstairs holding the rail is a 18-month milestone; at 12 months a child typically says 1–3 words, plays simple ball games, and may scribble spontaneously.

</details>

---
### Q1038 — Stranger anxiety develops at

Stranger anxiety develops at

- A. 3 months
- B. 4 months
- C. 7 months
- D. 11 months

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. 7 months** — **C. 7 months** — Stranger anxiety (fear of unfamiliar faces) typically emerges at 6–9 months when the infant develops object permanence and can distinguish familiar caregivers from strangers.

</details>

---
### Q1039 — What is the average weight gain

What is the average weight gain of the neonate per day

- A. 5-10 g
- B. 25-30 g
- C. 50-60g
- D. 100-150g

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. 25-30 g** — **B. 25-30 g** — After the initial physiological weight loss of 5–10% in the first week, neonates gain approximately 25–30 g/day, regaining birth weight by 10–14 days of life.

</details>

---
### Q1040 — Newborn loses how much weight in

Newborn loses how much weight in first week?

- A. 5 -10%
- B. 1-2%
- C. 10-20%
- D. None

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. 5 -10%** — **A. 5-10%** — Neonates physiologically lose 5–10% of birth weight in the first 3–5 days due to passage of meconium, loss of extracellular fluid, and limited initial caloric intake.

</details>

---
### Q1041 — What is the cardiothoracic ratio in

What is the cardiothoracic ratio in children is -

- A. 30-35%
- B. 40-45%
- C. 50-55%
- D. 60-65%

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. 50-55%** — **C. 50-55%** — The normal cardiothoracic ratio in children is up to 55% on a PA chest X-ray; neonates may have a ratio up to 55–60% due to a relatively larger thymus and heart.

</details>

---
### Q1042 — Most common cause of pneumonia in

Most common cause of pneumonia in early onset sepsis a neonate is

- A. Pnemococcus
- B. S. Pyogens
- C. E Coli
- D. S. Aureus

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. E Coli** — **C. E Coli** — Group B Streptococcus and E. coli are the two most common causes of early-onset neonatal sepsis and pneumonia; E. coli is the leading gram-negative organism acquired from the maternal genital tract.

</details>

---
### Q1043 — When does crying stop in cyanotic

When does crying stop in cyanotic spells ?

- A. Forced Expiration
- B. Forced inspiration
- C. Mid inspiration
- D. Crying is continuous

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Forced Expiration** — **A. Forced Expiration** — In hypercyanotic (Tet) spells, crying stops during forced expiration as the child becomes exhausted/unconscious due to cerebral hypoxia from severe right-to-left shunting and infundibular spasm.

</details>

---
### Q1044 — 3 month old child with indrawing

3 month old child with indrawing chest with respitratory rate 52/min classified as

- A. SIRS
- B. Respiratory distress
- C. Tachypnoea
- D. ARDS

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Respiratory distress** — **B. Respiratory distress** — In a 3-month-old, chest indrawing (subcostal/intercostal retractions) with a respiratory rate of 52/min meets WHO IMCI criteria for severe respiratory distress requiring urgent hospital management.

</details>

---
### Q1045 — Chronic lung disease in a infancy

Chronic lung disease in a infancy is defined as

- A. Need for supplemental oxygen at 36 weeks after conception
- B. Tachypnoea > 50 breaths/ min within 1 week of birth
- C. Presence of bilateral infiltrates on chest Xray for 2 weeks
- D. Reticulogranular pattern on chest Xray for 6 weeks

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Need for supplemental oxygen at 36 weeks after conception** — **A. Need for supplemental oxygen at 36 weeks after conception** — Bronchopulmonary dysplasia (chronic lung disease of infancy) is defined as oxygen dependency at 36 weeks postmenstrual age (Jobe and Bancalari 2001 definition).

</details>

---
### Q1046 — What is the shape of caecum

What is the shape of caecum in the newborn ?

- A. Ovoid
- B. Trapezoid
- C. Globular
- D. Conical

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Conical** — **D. Conical** — The neonatal caecum is conical/funnel-shaped because the taeniae coli are absent at birth; the characteristic sacculated adult caecum develops postnatally as the taeniae coli differentiate.

</details>

---
### Q1047 — Most common complication of Meckels Diverticulum

Most common complication of Meckel's Diverticulum lit children

- A. Abdominal pain
- B. Peptic ulcers
- C. Intestinal obstruction
- D. Painless Rectal bleeding

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Painless Rectal bleeding** — **D. Painless Rectal bleeding** — Meckel's diverticulum contains ectopic gastric mucosa that secretes acid, causing ulceration in adjacent ileal mucosa leading to painless lower GI bleeding, the most common complication in children.

</details>

---
### Q1048 — Which of the following is a

Which of the following is a X linked metabolic disorder?

- A. Fabry's disease
- B. Sandoff s disease
- C. Pompe disease
- D. Gaucher disease

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Fabry's disease** — **A. Fabry's disease** — Fabry disease is an X-linked recessive lysosomal storage disorder due to alpha-galactosidase A deficiency causing glycosphingolipid accumulation; Sandhoff, Pompe, and Gaucher are autosomal recessive.

</details>

---
### Q1049 — Testes are not palpable in

Testes are not palpable in

- A. SRY deletion
- B. DAX 1 deletion
- C. WNT- 4 gene mutation
- D. RSPO-1 gene mutation

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. SRY deletion** — **A. SRY deletion** — Deletion of the SRY (sex-determining region Y) gene causes 46,XY complete gonadal dysgenesis (Swyer syndrome) where streak gonads (not testes) develop, so testes are absent and not palpable.

</details>

### Q1050 — 1 year old child with multiple

1 year old child with multiple episodes of diarrhea presents with sunken dry eyes, depressed fontanelles, very slow skin pinch. The amount of fluid to be

- A. 600 ml
- B. 900 ml
- C. 1200 ml
- D. 1500 ml

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. 900 ml** — **B. 900 ml** — Sunken eyes, depressed fontanelle, and very slow skin pinch (>2 sec) indicate severe dehydration (>10%); WHO ORS plan C for a 10 kg child = 100 ml/kg = ~900–1000 ml IV Ringer's lactate.

</details>

---
### Q1051 — Hutchisons Triad is seen in

Hutchison's Triad is seen in

- A. Congenital Syphilis
- B. Tertiary syphilis
- C. Secondary Syphilis
- D. Primary syphilis

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Congenital Syphilis** — **A. Congenital Syphilis** — Hutchinson's triad (interstitial keratitis, Hutchinson's teeth, and sensorineural deafness) are late manifestations of congenital syphilis due to Treponema pallidum infection transmitted transplacentally.

</details>

---
### Q1052 — Hydrocephalus is best detected antenately by

Hydrocephalus is best detected antenately by :

- A. X-ray abdomen
- B. Amniocentesis
- C. Clinical examination
- D. Ultrasonography

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Ultrasonography** — **D. Ultrasonography** — Antenatal ultrasound detects hydrocephalus by measuring lateral ventricle width (>10 mm = ventriculomegaly); it is safe, non-invasive, widely available, and the standard of care for fetal anomaly screening.

</details>

---
### Q1053 — Which of the following is not

Which of the following is not a feature of Juvenile Idiopathic Arthritis?

- A. Rheumatoid nodules
- B. Spikes of high fever
- C. Uveitis
- D. Raynaud's phenomenon

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Raynaud's phenomenon** — **D. Raynaud's phenomenon** — Raynaud's phenomenon is associated with systemic sclerosis, SLE, and mixed connective tissue disease, not JIA; JIA features include high spiking fever, salmon rash, uveitis, and arthritis.

</details>

---
### Q1054 — Eyelid papules and hoarse cry in

Eyelid papules and hoarse cry in a child is suggestive of -

- A. Congenital syphilis
- B. Croup
- C. Lipoid proteinosis
- D. Acrodermatitis enterohepatica

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Lipoid proteinosis** — **C. Lipoid proteinosis** — Lipoid proteinosis (Urbach-Wiethe disease) is an AR disorder of extracellular matrix protein 1 causing hyaline infiltration of skin and mucosae, producing eyelid papules (moniliform blepharosis) and hoarse cry from laryngeal infiltration.

</details>

---
### Q1055 — Which part of the spine is

Which part of the spine is most commonly affected in Rheumatoid arthritis:

- A. Cervical
- B. Lumbar
- C. Thoracic
- D. Sacral

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Cervical** — **A. Cervical** — Rheumatoid arthritis preferentially affects the cervical spine, particularly C1-C2 (atlantoaxial subluxation due to destruction of the transverse ligament), which can cause cervical myelopathy.

</details>

---
### Q1056 — Hammer toe deformity is seen in

Hammer toe deformity is seen in -

- A. Rheumatoid arthritis
- B. Fracture distal phalanx ofgreat toe
- C. Bunion
- D. Osteochondritis

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Rheumatoid arthritis** — **A. Rheumatoid arthritis** — Hammer toe (flexion deformity of PIP joint with hyperextension of MTP and DIP) results from synovitis and tendon imbalance; chronic RA causes forefoot deformities including hammer toes and hallux valgus.

</details>

---
### Q1057 — Among various types of psoriatic arthrirtis,

Among various types of psoriatic arthrirtis, which variety is most common ?

- A. Classic
- B. Oligoarticular
- C. Rheumatoid
- D. Spondylitis

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Oligoarticular** — **B. Oligoarticular** — Asymmetric oligoarticular (involving <5 joints) is the most common pattern of psoriatic arthritis, accounting for ~40% of cases; it typically affects large joints and DIP joints asymmetrically.

</details>

### Q1058 — On CT chest halo sign is

On CT chest 'halo sign' is noted in

- A. Pulmonary hydatid cyst
- B. Invasive pulmonary aspergilossis
- C. Round pneumonia
- D. Bronchiectasis

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Invasive pulmonary aspergilossis** — **B. Invasive pulmonary aspergillosis** — The CT halo sign represents a nodule surrounded by ground-glass opacity (haemorrhage around fungal invasion); it is the classic early finding in invasive pulmonary aspergillosis in immunocompromised patients.

</details>

### Q1067 — Nigro Regimen is used for -

Nigro Regimen is used for -

### Q1068 — year old male came with jaundice,

A patient (age not captured in source) who is a year old male came with jaundice, palpable mass in the right hypochondrium not associated with pain. The probable diagnosis is -

- A. Carcinoma Ampulla of Vater
- B. Acute cholecystitis
- C. Chronic Cholecystitis
- D. Chronic Pancreatitis

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Carcinoma Ampulla of Vater** — Courvoisier's law: painless jaundice with palpable gallbladder indicates periampullary/pancreatic head tumor obstructing CBD distal to cystic duct insertion.

</details>

---
### Q1069 — year male presented with jaundice, pale

year male presented with jaundice, pale stools, dark urine and mass in the epigastric region. Which of the following diagnosis in unlikely ?

- A. Pancreatic cancer
- B. Biliary Cancer
- C. Periampullary Cancer
- D. Chronic Cholecystitis

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Chronic Cholecystitis** — Obstructive jaundice triad with epigastric mass indicates malignancy; chronic cholecystitis causes contracted, non-palpable gallbladder without obstructive jaundice features.

</details>

### Q1070 — Which of the following stoma is

Which of the following stoma is formed in Hartman's procedure ?

- A. End Colostomy
- B. End Iliostomy
- C. Loop Iliostomy
- D. Caecostomy

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. End Colostomy** — Hartmann's procedure: sigmoid resection with end colostomy and oversewn rectal stump, done for obstructed/perforated left colon when anastomosis is unsafe.

</details>

---
### Q1071 — Most common site of colorectal carcinoma

Most common site of colorectal carcinoma is -

- A. Rectum
- B. Sigmoid Colon
- C. Ascending Colon
- D. Descending Colon

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Rectum** — Rectum is the commonest colorectal cancer site due to fecal stasis and prolonged mucosal exposure to carcinogens in stored stool.

</details>

---
### Q1072 — Riglers sign is suggestive of -

Rigler's sign is suggestive of -

- A. Pneumothorax
- B. Pneumoperitoneum
- C. Peritonitis
- D. Hemothorax

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Pneumoperitoneum** — Rigler's (double-wall) sign: gas outlines both mucosal and serosal sides of bowel wall on X-ray, diagnostic of pneumoperitoneum.

</details>

---
### Q1073 — Bilateral parotid enlargement occurs in all,

Bilateral parotid enlargement occurs in all, Except:

- A. Sjogren's syndrome
- B. SLE
- C. HIV
- D. Chronic pancreatitis

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. SLE** — Sjogren's, HIV (DILS), and chronic pancreatitis cause bilateral parotid enlargement; SLE typically causes lymphadenopathy/serositis, not parotidomegaly.

</details>

### Q1074 — year old male presenting with acure

A patient (age not captured in source) who is a year old male presenting with acure pancreatitis is now having refractory hypoxia. The X RAY of chest would show

- A. Bilateral infiltrates
- B. Pneumatocoeles
- C. Ground glass appearances
- D. Hilar lymphadenopathy

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Bilateral infiltrates** — Acute pancreatitis triggers ARDS via systemic inflammatory cytokines causing diffuse alveolar damage, producing bilateral patchy infiltrates on chest radiograph.

</details>

### Q1075 — Medulloblatoma arises exclusively from the cells

Medulloblatoma arises exclusively from the cells of

- A. Immature embryonal cells
- B. Ependymal cells
- C. Neurons
- D. Spindle shaped cells

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Immature embryonal cells** — Medulloblastoma is a PNET arising from primitive embryonal neuroectodermal cells of cerebellar external granular layer/roof of fourth ventricle.

</details>

### Q1076 — Hypoxic Ischemic encephalopathy true is

Hypoxic Ischemic encephalopathy true is ?

- A. Lower limbs affected more than upper limbs
- B. Prox. Muscles > distal muscles
- C. Seizure
- D. Trunk involved

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Seizure** — Neonatal HIE causes cortical/subcortical necrosis triggering seizures within 24 hours; seizures are the hallmark clinical sign of moderate-severe HIE.

</details>

---
### Q1077 — Which of the following is not

Which of the following is not true about encephalocoele?

- A. It is a neural tube defect
- B. Common in the frontal region
- C. Can be associated with hydrocephalus
- D. It is protrusion of neural tissue through a defect

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. It is a neural tube defect** — Encephalocoele is a post-neurulation defect of skull mesoderm, not a primary neural tube closure defect; neural tissue herniates through cranial bony gap.

</details>

---
### Q1078 — Most common site for opening of

Most common site for opening of TAPVC is -

- A. Supracardiac
- B. Cardiac
- C. Infracardiac
- D. Multiple

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Supracardiac** — Supracardiac TAPVC (most common, ~50%): all pulmonary veins drain via vertical vein into left innominate vein then SVC, creating snowman silhouette.

</details>

---
### Q1079 — Autosomal recessive Polycystic kidneys - all

Autosomal recessive Polycystic kidneys - all are true except -

- A. Seen in adults
- B. Defective gene is PKHD1
- C. Both kidneys show innumerable cysts
- D. USG shows salt and pepper appearance

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Seen in adults** — ARPKD (PKHD1, fibrocystin) presents in infancy/childhood with bilateral salt-and-pepper kidneys; adult presentation is characteristic of ADPKD (PKD1/PKD2).

</details>

### Q1080 — Investigation of choice for CONFIRMING Henoch

Investigation of choice for CONFIRMING Henoch Schonlein purpura is -

- A. Serum IgA levels
- B. CRP levels
- C. Renal Biopsy
- D. DTPA

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Renal Biopsy** — Renal biopsy showing IgA mesangial deposition with leukocytoclastic vasculitis is gold-standard confirmatory test for HSP nephritis diagnosis.

</details>

### Q1081 — Which of the following is correct

Which of the following is correct about shock in child?

- A. Tachycardia is a very sensitive indicator of depletion of
- B. Mottling of extremities is seen in early shock
- C. Confusion, stupor and coma are early signs
- D. Respiratory rate is more sensitive than heart rate as an

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Tachycardia is a very sensitive indicator of depletion of** — Children compensate shock by tachycardia first (sympathetic response); hypotension is late, making tachycardia the earliest sensitive sign of volume depletion.

</details>

### Q1082 — Inotropic support for severely dehydrated child

Inotropic support for severely dehydrated child with dopamine is done at what rate -

- A. 0.1-0-5 microgram/kg/min
- B. 1-5 microgram/kg/min
- C. 1-5 mg/kg/min
- D. 10-15 mg/kg/min

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. 1-5 microgram/kg/min** — Dopamine inotropic dose 1-5 mcg/kg/min stimulates beta-1 receptors increasing cardiac contractility; higher doses cause vasoconstriction via alpha receptors.

</details>

---
### Q1083 — In acute diarrhea following is used

In acute diarrhea following is used to decrease duration and severity -

- A. Zn
- B. Mg
- C. Fe
- D. Ca

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Zn** — WHO recommends zinc supplementation 10-20 mg/day for 14 days; zinc restores enterocyte integrity, reduces diarrhea duration, severity, and recurrence.

</details>

---
### Q1084 — Consanguinous marriages increase risk of diseases

Consanguinous marriages increase risk of diseases -

- A. Autosomal dominant disease
- B. Autosomal recessive disease
- C. X linked dominant diseases
- D. Environmental diseases

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Autosomal recessive disease** — Consanguinity increases homozygosity for rare recessive alleles shared from common ancestor, raising risk of autosomal recessive disorders in offspring.

</details>

---
### Q1085 — Floor of nasal cavity in children

Floor of nasal cavity in children is made of -

- A. Palatine bone and vomer
- B. Sphenoid and ethmoid
- C. Nasal bone and maxilla
- D. Palatine bone and maxilla

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Palatine bone and maxilla** — Nasal floor (hard palate) formed by palatine process of maxilla anteriorly and horizontal plate of palatine bone posteriorly in both children and adults.

</details>

### Q1086 — Reduced osmolarity ORS does not contain

Reduced osmolarity ORS does not contain which of the following ion -

- A. Sodium
- B. Potassium
- C. Lactate
- D. Citrate

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Lactate** — WHO low-osmolarity ORS (245 mOsm/L) contains sodium, potassium, citrate, glucose, and chloride; lactate was replaced by citrate for stability.

</details>

---
### Q1087 — Osteoclasts have all of the following

Osteoclasts have all of the following except -

- A. Bone resorption
- B. Receptor for parathormone
- C. Ruffledborder
- D. RANK ligand

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Receptor for parathormone** — Osteoclasts have ruffled border and RANK receptor but lack PTH receptors; PTH acts indirectly via osteoblast RANKL to stimulate osteoclasts.

</details>

---
### Q1088 — PTH acts directly on which cells

PTH acts directly on which cells ?

- A. Osteoclasts
- B. Osteocytes
- C. Osteoblasts
- D. Macrophages

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**C. Osteoblasts** — PTH receptors (PTHR1) reside on osteoblasts; activated osteoblasts secrete RANKL which then stimulates osteoclast differentiation and bone resorption.

</details>

### Q1089 — Tennis elbow is characterized by

Tennis elbow is characterized by ?

- A. Tenderness over the medial epicondyle
- B. Tendinits of common extensor origin
- C. Tendinitis of common flexor origin
- D. Painful flexion and extension

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. Tendinits of common extensor origin** — Lateral epicondylitis (tennis elbow): degenerative tendinosis of common extensor origin, especially ECRB, from repetitive wrist extension/supination.

</details>

### Q1090 — Flexor Digitorum Profundus tendon avulsion injury

Flexor Digitorum Profundus tendon avulsion injury leads to -

- A. Jersey Finger
- B. Mallet Finger
- C. Gamekeepers Thumb
- D. Boutonierre Deformity

### Q1091 — Extensor Carpi Radialis Longus is -

Extensor Carpi Radialis Longus is -

- B. Extensor and radial Deviator of the wrist

### Q1092 — Thomas test is used for testing

Thomas test is used for testing?

- A. Hip flexion
- B. Knee flexion
- C. Hip abduction
- D. Hip rotation

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Hip flexion** — Thomas test detects fixed flexion deformity of hip: opposite hip is fully flexed to obliterate lumbar lordosis, exposing affected side's flexion contracture.

</details>

### Q1093 — A child presented to an orthopaedic

A child presented to an orthopaedic clinic with a limp. The surgeon suspected him to have a fixed fledon deformity of the hip. Which test should

- A. Thomas test
- B. Trendelenburgs test
- C. Nelatons test
- D. Telescoping test

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Thomas test** — Thomas test detects fixed flexion deformity at hip by eliminating compensatory lumbar lordosis through flexion of contralateral hip joint.

</details>

### Q1094 — Effect of hypoparathyroidisrn on bones include

Effect of hypoparathyroidisrn on bones include -

- A. Brown tumours
- B. Subperiosteal Resorption of Bone
- C. Multiple Cysts in Bone
- D. NoneoftheAbove

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. NoneoftheAbove** — Hypoparathyroidism causes low PTH and hypocalcemia but no characteristic bone lesions; brown tumors, subperiosteal resorption occur in hyperparathyroidism.

---
### Q1095 — AII of the following are true

AII of the following are true regarding application of POP Cast except -

- A. Putting the Plaster roll in warm water hastens setting time
- B. It is anhydrous Calcium phosphate
- C. It can applied in presence of extreme swelling
- D. Gangrene is known complication of a tight plaster cast

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**B. It is anhydrous Calcium phosphate** — Plaster of Paris is calcium sulphate hemihydrate (CaSO4.1/2H2O), not calcium phosphate; it hardens by rehydration to gypsum (calcium sulphate dihydrate).

</details>

---
### Q1096 — In uncemented arthroplasty of the hip

In uncemented arthroplasty of the hip , the stem remains attached to the bone by -

- A. Bone Ingrowth/ ongrowth over the surface of the stem
- B. Mechanical bonding between the stem and bone
- C. Press fitting of the stem in the tight canal
- D. Adhesion between the stem and bone due to ashesive

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A. Bone Ingrowth/ ongrowth over the surface of the stem** — Uncemented hip prostheses have porous/hydroxyapatite-coated surfaces allowing bone ingrowth (osseointegration) into pores, providing biological fixation over weeks.

</details>

---
### Q1097 — Vertebra Plana is seen in all

Vertebra Plana is seen in all except -

- A. Histioctosis X
- B. Leukemia
- C. Excessive use of systemic steroids
- D. Scheurmanns Disease

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**D. Scheurmanns Disease** — Scheuermann's disease causes anterior wedging with kyphosis, not complete vertebral body collapse; vertebra plana indicates pancake-flat vertebra from eosinophilic granuloma, leukemia, steroids.

</details>

---
### Q1098 — What is luxatio erecta

What is luxatio erecta ?

- A. Anterior Dislocation of the shoulder joint
- B. Inferior Dislocation of the shoulder joint
- C. Anterior Dislocation of the HIP joint
- D. Posterior Dislocation of Hip joint
